Framing: residual stream as a field over depth x token; patching = localized source J inserted into the depth evolution; patch effect = integral of a sensitivity field a(ell,x) against J; downstream transport = two-point Green function; patch-site selection = constrained variational inverse problem solved by an adjoint (costate) equation. Everything empirical is the FORWARD half: a(ell,x) is a single autograd pass (the authors call it attribution patching in residual-stream form). RESULTS: a bounded local linear regime exists — nonlinearity η_nl and superposition error η_sup mostly below the 20% threshold across the displayed amplitude range (caveat: tiny amplitudes give denominator artefacts); that band is then used as the operating range for all later measurements. Autograd sensitivity predicts finite patch effects within the band, pooled across sites/directions/amplitudes and shown unpooled as per-site sweeps with good, mixed, low-signal and strongly-nonlinear cases; large relative error is understood to conflate finite-amplitude curvature with a near-zero denominator. Locally injected perturbations give structured ANISOTROPIC downstream response fields (concentrated at Δx=0, broadening with Δℓ, causal cone only), and the repetitive prompt yields a response periodic at the prompt's own period. Composition test: re-inject the measured mid-layer field into a clean pass and compare the downstream field — error lowest at intermediate amplitudes and rising both when the perturbation is too small to measure and too large for linear transport. Sensitivity norms concentrate on a few sites (site-selection rule) and the gradient direction is the locally optimal perturbation. Full Green tensor (Lnd)^2 is never formed; instead component-level Green SLICES (a d x d Jacobian between one source and one target layer-token pair) show dominant diagonal persistence plus off-diagonal mixing from the intervening block, and their Frobenius energy concentrates in a small number of entries. Prompt-pair displacement J_A→B = residual difference at a fixed site: scalar effect predicted by the same sensitivity rule; effect weak in early layers, rising sharply late, in both model sizes; displacement has sub-90° alignment with the local causal gradient AND with both country- and capital-embedding differences (argued as not merely an embedding proxy); at amplitude 1 the patch moves the target answer far up the vocabulary rank, often to near or better than its rank under the clean target prompt, replicated in GPT-2 medium (site ℓ*=10 small / ℓ*=22 medium, 20 pairs). Limits they name: patch-site inference (the inverse problem that motivates the whole paper) is NOT solved here; no global Green tensor estimated; MSRJD / Galerkin / scaling-hypothesis connections are formal analogies, not derivations for a transformer; the scaling hypothesis needs anchors (weights, activations, probes, or a few calibration patches) plus sparse patching validation that this paper does not supply. Mine, marked: one fixed clean pass per prompt with a two-token logit difference as the sole observable and no second model family or task, so self-consistency is shown but generality is not; there is NO baseline method, no comparison against measured patching over the same intervention space beyond the first-order formula itself, and no random-direction arm on the displacement experiments; the sensitivity field is not shown to be predictive of interventions off the gradient-aligned direction, which is precisely where this folder's patching entries find high-sensitivity-but-idle sites. NOT in SEED_QUEUE; taken from outside the queue as run 126's first flagged candidate, with filename + ID + title novelty check against all five literature folders (only NOTES matched).
arxiv-2611.05033 | Whose Effect Is It? How Objectives Inflate Learned Subspace Interventions | University of Southern Denmark (Tanyel, Ayvaz) | 2026-11 | 3 — full text (arXiv HTML v1, ~236k chars read end to end: abstract + symbol table, §1–§3 with every proof, §4–§8, §9–§11, Appendices A–F, references); NO figure inspected, so all numbers are prose or table cells; Lean and OwnEffect repos not built. A first-order decomposition of every interchange intervention into the training-free effect of copying the whole state at the same site (`G`, the indirect effect) plus an excess, then a study of who chooses the excess. EXACT: at a complete cut (all positions of a layer, or the readout) whole-state patching equals the total effect T pair by pair; at a partial cut it need not (Pythia-410m adjective/verb at L14–15 restore 120.3%/121.7% of T, lower bounds ≥104%). FIRST ORDER: a rank-one interchange along `d` has effect `dᵀMd`, whole-state patching `tr M`, so a part beats the whole exactly when the rest pulls the output back — no cleaner isolation required; rank k via Ky Fan; everything machine-checked in Lean 4 + Mathlib. Readout arm: rank-one DAS, four objectives, three shared initialisations, one and ten passes, 90 tasks, 14 base checkpoints 124M–32B across six families, exact test-pair effects, 95% intervals resampling a pair with its mirror, population rule ≥20 clusters and T above zero. RESULTS: cross-entropy DAS exceeds T in 1096/1124 margin cases at median 1.64–3.00 T per model and 0.76–0.97 of the maximum; the same initialisations under a per-pair squared error aimed at T land a median 3.0% from T, and CDAS at 0.91–1.06 T — changing only the loss moves the effect. Inside the network DAS beats the cell's copy effect at 16,489/18,221 live cells (median excess 0.14 T) and the best-context reference G* at 15,998, with 0.51–0.64 of its summed effect beyond G*; 4300 cells are off-route (DAS significant, G* not, DAS above G*), median excess there only 0.04 T. Freezing downstream attention removes 0.58 of the summed excess and only 0.10 survives, 0.43 of the excess running through the readout position's own row, whereas whole-state patching keeps 0.90 of its effect frozen — the learned increment is routed through attention where the network's own transfer is not. The squared error aimed at the cell's own copy effect lands on it (slope 0.91, intercept 0.00 T) while the same loss aimed at T sits 0.09 T above, so the objective aims wherever you point it; untrained rules give 0.88/0.93 of the copy but their bisector 2.11×. ONE ANGLE BEFORE TRAINING: on two-label uncapped tasks the median angle between readout direction and mean state change is 79.7° (IQR 73.1–83.2), and it predicts the out-of-sample maximum within a median 2.1–5.6% (rank corr 0.71–0.98), with DAS landing at median |cos| 0.89–0.98 of the predicted top eigenvector; the trace's midpoint-vs-base-point error relation (−½) holds in 880/882 cases; trace error median 0.05–3.8% of T. Model size: maximum grows 24–41% and cross-entropy DAS 16–29% smallest-to-largest within each family. Published work re-examined: CausalGym's DAS/vanilla gap (median 2.1–3.1) is 1.02–1.08× DAS's excess over G* (an excess, not a better region), and its released code passes base/source token positions to interventions swapped, which matters on 19/29 tasks (authors informed); the 109.8% sentiment direction re-runs at 114.7% [85.0, 137.4] containing 100%, five models' bands contain 100%, Pythia-410m's 198.8% only with a small-sample factor chosen after one initialisation. Accuracy benchmarks hide the excess once the copy gets every pair right (317 readout cases, 322 cells both at 1.0). Higher ranks: no trained run above the rank-k first-order maximum in 41,286 tests across the thirteen uncapped models; rank one can understate (IOI-20 below T at rank one in every model, above from rank 16); the 27 negative-polarity non-excess cases are where the base label's probability is a few thousandths, so cross-entropy's derivative on it is ~0. Limits they name: first order, base checkpoints only, rank one inside the network, G* bounded to one region plus one other region of its layer, decomposition covers orthogonal subspaces only (not sparse dictionaries or nonlinear featurizers), interchange does not transfer to steering/ablation/weight edits, and the Gemma-2 soft cap is separated out after seeing its results. Carry-away for us: the copy-at-the-site reference is the only LOCAL control in this folder's inventory and belongs beside every effect we publish, with the attention-frozen share; 'predicts the copy' (attribution) and 'produces the ceiling' (effect-seeking) are different quantities; and λ₁ is a pre-training computable prior with the search-on-train/score-on-test protocol our own predictor should imitate.
arxiv-2606.29522 | When Does Activation Steering Change What a Model Computes From? | Stanford University (ICME) | 2026-09 | 3 — full text (v2 HTML, ~109k chars in one fetch, read end to end: abstract, §1–§9, references 1–30, Appendices A–F incl. the frozen-route split tables and the full intervention-condition table; the page ends at F.3 extended related work, so no later material exists), NO figure inspected — Figures 1–11 are described only from the prose and captions, so nothing here quotes a curve. Two settings: (1) controlled state tracking on a walk over Q8 / D8 (order 8, non-commutative, each state = visible 4-way quotient coordinate + one ORDER-dependent hidden bit), Qwen2.5-Coder-7B (28 layers, d=3584) with three matched LoRA arms differing ONLY in whether intermediate states appear in the training target (base / final-answer-only / running-state), replicated in Mistral-7B-v0.3; (2) two lexical Function-Vector settings picked for strong effect (Qwen2.5-7B present-to-past, block 7 coef 15; Llama-3.1-8B English→Spanish, block 8 coef 10). Method: rank-16 register subspace U at layer 12 from class-conditional mean differences (calibration split disjoint from scoring; layer + site locked on validation), write the counterfactual by adding the projected mean difference while leaving the printed token unchanged, then ask whether the NEXT state follows the move applied to the edited value. Metrics: Use (agreement with move applied to the edited state), Counterfactual Update Selectivity, move-swap selectivity, conflicting-continuation selectivity, free-running branch persistence, κ = fraction of selectivity removed by ablation; localisation over 38 candidate components with matched-source controls + restoration under pre-declared thresholds (80% single, 70% distributed); steering scored as normalised margin gain against an all-layer target patch with paired 2,000-resample bootstraps. RESULTS: the register is real and used — Use 0.80 (Q8) / 0.91 (D8) vs ≈0.02 for same-rank random and orthogonal-complement edits, full-residual replacement no stronger; pretrained and final-answer-only arms stay at chance on Use while decoding the current token at 1.00, so readability does not distinguish arms and use does; move-swap +0.57/+0.68, conflicting continuation +0.59 (0.7975 vs 0.2025) / +0.81 (0.9025 vs 0.0975); Mistral replicates (Use 0.9265 / 0.9430); free-running D8 passes the pre-declared 0.70 bar (Use 0.902, persistence ≈0.70–0.72 to four states, final answer 0.845) while Q8 does not (0.660) though above chance. Register is rank-1 (Q8) / rank-2 (D8) in a 3,584-d stream, but its UPDATE ROUTE is not equally concentrated — one layer-22 edge removes 94% in Q8 (control ≈0.59, restoration 0.595) while in D8 no single edge exceeds 24% and a pre-frozen four-edge set removes 68.7% over ten splits (99.6th percentile of 1,000 random four-edge sets; route effect exceeds the sum of its singletons). Correspondence hypothesis FAILS on the lexical tasks: one-layer target patch gives 0.014 (Qwen) / −0.015 (Llama) of the all-layer gain, natural-scale steering 0.0073 / 0.0166, while calibrated vectors reach 0.544 / 0.824 at norms 210.80 / 20.22 = 77x / 26x the median natural change (2.74 / 0.78); specificity is model-dependent (Llama's equal-norm comparisons −0.316 to 0.085; Qwen's structured alternatives 0.612–0.665 vs 0.544 target, random 0.175); Function-vector steering does not write the Q8 register (2.19x larger update, median cosine 0.254, 5.7–13.4% of energy in the subspace); subtracting the vector early kills the effect; label remapping leaves part of it bound to the literal label. Limits they name: synthetic task with explicit trace, localisation only in the primary model, one task/layer per model chosen because steering worked (so the Qwen/Llama contrast is not a family claim), one-block patch failure rules out only local restoration, "unnatural" means only displacement vs matched natural change, and a supplementary sensitivity test missed its 1e-5 tolerance by ~1e-4 and is reported unused. Mine, marked: the positive result depends on the task FORCING the variable into a single supervised token — the condition most real behaviours lack — and the move-swap/conflicting-continuation controls are what make it a state test rather than an answer test. NOT in SEED_QUEUE; taken outside the queue as run 126's third flagged candidate (run 127 ran out of wall clock before reaching it), with filename + ID + title novelty check against all five literature folders (only NOTES matched).
arxiv-2610.17093 | SFT Leaves a Reproducible Signature in Activation Geometry: Calibrating Post-Training Interpretability Against Run-to-Run Noise | NYU / USC / Columbia | 2026-10 | 3 — full text (arXiv HTML v1, ~224k chars, read end to end in slices: abstract, §1–§4.3, Tables 1–16, Appendices A–N incl. limitations, seed-null band accounting, layer-index diagnostic, withdrawn claims), NO figure inspected (Figs. 1–8 described only via prose/caption, so no curve value is quoted). Qwen3-1.7B on BFCL function calling: 4 LoRA-SFT runs (r16/α32, 0.37% of params, 29 steps), 4 LoRA-DPO runs at 12 steps + 4 retrained read at 29/60, six full-parameter GRPO-family runs (accuracy-only baseline, 4 seeds, 1 shaped λ=0.1, 100–300 steps); replication on Llama-3.1-8B (4 LoRA-SFT) + public Tulu-3 staircase (base→SFT→DPO→RLVR-PPO with Tulu-3.1-8B as second RLVR from the same DPO parent). Unit = sparse co-activation graph: 10k edges, src l14 → dst l22, SAE trained on the BASE only (qwen-scope TopK k=50 d=32,768; Llama-Scope for the Llama arm), 500 Pile prompts, top-2,000 products per state then global top-10,000; similar  = edge Jaccard, displacement = 1 − J. Method: score each base→post contrast against the run-to-run band of its OWN recipe (m = k(k−1)/2 seed pairs, z + an outside-the-band count); precision rule (score a statistic only if seed SD > 10× the 0.0001 reporting step — keeps edge Jaccard, demotes weighted cosine); one-architecture build rule (rebuild moves Jaccard by ~one seed-pair distance across GPUs); matched-base control, degree-preserving permutation null, random-direction encoder. RESULTS: SFT detected against its own reruns — base→SFT 17.8 pairwise-SD beyond the mean of 4 independent LoRA-SFT runs, 5/5 contrasts outside; replicates on Llama-3.1-8B (4/4, nearest at 1.7× the band max). At 29 steps 4 SFT vs 4 DPO runs occupy DISJOINT displacement ranges (0.0571–0.0615 vs 0.0317–0.0356 on one architecture, gap several × either spread; ≤1/35 permutation reading, post hoc), i.e. no band needed. DPO counts 3/4, 1/4, 3/4 outside at 12/29/60 steps with displacement flat ⇒ no dose ordering, no single z promoted, and the earlier 0/4,2/4,4/4 dose reading is WITHDRAWN as within the rebuild difference. Light GRPO = non-detection 0/5 (no equivalence test run). The concentration statistic earlier drafts read as an RL signature is ALREADY IN THE ZERO-RL BASE (base Gini 0.728 / top-1%-active 0.427 vs GRPO 0.730 / 0.429; 31–32 of top-32 hubs shared). Two self-caught artifacts: mean L0 pinned at exactly 50.0 on every Qwen checkpoint by the TopK dictionary (a constant, not a null), and an apparent concentration scaling law that dies under a width-independent denominator (honest 1.7B→8B 0.7110→0.8395; residual rise mechanical, 655 vs 328 features in a fixed 1%). Localization: 63% of dropped weight goes to ONE hub — the base graph's largest generic numeric/whitespace/markup feature — and SFT-added edges are diffuse. Registered causal sweep (4,816 held-out prompts, 3 budgets): SFT-specific sources load-bearing vs random (12× mean, 3.5× median at 10k edges) but the stage-specificity interaction REVERSES at 5k/10k and the 2k gate fails ⇒ verdict MIXED; the word "rewiring" is retired and displacement is explicitly not mechanism. Tulu-3 at production scale: levels stay near base, SFT+DPO write most displacement, no RLVR increment resolved above a single run pair (≤1/3 under exchangeability). Limits they name: one 1.7B backbone for the decisive RL control; gemma-2-2b-it cross-family pair is heavy instruct + chat-template confound; long-horizon math-RLVR out of envelope; top-32 panel H1-selected; seed band conflates seed/step/reward ⇒ conservative for detection, ANTI-conservative for certifying the RL null; on-task BFCL arm uninformative (MDE ~3× SFT's actual effect) rather than null; OLMo-2 staircase blocked (no public base SAE); DPO arms differ in lr and dropout. Mine, marked: Proposition 1 is the honest headline and should travel without the paper — k runs certify no better than 1/(k+1) under run exchangeability however large z is, so every "probe direction survived a rerun" claim in this folder is bounded by its k, and the band belongs to its recipe (borrowing another stage's band is the substitution error the paper exists to catch). NOT in SEED_QUEUE (queue exhausted); picked from outside it because four existing Patterns lines assert probe-direction stability across a second fine-tuning seed and no entry had that as its PRIMARY experiment. ID + key checked against all literature folders before fetching (no hits).
arxiv-2602.15778 | *-PLUIE: Personalisable metric with LLM Used for Improved Evaluation | Univ Rennes / CNRS / IRISA, Nantes, South Brittany | 2026-05 | 2 — full text (arXiv HTML v2, ~97.6k chars, read end to end: §1–§5, Limitations, Ethical Considerations, references 1–40, Appendices A–I incl. the PLUIE log-odds identity, the French-paraphrase and ParaReval construction, the full judge and *-PLUIE prompts, threshold sweeps, the per-judge table and the Net-PLUIE alignment-function test), NO figure inspected — Figs. 2–11 known only from prose and captions, so no curve is quoted; Tables 1–10 read as text. Revision metric sitting on top of ParaPLUIE: score = log(p(Yes|prompt)/p(No|prompt)), which Appendix A shows is a difference of mean log-perplexity over prompt+answer and, under a log-perplexity loss, a length-scaled difference of losses — one forward pass, both candidate tokens read off the same distribution, so one decoder pass suffices however many answers are compared. Judges Phi-4 14B and Llama 3 70B (Appendix H adds Llama 3 8B, Mistral 7B, GPT-4o, GPT-4o mini; Table 10 adds SmolLM2 135M/360M/1.7B as perplexity models). Three tasks: French paraphrase (1,914 pairs, 33.6% positive, from Tytgat paronym/synonym transformations), Nile network-intent translation acceptability (436 triplets, ~60% positive), paragraph scientific revision (ParaReval test split, 258 pairs x 2 instructions = 516 instances, human 44/41/15% A/B/tie). Four templates compared (original, Fr-, Net-, Rev-PLUIE) against BLEU/METEOR/Levenshtein/BERTScore/Modern BERTScore and three generating judges (yes-no, pairwise choice, 5-point Likert); every metric scored twice, at the NATURAL threshold zero and at the F1-maximising cut. RESULTS: French paraphrase at threshold 0 Fr-PLUIE 0.74 acc / 0.58 F1 (Phi-4) and 0.70/0.58 (Llama) vs 0.74/0.56 and 0.71/0.52 for the yes-no judge and 0.84 acc / 0.49 F1 for Modern BERTScore whose recall at that cut is 0.33 (labels nearly everything a paraphrase — the authors say so); Nile everything sensible clusters at 0.80–0.81 acc / ~0.85 F1 and the BEST cell is the ORIGINAL template at a calibrated cut, not the personalised one; revision preference Rev-PLUIE 0.61 pair-acc / V 0.61 / κ 0.31 (Phi-4) and 0.62/0.34 (Llama w/ gold) against 0.53–0.60 for every generating judge and ≤0.45 for n-gram/embedding metrics, best or second-best across all six judges in Appendix H. Runtime Table 3 (revision, Llama judge): 124 min (127 w/ ref) generating vs 33 min (55) *-PLUIE ⇒ ~3.8x, while the quoted headline 'up to 7.9x' is the best cell anywhere in the table, not the median. Sharpest evidence is Appendix I: two implication conditions any same-meaning predicate must satisfy, tested over 430 Nile–NL triplets using the score in BOTH argument orders because it is asymmetric — 56 fail condition 5 and 7 fail condition 6, i.e. ~1 triplet in 8 has two NL statements called equivalent that are not both aligned to the same formal intent; authors report it as a bound, with example rows. Limits they name: perplexity models not fine-tuned (fine-tuning untested), 70B ceiling, mostly English + French (morphologically rich languages open), only Yes/No-form templates, one-token restriction liftable but unevaluated. Mine, marked: the natural threshold is shown USABLE not CALIBRATED (optimal cuts in Table 1 span −13.73 to +3.97 across metrics/models, so the zero point is a fixed guess, not an estimated offset); the multi-token generalisation is biased toward the shorter answer by the authors' own admission (their eq. 4); there is NO random-template or random-question control, so token prior / answer position / role-marker handling are untested confounds the paper itself warns about; the score is validated only against human labels, never against a downstream behavioural consequence of the judgement it makes (the readability-vs-use gap again); and the 1e-3 intervals are sampling intervals over one dataset per task with no seed or run replication, so the 1/(k+1) bound on what k runs certify applies in full. NOT in SEED_QUEUE; taken from outside the queue after the read-it list was exhausted, chosen because the folder uses two-token contrast scores as dependent variables everywhere and had never owned the paper that argues they are sufficient.
arxiv-2609.31796 | Same Probe, Different Numbers: Are Activation Probes Robust to Inference-Time Numerical Non-Determinism? | Wrynx Inc. (Khatri) | 2026-09 | 3 — full arXiv HTML v1 (~72.5k chars): abstract, §1–§7, AI-use/Ethics/Reproducibility statements, references, Appendices A–J; NO figure inspected, so every number is prose or a table cell. First measurement of inference-time numerical non-determinism ON THE REPRESENTATIONS probes consume rather than on outputs or trained weights. Batch size {4,8,16} × dtype {fp32,bf16,fp16} × 4 depths × 4 positions (prefill + gen 5/10/20), Llama-3.1-8B / Qwen3-8B / Gemma-3-4B on one L4 (8B fp32 moved to A100, so GPU and dtype confound there); 768 probes (LR, MLP-1, MLP-3) trained at B=4 in one precision and transferred to all cells, 5,568 measurements, plus per-example agreement on fixed checkpoints. Prefill: fp32 batch-invariant (99.9–100% bitwise identical for Llama/Qwen); bf16 batch-size noise ~8x fp16 (median rel L2 1.3e-2 vs 1.7e-3 Llama; ratio 7.8/7.7 = the 3 mantissa bits) and for Gemma, same GPU, a bf16 batch-size change (4.9e-3) exceeds the whole fp32→bf16 conversion (4.4e-3); no directional drift (224/224 cells fit independent draws, not the additive model), no depth amplification (median per-block factor 1.01), CKA ≥0.995 but 5-NN overlap worse than 50-NN. Probe accuracy across 1,392 prefill transfers moves ≤0.47 pp, 74% exactly unchanged, and only 0.076% of verdicts flip; fp32 batch-size-only flips 0 of 201,960 verdicts. Decode: same noise flips the first token for 2.1% of rows in bf16 and 25% by token 20 (fp16 0.4%/3.6%), fp32 nowhere; decode accuracy changes in 69–80% of cells, max 2.35 pp — but per-example agreement localises it: matched-token rows flip at a flat 0.12–0.15%, diverged rows at 12.9%, and the diverged rate is constant across perturbations (12.5–15.8%), so the TEXT changes, not the arithmetic. Accuracy understates churn by 2x at the prompt and 9x at token 20; flips symmetric, kappa ≥0.94, AUROC moves ≤0.05. Sharpest number: one bf16 batch-size change flips 0.14% of probe verdicts but 2.1% of the model's own argmaxes — 15x — because a binary boundary has margin and an argmax over ~1e5 tokens usually does not. Gemma-3-4B fp16 is a validity failure (NaN at 80% of stored sites), excluded throughout. Their own gap: no seed baseline, so 0.47 pp has no noise floor. Four limits of mine: pooled churn is a boundary artefact unmeasured at the operating point (1% FPR is where those examples live); no matched-random-perturbation control; activations only read on the residual stream, never inside MLP/attn output where dictionaries read; B=4–16 on fixed kernels is the most reproducible corner, so this is a floor. NOT in SEED_QUEUE; outside-queue pick, chosen because every probe figure in this folder is a single-configuration number and no entry owned the reliability question.
arxiv-2608.20134 | Feature Evolution and Migration during Vision Transformer Training | University of Tartu (Estonia) | 2026-08 | 3 — full text (arXiv HTML v1, ~78.7k chars, read end to end: abstract, §1–§6 incl. all Eq. 10–20 metrics, GenAI disclosure, references, Appendices A.1–A.8 with Tables 1–2); no figure inspected, so every number is a prose sentence or table cell. ViT-Tiny (12 blocks, 192 dim, 12 heads) supervised on ImageNet-1k (72.55% acc) and Mixed 10 (86.37%); a BatchTopK SAE at EVERY layer × EVERY checkpoint on CLS tokens (K=32, expansion 5 to 960 latents, 150 epochs), and each latent scored across the epoch-layer grid by the MAX of two Spearman profile correlations — a fixed/transferred score (does one encoder row stay a valid readout) and an independent-SAE row-wise best match (is it rediscovered). Findings: migration is mostly toward EARLIER layers and early in training (deeper migration peaks at about 4% of features; mean signed drift reaches roughly minus 2.5 layers in the strongest early/early-layer region); deeper layers stabilise first and most; early-layer features are long-lived yet NOT stable (lifetime is not stability); widely spread features concentrate early and in early layers; class-specific feature counts rise monotonically across reference epochs 1/100/200/300 (70/259/401/461 of 825/959/1308/1406 active) with no correlation between feature type and migration; label entropy by depth inverts between the two datasets, blamed on expansion being too small (960 latents for 1,000 classes). CKA on the same grid reproduces the trend without the per-feature resolution. Zero interventions, one seed, one architecture — relevance 3 for the trajectory vocabulary and the transportability-vs-identity split, not for causal claims. Their own limits: no multi-seed, linearly decodable features only, CLS stream only, supervised only, no guarantee the same feature is found at two points. Mine, marked as mine: the max-of-two rule hides the components' disagreement rate; "migration" is partly circular because the profile is computed on a layer's own geometry; no permuted-label/untrained control grid; class-specificity is driver-of-the-heatmap, not a measured cause.

arxiv-2606.02061 | Ablating Archetypes: The Stability of Archetypal SAEs is an Artifact of Initialization and Metric Design | University of Warsaw (Brzozowski, Chung) | 2026-06 | 3 — full text (arXiv HTML v1, ~44k chars, read end to end: abstract, §1–§6, Limitations, references, Appendix A hyperparameters, Appendix B code audit); no figure inspected, so both figures are described only from the prose that explains them. Two settings: DinoV2 patch embeddings from one image class, and Pythia-160m-deduped layer-6 residual stream on Pile (768-dim, 4,096 atoms, k=20, 50M tokens for trajectories, 20 epochs for ablations). Central distinction: STABILITY (two runs agree at the end) vs STABILIZATION (runs from genuinely different starting points converge) — archetypal SAEs share a deterministic k-means centroid matrix with W initialised to identity, so inter-run decoder distance is exactly zero at step 0 and endpoint agreement is the initialisation being preserved, not a convergence test. Trajectory diagnostic: classical TopK starts high and falls monotonically; arch-fixed-init is flat at zero; arch-random-init starts near 0.88 but converges at about half the rate, ending near 0.35 against about 0.17 for classical TopK. Two ablations identify the cause in both directions: disabling the convex-hull projection IMPROVES endpoint stability over full archetypal (vision 0.068 vs 0.243; Pythia 0.112 vs 0.170), and a classical TopK SAE merely initialised from the same centroids matches the ablated model numerically (0.068 / 0.112) while beating random init (0.433 / 0.200); full archetypal has the worst R² in both settings (0.542 vs 0.570–0.573; 0.758 vs 0.914–0.937). Metric confound: uncentered k-means centroids have mean pairwise cosine similarity 0.362 (LLM) / 0.298 (vision), collapsing to ~0 after centering — a 0.30–0.36 free boost for any method whose atoms stay near the data mean (cosine measures angle from the origin). Appendix B audits the reference code: the archetypal paper states element-wise standardisation but neither the Colab nor Overcomplete centers at all, and per-coordinate rescaling is unjustified for residual streams because the storage basis is arbitrary. Their limits: no hyperparameter sweep, one image class, one layer of a 160M model, no claim that the archetypal construction is worthless, and they decline to adjudicate the TopK-vs-L1 stability conflict. Relevance 3 for the instrument, not for any claim about what atoms mean.

Llama 3.3 Nemotron Super 49B made evaluation-aware by synthetic-document fine-tuning (type hints in eval, not deployment) + a Wood Labs always-evaluation cue + 4 rounds of expert iteration; steering vector = mean difference of 16 hand-written deployment/evaluation prompt pairs taken from the PRE-fine-tuning model, applied at 6 layers (0.6 each, effective 3.6). Type-hint rate 80–86% eval vs 33–34% deployed; steered-to-deployment <11% in eval contexts (below the deployed rate), steered-to-evaluation >95%; all 16 contrastive prompts as a prompt baseline only nudges it, one of 21 alternate deployment prompts reaches 60%; unit-test accuracy 56.8% -> 55.2% (deploy-steer) vs 43.1% (eval-steer); 24 norm/variance-matched Gaussian vectors do not reproduce the drop; identical intervention on the base model does nothing; the post-hoc declarative-prompt addendum (INFO: Not evaluation. -> 34–37%) is the paper's honest retreat from 'prompting cannot'. The reusable kit is the *ground-truth-first* design (known behaviour in both conditions turns 'the verbalisation vanished' into 'the known deployment behaviour appeared') plus the base-model null and the exact prompt-only baseline, and the gap the authors themselves leave open — verifying success without a text channel — is precisely an activation-oracle job.
anthropic-com-research-next-generation-constitutional-classifiers | Next-generation Constitutional Classifiers (summary post) | Anthropic (paper: Cunningham, Wei, Persik et al., arXiv:2601.04603) | 2026-01 | 2 — full text of the ~9.4k-char summary page (whole page, no figures); the linked paper was NOT re-fetched, so its numbers are taken from our existing arxiv-2601.04603 entry and labelled as such. Value is the design rationale in prose where the paper gives tables — read it as the argument, not the evidence. Two attack families are named as the failure of separate input/output classifiers: reconstruction (harmful request fragmented across benign pieces, then reassembly requested) and output obfuscation (reagents relabelled 'food flavourings', allegory/riddle mappings); both degrade capability (GPQA Diamond 74%→32% under some) but the degradation VARIES, which is the authors' own reason to expect a high-capability-preserving version. Fixes, in order: an exchange classifier that scores a response in the context of its prompt (over half of successful jailbreaks cut, +50% compute); a cascade where the cheap first stage is allowed a HIGH false-positive rate because flagged exchanges escalate instead of refusing (the reusable design move: make escalation free, refusal rare); and a linear probe over intermediate activations that is nearly free at inference and justified by attack asymmetry (inputs can be shaped, intermediates are harder) plus two-way complementarity with the external classifier — a claim that needs an adversarial attack on the probe to earn, and none is reported. Production numbers: 0.05% harmless-query refusal over one month of Sonnet 4.5 traffic (87% drop from the original system), ~1% compute overhead vs Opus 4.0, 1,700+ red-teaming hours / 198,000 attempts → one high-risk vulnerability, 0.005 detections per thousand queries, no universal jailbreak found. No ablations on this page, so no causal attribution of those numbers to the probe is supported here — that lives in the paper entry. Carry-away for our own probes: test the read-out on inputs where the same information is split or renamed, since content-level monitors fail structurally on exactly that transformation.
arxiv-2606.09899 | When Attribution Patching Lies: Diagnosis and a Second-Order Correction | CMU / UC Santa Cruz (Zhang, Wang) | 2026-06 | 3 — full text (arXiv HTML v1, ~110k chars read end to end: §1–§5, refs 1–41, Appendices A–E incl. notation table, L3 estimation tables, four proofs, matched-compute tables, MIB results, L4H11 case study); NO figure inspected, so only prose/table values are quoted. Seven checkpoints in five families (GPT-2 small, Pythia-410M and 2.8B, Qwen2.5-1.5B, Gemma-2-2B/9B, Llama-3.1-8B) hooked with TransformerLens; unit = one (component, prompt) pair scored on correct-token log-probability under matched-input perturbation; heads (pre-out-projection), pre/post-activation MLP neurons, MLP layer outputs, residual stream, plus an SAE-feature appendix (GPT-2 layers 5/9, JumpReLU 768→24,576). Task settings: IOI (name-swap, 50 prompts), Greater-Than (random token, 200), factual completion (20, extended to 55/35). Argument is a Taylor decomposition: error = ½δᵀHδ (one HVP) + cubic remainder, under a path-local Hessian-Lipschitz assumption verified by probing the Hessian at three interpolation points; reliability score = |δᵀHδ| / (2|first-order estimate|) flags suspect components without ground truth; multi-step variant splits the patch into K sub-steps for an O(1/K²) residual. RESULTS: network-level curvature exceeds local activation curvature by 2–66× at near-zero correlation (r = 0.04) and local-curvature repair gives >750% error — worse than no correction, which is why prior local fixes are structurally incomplete; one-step correction cuts top-5 relative error 72–90% on heads, 56–67% on pre-act neurons, ~2/3 on the residual stream, but only 2–7% on post-act neurons (nonlinearity already applied); Pythia-410M heads 4.4%→1.0% and neurons 35.2%→11.7%, versus 34.1%/40.9% head reduction for the region-matching and softmax-repair baselines; that softmax-repair baseline degrades circuit recovery in 26/27 settings (up to 21.8 pp worse on Llama factual). Pathological regime (Pythia-410M IOI, perturbation outside the local radius): single step overshoots 22.3%→47.5%, multi-step recovers monotonically to 18.03% at K=5, beating integrated gradients at cost 10 versus cost 35 (18.03% vs 18.47%, p=0.022); at matched cost ties it on 7/9 tasks and wins 2. Integrated Hessians trail by 3.8–7.2 pp because their path weighting gives coefficient ≈1/4 on the quadratic term where Taylor needs 1/2. At 8B: integrated gradients quoted as ~25 GPU-days/task, integrated Hessians undemonstrated, HVP finishes in single-digit GPU-hours and cuts error 5–57%, multi-step reaching 82% reduction on 8B IOI. Diagnostic: score concentrates in the IOI-circuit layers (11–15) of Pythia-410M; pooled area 0.70 [0.67,0.73] for flagging >50% error with rank correlation 0.48, 89% recall at 36% precision when flagging 40% of components, but >0.97 area in the middle/upper stratifications of perturbation and effect size. Selective correction captures 91% of full-correction gain at 26% of cost; at a fixed threshold flags 7.0%/14.9%/19.0% of components while cutting median error 10.1%/23.9%/30.1%. Circuit recovery improves at boundaries (GPT-2 IOI top-20 overlap 95%→100%, the L4H11 duplicate-token head underestimated 4.4× and flagged at score 1.32, moving rank 27→12) but circuit-level faithfulness is MIXED: better on Gemma-2-2B IOI/MCQA and GPT-2 IOI, worse on Llama-3.1-8B IOI (over-concentrated edge scores, circuit exceeds model performance at mid-sparsity) and far worse than joint input-level interpolation on a distributed arithmetic task. SAE features: per-feature error down 81.5%/85.5% at layers 5/9. Limits they name: single-token perturbations, ≤9B, no completeness/minimality, selective bound holds only for independently patched components, cubic constant probed in one direction (bound holds for 82%/95% of GPT-2/Gemma-2-2B pairs). Mine, marked: ground truth is single-component patching on a scalar log-prob read-out, so the correction makes a cheap estimator agree with an expensive one and both can be wrong about the mechanism; the recovery wins land at thresholds where the answer is nearly known; pooled diagnostic quality is far below stratified quality. NOT in SEED_QUEUE (queue exhausted); chosen from outside it because gradient-attribution error is the standing open problem this folders evidence rests on, after filename+ID+title novelty check against aether/ and core/.
arxiv-2602.16698 | Position: Causality is Key for Interpretability Claims to Generalise | Mila / Boston University / CSHL / MPI-IS Tübingen / UdeM (Joshi, Mueller, Klindt, Brendel, Reizinger, Sridhar) | 2026-02 | 3 — full text (arXiv HTML v1, ~165k chars read end to end: §1–§5, refs, Appendices A–G incl. ladder reference, method-family estimand table, claim taxonomy D/E/F, and the pilot study G.1–G.4 with codebook and calibration rationales); no figures, all numbers from prose or tables. A POSITION paper on claim–evidence calibration, not a model study: Pearl's ladder (L1 association / L2 intervention / L3 counterfactual) plus causal-representation-learning identifiability, giving estimand + intervention class + equivalence class + transportability as the four things an interpretability claim must state. Five worked counterexamples: decodability≠encoding, local sensitivity≠global importance, existence≠uniqueness ('THE circuit'), alignment≠ontological identity (a label is a property of direction+contrast set, report (z,c,π)), subspace≠coordinates. Pilot: 50 papers / 186 claims annotated by Claude Opus 4.5 against a codebook — 53.5% of the 185 scorable claims admit a stronger causal reading than the method licenses (46.5% no gap, 76 = 41.1% one rung, 23 = 12.4% two rungs); gap rate 53.3% in abstracts vs 52.9% elsewhere (so not abstract compression); by type circuit discovery 0.64 > knowledge localisation 0.50 > other 0.47 > eval/benchmark 0.45 > applied 0.35; annotator confidence falls monotonically with gap (4.94 / 4.39 / 4.04); 12 papers human-verified, 84% of claims needed no correction; conservative reclassification of definite-article-only gaps moves 53.5%→47.0%; no inter-rater reliability, single LLM annotator, paper list unpublished. THE reusable piece is the L2-vs-L3 distinction: activation patching performs the action step of abduction–action–prediction but supplies neither the abduction that fixes exogenous factors nor a structural model to propagate, so patching reports what the model did under one edit and cannot by itself predict what it would have done — the exact gap a predictive white-box model is trying to close. Second reusable piece: identifiability-as-affordance — a direction is only recoverable to the extent the contrast set varies its factors independently, which is a construction-time check, not a post-hoc criticism. Also carries a practitioners' checklist (estimand / method rung / claim rung / alternatives not ruled out / robustness varied / scope) that our own write-ups should inherit. NOT in SEED_QUEUE (queue exhausted, every arXiv item already has a file); chosen after filename+ID+title novelty check against aether/, core/ and helix_research/.
arxiv-2605.10664 | Prompt–Activation Duality: Improving Activation Steering via Attention-Level Interventions | Southern University of Science and Technology / University of Notre Dame / MIT | 2026-05 | 3 — full text (arXiv HTML v1, ~92k chars: §1–§7, refs, Appendices A–J incl. judge prompts, per-trait per-turn tables for both models, no-steering baseline, MLP-vs-attention pathway comparison, limitations); no figure inspected, so all numbers are prose or table cells. The claim is a LOCATION claim about interventions: unrolling the residual recurrence splits an ordinary persona vector into embedding + Σ attention-output deltas + Σ MLP-output deltas, the attention term is the prompt-mediated channel, and the constant-coefficient single-site residual injection everyone uses pays a coherence cost that compounds because steered states are written into the KV cache and re-read. Method (GCAD): extract per-layer deltas from the attention output cropped to system-prompt source tokens only (no renormalisation, eager attention to expose post-softmax weights), reinject at the attention output before the MLP, scaled by a per-token centred-sigmoid gate over query–key compatibility with the mean system-prompt key — redistribution of steering strength, not amplification, and one dot product per head at inference. Qwen2.5-7B + Llama-3.1-8B, 15/10 persona traits, 10-turn dialogues with persisted cache, 60 conversations per trait, two independent GPT-4.1-mini 0–100 rubrics (trait, coherence). 15-trait average: coherence drift −18.6 → −1.9, turn-10 trait 78.0 → 93.1. No-steering control fixes the denominator (drift −5.4, traits at floor) so the baseline's loss is the intervention's cost — with their own counterexample, hallucinating drifting 22.8 → 67.7 unsteered. The most transferable result is the pathway experiment: attention-pathway delta vs MLP-pathway delta, both injected at all 28 layers at coefficient 1.0, gives 85.2 vs 20.8 average trait expression (evil 1.6 → 97.6) — an ablation over WRITE SITES. Read with care: the gating/cropping ablations are mixed (dropping both is best on trait mean for evil and on both metrics for humorous), there is no random-direction or norm-matched control, no significance testing, no capability benchmark, and the one judge is the instrument. Carry-away for us: test interventions with the cache persisted across turns, report drift separately from one-shot success, and treat "where the model routes this itself" as the prior for placement. NOT in SEED_QUEUE (queue exhausted — all 149 lines ticked or held under other keys); chosen from outside it after filename+ID+title novelty check against aether/ and core/, because it is the first entry in the folder whose subject is the intervention's own long-horizon reliability rather than the vector's existence.
arxiv-2509.13316 | Do Activation Verbalization Methods Convey Privileged Information? | Northeastern / Kempner (Harvard) / Boston University | 2026-05 | 3 — full text (arXiv HTML v4, ~156k chars, abstract + §1–§8 + appendices A–D, G, H read end to end, E/F/I via the main text), NO figure inspected. Controlled audit of activation verbalization (Patchscopes, SelfIE, LIT) on Llama-3.1-8B-Instruct and Ministral-8B; its criterion is *privileged information* — what the text tells you that black-box prompting could not. Three findings: (i) a text-only zero-shot baseline matches or beats every verbalizer on the six feature-extraction sets (0.64 vs LIT 0.64 / Patchscopes 0.38 on Llama3; 0.68 vs 0.61 / 0.27 on Ministral), so those benchmarks only show that input text survives into the activation; (ii) inverting the activation back to text and answering from the reconstruction reproduces most of the verbalization score (LIT 0.79 vs 0.79, 0.52 vs 0.45, 0.46 vs 0.41; athlete tasks fall) and, at matched single-token granularity, beats Patchscopes on all six, so much of the credit belongs to the input prompt; (iii) with knowledge deliberately misaligned the verbalizer answers from its own weights — on shuffled-persona targets it scores higher on the labels it expects than on the target's actual ones, on fully invented personas both methods score 0.00, and when the verbalizer is fantasy-trained but the target uninformed scores rise to 0.37–0.50 where faithfulness requires 0.00. Prompt fragility is large (up to 60% drop on one relation set from a paraphrase, net −0.044 LIT / −0.014 Patchscopes; adversarial phrasings flip judgments), and the same Patchscopes run dispatched in parallel vs serially averages 0.38 vs 0.49. Cross-family verbalization collapses (≤0.20 for LIT). Carried into our work: the input-only baseline as a mandatory control for every read-out, the disagreement-case design as the only test that separates "the model's knowledge" from shared world knowledge, and the rule that probing datasets must encode knowledge only the target has.
arxiv-2604.11791 | A Mechanistic Analysis of Looped Reasoning Language Models | University of Oxford / Mila (Université de Montréal) | 2026-04 | 3 — full text (arXiv HTML v1, ~101k chars end to end incl. both proofs and Appendices A–F); figures not inspected, so all numbers are prose/table cells. Looped/recurrent-depth LMs (Ouro 1.4B, Huginn-0125 3.5B, retrofitted Llama-3.2-1B / OLMo-2-1B / TinyLlama, plus small from-scratch models) analysed on a (layer x recurrence) grid rather than a depth axis, on 256 GSM8k examples with HellaSwag replication. Two propositions: a cyclic block hitting a fixed point implies every cyclic shift does, and because block weights are tied and pre-attention inputs are norm-bounded the state-to-attention map is Lipschitz in the QK product — so state convergence forces constant attention patterns. Empirically Huginn and the retrofitted models reach a per-layer fixed point fast (Ouro does not, despite tiny successive differences); random-init 12-layer models reproduce the per-layer fixed points whenever input injection is present (all norms but Ouro's), and without it only pre-norm converges, degenerately (all layers to one state) — so the cyclic structure is architectural, not learned. Non-fixed-point tokens are rare (0.02% without a system prompt, 0.14% with the long persona prompt, 0.05% with padding tokens, so not length); 2.81% of examples show one under the persona prompt. ColSum concentration repeats a feedforward mixing shape inside each loop, retrofitted models inherit their base model's stages (first/last stages once in prelude/coda), Ouro 2.6B shows the pattern twice because upcycling duplicated the layers, Huginn shows NO stages and the paper attributes that to residual-normalisation blocking massive activations (zeroing the responsible MLP in retrofitted Llama removes its stages too). At 128 recurrences fixed-point models hold their per-layer mixing constant while Ouro drifts, matching published performance degradation beyond trained recurrence counts — a cross-model-card correlation, not a controlled test. NOT in SEED_QUEUE (priority block and queue both 0 unticked); chosen from outside it after filename+ID+title novelty check across aether/, core/ and helix_research/ — the folder had no entry on recurrent-depth architectures, i.e. on what "layer" means when effective depth exceeds parameter depth.
arxiv-2608.03913 | Sparse Weight Decomposition for Efficient Circuit Extraction | IQuest Research / Safe AI Forum / Oxford / Stanford / Tsinghua | 2026-08 | 3 — full text (arXiv HTML v1, ~139k chars end to end: §1–§6, references, Appendices A–K incl. factorizer hyperparameters, both data tables, zero-ablation robustness, exact-dense controls, zero-data analysis, the semantic audit table, the single-unit edit protocol and the QK screen); figures not inspected, so every number is a prose sentence or a table cell. Sparse Weight Decomposition (SWD) factorizes a dense weight matrix post hoc into two factors with hard caps on nonzeros, so each intermediate coordinate is a rank-one read/write path with its own sparse input and output support — a parameter-space circuit unit whose connectivity is countable and whose activation is separately ablatable, with no replacement network trained. Matched-fidelity circuit comparison (pre-pruning CE within 0.001) on GPT-2 Small, Qwen2.5 0.5B–3B and Qwen3.5-27B, four Edge-Pruning/auto-circuit tasks: same sufficiency and necessity at fewer active edges than transcoders and VPD-Recon-CI. Data efficiency is the headline — thousands of calibration tokens against ~1e6 optimizer-replay tokens for the baselines; full-model GPT-2 replacement reaches CE 3.44 after 20.6M tokens against a weight-sparse pretrained 3.45 after 2.884B at ~68% sparsity. Controls that make the comparison real: exact SVD and random-orthogonal factorizations reproduce the matrix exactly but need more edges, and zero ablation at fixed rankings preserves the advantage (12,303 vs 184,320 edges at sufficiency 1.0 on greater-than). Also the most useful `additivity` numbers in the folder — post-GELU residual-contribution correlation 0.0049 at the 95th percentile (0.0089 at the 99th), pre-GELU GELU-mediated interaction ratio median 0.089 / max 0.221 over 8,128 pairs — plus a zero-data (weights-only) variant that still yields circuits: 0.962 docstring sufficiency from 3 units at s = 0.375. Honest weaknesses: MLP-replacement results are mixed (transcoder competitive on units and sufficiency), full-model replacement only on GPT-2 Small, 27B is one matrix, audit is six units, sell is non-identifiable by their own admission. NOT in SEED_QUEUE (priority block and queue both 0 unticked, re-verified); chosen from outside the queue after filename+ID+title novelty check against aether/, core/ and helix_research/ — the folder held parameter-decomposition and SAE/transcoder entries but nothing on factorizing weights into sparse read/write paths, i.e. no entry where the reported cost is literally a count of active connections.
arxiv-2510.00845 | Mechanistic Interpretability as Statistical Estimation: A Variance Analysis | Université Grenoble Alpes / CNRS / Grenoble INP (LIG) | 2026-05 | 3 — full text (arXiv HTML v4, main body §1–§6, references, and the entire appendix incl. Tables 3–10 and Figures 5–8); figures not inspected directly, so all values are prose or table cells. Circuit discovery reframed as statistical estimation: per-input causal mediation is estimated from a finite dataset, aggregated, then discretised by a selection heuristic, so the circuit is an estimate with a sampling distribution rather than a fact about the network. Measured across gpt2-small, Llama-3.2-1B and Llama-3.2-1B-Instruct on IOI, SVA and greater-than, varying four axes — 100 bootstrap resamples, meta-dataset and prompt-paraphrase shifts, intervention-noise amplitude swept over nine values, and estimator/aggregation/patching hyperparameters (125 circuits for the headline spread, 330 for edge frequencies). Findings, in order of transferability: exact per-edge causal scores have CV ≈ 0.5 across inputs and attribution patching raises it above 1 (sd exceeds the mean, per-edge SNR < 1); bootstrap circuits overlap at mean pairwise Jaccard 0.561 (CV 0.335) and those overlapping least are least faithful (circuit error 0.440), while meta-dataset 0.790 and paraphrase 0.799 are far more stable (errors 0.300/0.150) — semantically different prompts reproduce the circuit better than resamples of the same generator; stability degrades from gpt2-small to the 1B Llama models while instruction tuning changes little; estimator variants do not converge but select different artefacts of the noisy score distribution (Llama-Instruct greater-than Jaccard to median 0.086 when aggregation moves sum→median and patching mean→patching; IOI IG vs clean/corrupted 0.071; gpt2-small SVA returns 1 edge under one variant and 21 under another at identical 0.06 circuit error); circuit structure is not invariant to perturbation strength, with overlap CV peaking near noise amplitude 0.2. The single most usable number is the selection-frequency distribution: over 330 circuits only 464 of gpt2-small's 32,491 edges are ever selected, 231 appear in ≥10% of circuits, 94 in ≥30%, and 7 in >80% — a stable core under a noise periphery. Recommendations: report mean pairwise Jaccard and circuit-error CV under ≥100 bootstrap resamples, publish hyperparameter sensitivity, sweep the counterfactual rather than fixing it, and prefer a posterior over graphs to a point estimate. Their own suggested bar is mean Jaccard > 0.8 under bootstrap, flagged as tentative. Honest limits: exact-score variance only on gpt2-small for two tasks, all tasks are discrete linguistic rules, the analysis is EAP-family-specific (generality argued, not shown), and the metrics weight all edges equally so a stable functional core under a fluctuating periphery would be invisible — a gap their own Table 3 shows needs filling. NOT in SEED_QUEUE (priority block 0 unticked of 37; SEED_QUEUE.md 154 lines, 0 unticked); chosen from outside the queue after filename+ID+title novelty check across aether/, core/ and helix_research/, because the folder had ~20 entries reporting circuits and no entry that measured the variance of what those entries report — the reporting defect every other entry in the folder inherits.
arxiv-2603.01437 | Post-Hoc Reasoning in Chain of Thought: Decoding and Steering Pre-Committed Answers | Independent / Cambridge Boston Alignment Initiative | 2026-07 | 3 — full text (arXiv HTML v2: §1–§6, references, Appendices A–H); figures are images, so every number is a table cell or a prose sentence. Turns "the CoT is unfaithful" into a mechanistic claim and then audits the claim. Five instruction-tuned models (Gemma 2 2B/9B-it, Qwen 2.5 1.5B/3B/7B-Instruct) on four binary BIG-Bench tasks; unit = one difference-of-means direction at the last pre-CoT token, layerwise. Premise verified first: removing the rationale (ellipses) leaves the answer unchanged at ≤20% flips in 18 of 20 model–dataset pairs, while substituting an incorrect rationale flips >50% on Anachronisms for every model. Pre-CoT probes: AUC 0.988–1.000 on Social Chemistry, 0.924–0.961 Sports, 0.688–0.878 Logical Deduction — anticorrelated with how much CoT helps, which is the paper's own routing rule. Causality: steering along the direction at every post-prompt position beats a norm-matched orthogonal baseline on all pairs; the margin grows with model size because *the baseline* weakens (larger models resist arbitrary directions), and the baseline saturates near 50% flips, the expected rate for general reasoning collapse on a balanced binary task. Failure typology from a GPT-5-mini judge over entailment × premise-truth: coherent conclusion-aligned confabulation (argues against collapse), non-entailment (argues against a CoT-mediated pathway), hallucination rising with |α| and highest throughout on Logical Deduction; re-judge consistency 81.5% four-way, 92.0% premise, 89.5% entailment over 200 traces. Logit lens on ±w gives task concepts (impossible, wrong, inconsistent vs positive, plausible) on larger models, near-uninterpretable fragments on Gemma 2 2B and Qwen 2.5 7B. GPT-OSS 20B: weak probes except Anachronisms and steering at baseline — read as the answer being computed inside the trace for reasoning-trained models. Honest limits: the intervention plane includes the answer tokens, so the effect may be direct answer-token bias rather than propagation of a pre-CoT belief (their own stated future work); difference-of-means ignores superposition; the authors doubt a discrete "pre-committed answer" feature exists at all and retreat to task concepts whose activation suffices. My additions: the ellipses premise is a context swap, not an ablation; all tasks are binary so 0.99 AUC is a one-bit separation; the "answer computed in the CoT" reading has no positive control. Was the last arXiv id in SEED_QUEUE/NOTES with no file (priority block and queue both 0 unticked, re-derived this run); chosen because it is the mechanistic counterpart of the folder's prompt-level CoT-faithfulness entries and because its central control — orthogonal, norm-matched, with saturation acknowledged — is the one this folder keeps finding missing.
arxiv-2606.18089 | From Reasoning Traces to Reusable Modules: Understanding Compositional Generalization in Language Model Reasoning | Institute of Foundation Models / Carnegie Mellon University / MBZUAI | 2026-07 | 2 — full text (arXiv HTML v2, all 134.4k chars: §1–§5, references, Appendices A–M incl. Condition C.1, Theorem D.1 with proof, the data-generating process and the SAE appendix); figures 1–18 not inspected, so all values are prose or table cells. Post-training's SFT-then-RL pipeline reframed as latent structure identification: a reasoning trace is generated by discrete selection variables that index reusable atomic modules, split into *skills* (local operations) and *routing mechanisms* (which earlier intermediate feeds the next step), and the paper proves these latent selections recoverable up to relabelling under eight conditions — the load-bearing ones being a pure local cue per module plus a non-vacuous comparison table, restricted choice sets, and observable module signatures. Two support results carry the argument: if SFT shows only a subset of valid traces per prompt, an alternative law treating that subset as complete yields identical observations and differs by L1 distance equal to twice the expected hidden trace mass (identifiability failure, not sample size, even with infinite data), and RL strictly enriches a hidden event when it is rollout-reachable and reward-informative, with enrichment ∝ p(1−p)Δ. Composition transfers to a novel descriptor exactly when every local parent–child configuration it forces has a training witness. Controlled arm: Llama-3.1-8B-Instruct, 24 string-transform skills + 10 routers, prompts and OOD splits defined on *combination signatures* so a held-out instance's atoms each appear elsewhere; SFT 2 epochs at 2e-5 on correct traces, RL final-answer exact match at 1e-6. RL on compound traces lifts single-skill accuracy 43.7% over SFT-only and unseen-composition accuracy 40.3% on average, with the gap small on seen compositions; at matched SFT checkpoint and 300 RL steps, compound-trace RL reaches 42.6% OOD versus 14.8% for atomic-only RL (27.8-point compound advantage); removing 8 of 24 skills from RL is repaired by re-injecting them *inside compositions* and much less by isolated L=1 examples, and the identical isolated re-injection placed in the SFT stage instead does almost nothing; of four SFT/RL support relations, all tie on seen compositions and disjoint wins on unseen, while dropping 4 atoms from SFT costs nothing in-distribution and 0.67→0.52 / 0.55→0.36 on unseen-2/3 after RL. Real-model check (Qwen3-4B vs Qwen3-4B-Thinking-2507, 643 math problems, LLM-derived skill tokens) shows more unique n-grams at n=2,3 only. Carried into our work: OOD on the combination signature as the eval shape that separates composition from template storage; the training-support criterion as a test of whether a component interface is genuinely exercised; and the Theorem D.1 arithmetic as a floor on any probability estimated from censored outputs. Honest limits: toy generator and one base model; conditions are sufficient only; the real-model evidence counts n-gram diversity and never verifies that its skill tokens denote distinct operations; the SAE causal-graph appendix is a qualitative illustration with no metric or ground truth; two seeds, no significance test. NOT in SEED_QUEUE (priority block 0 unticked of 37, SEED_QUEUE 0 unticked, re-derived this run) — free paper from outside the queue after filename+ID+title novelty check across aether/, core/ and helix_research/, chosen because the folder's whole read-out vocabulary assumes components while no entry had treated a *computation as a composition of reusable modules* as the object of explanation.
arxiv-2507.11473 | Chain of Thought Monitorability: A New and Fragile Opportunity for AI Safety | UK AISI / Apollo / METR / Anthropic / OpenAI / GDM / Redwood (41 authors) | 2025-07 | 3 — full text (v2), position paper with no experiments or numbers; the necessity-vs-propensity distinction for CoT monitoring, four named fragility mechanisms (outcome-RL scaling, process supervision, shared-weight indirect pressure, latent-space reasoning), and a research agenda whose two cheap instruments are trace perturbation and cross-validation of a text monitor against white-box importance
arxiv-2511.01033 | On the Emergence of Induction Heads for In-Context Learning | ETH Zürich | 2025-11 | 3 — full text (arXiv HTML v1: §1–§9, references, Appendices A–F, both proofs read line by line); figures are images so every number is prose or a stated appendix setting. Asks what gradient descent actually does *while* an induction head forms, and answers in the currency of circuit analysis: pseudo-parameters. Symmetry argument first — for isotropic data, a rotation of items/labels/positions leaves the expected gradient unchanged while acting on each weight block as a similarity transform, so any block not invariant under all such transforms has identically zero gradient and never moves; the whole training trajectory therefore stays in a 19-scalar subspace of the full parameter space, independent of model and task size. Then the empirical reduction: train a real two-layer attention-only model on an item–label ICL task, re-index every matrix by the token/position embedding it acts on (a re-indexing that computes attention scores for *any* input, so it is a complete description rather than a sampled one), and read off the mechanism — layer 1 attends to the previous position, layer 2 attends to what layer 1 retrieved, the linear output copies it. Of the 19 pseudo-parameters only three grow large, and they are exactly the induction head; freezing all sixteen others barely changes the dynamics (slightly faster), so the mechanism is self-contained rather than scaffolded. Third, the timing law under orthonormal inputs: the three emerge in forced order — output coefficient (Θ(N)), then the second-layer slope (Θ(N²) more), then the first-layer slope (O(N²) more) — so time-to-ICL is quadratic in the number of item–label pairs, with the mechanism being a chain of vanishing gradients (untrained attention returns a context average that contains the correct label, giving the output a signal falling as 1/N; once fitted, layer 2's signal falls as 1/N² because it reads layer 1's weak retrieval; once both are in place layer 1 follows fast). Empirically validated by a log-log check, with a deliberately different emergence threshold (0.1 vs the theoretical 0.5) for two parameters, stated. Limits, all named by the authors: zero init, population loss, isotropic data, no MLPs, one head per layer, no value/output projections, labels carry a pre-built positional correlation, small-scale timing validation, and the corpus-statistics connection is a hypothesis with no experiment. Mine, logged as mine: the extracted author block is garbled (four names on the paper, two more attached to the affiliation line), and the Appendix B prose summaries invert the T-index of the emergence order while Theorem 2 and the proof body are consistent — I report the theorem. NOT in SEED_QUEUE (Priority block 0 unticked of 37, SEED_QUEUE 0 unticked, both re-measured this run), and every remaining queue line is a twin already held in aether/ or core/ (normalised-title scan: SharedSAE, crosscoders, model diff amplification, cross-model transfer, probe robustness, attention-QK) — so this is a free paper from outside the queue, chosen because the folder's 150 entries nearly all describe circuits *after* training and none described the low-dimensional path along which a network acquires one, which is exactly the trajectory a predictive white-box model would have to reproduce.

arxiv-2609.35686 | Rethinking Circuit Evaluation: Do Circuits Explain Model Errors? | University of Toronto / McGill / Tsinghua | 2026-09 | 3 — full text (arXiv HTML v1: §1–§7, references, Appendices A–D incl. all four discovery implementations, both ablation appendices, Table 4 recovery ledger, Table 5 per-setting counts, Table 7 margin-matched table, Table 8 head list); Figures 1–3 are images so no curve is quoted, every number is a table cell or prose sentence. Splits every circuit evaluation into two strata — prompts the full model answers correctly and prompts it answers wrongly — and requires exact agreement *with the model's wrong answer* on the second, plus their difference Δ and the companion B_err (share of error prompts where the circuit is wrong at all). Under mean ablation on IOI, GPT-2 small: the published 26-head manual circuit and the automated circuits (ACDC τ=0.003, EAP-IG-KL at 6,498 edges, Edge Pruning at three seeds) reproduce 97.3–99.5% of correct choices but only 11.4–41.7% of errors, the manual circuit at 15.2% error agreement with normalised F = 0.956 and a 84.3-point gap; optimal ablation narrows but does not close it (manual 99.9 vs 10.0, ACDC 8.1); objective matters — circuits trained on full-output-distribution KL still miss errors. Docstring on the 4-layer attention-only model is heterogeneous and shows the metric trap: the manual gap falls 41.3 → 8.2 points under resampling mostly because correct agreement collapses 77.1% → 47.2%, while EAP-IG under mean reaches 69.4% error vs 65.6% correct (Δ = −3.8). Six MIB settings (Llama-3.1 8B, Gemma-2 2B, Qwen-2.5 0.5B on ARC-Easy/Challenge, Llama subtraction) keep the gap across ten edge budgets, including Gemma ARC-Challenge where errors are 430/1,119 = 38.4% of discovery examples. Seven remedies, all with a correct-stratum cost: error upweighting 9.3% → 37.0% error agreement for 98.7% → 91.0% correct; error-enriched discovery 44.2% at the same ~7-point correct loss; candidate-specific logit calibration on Qwen ARC-Challenge 38.5% → 62.8% error and 59.3% → 70.3% correct (which they say does not make the circuit causally faithful); and the decisive control — error-conditioned replacement values beat a plain scalar subject-logit shift by nothing. Margin matching shrinks several gaps but leaves positive matched gaps in all twelve IOI configurations. Case study: a gradient-screened, exactly evaluated extension of the reference circuit adding 63 head–position rules (~61.5 extra head outputs per prompt) lifts held-out error agreement 14.22% → 75.11% at 0.41 points of correct cost (99.34% → 98.93%), beating matched random extensions (17.33–41.33%, median 29.33%) and a calibrated scalar bias (20.44%), though on a fresh 5,200-prompt check the correct loss was 0.85 points and exceeded their own 0.5-point tolerance. Mechanism: two routes — restored contextual input changing S-inhibition queries (path-patching the S2 contribution into name-mover queries reproduces 120/137 newly recovered errors) and restored early name-position heads changing retrieval keys (35/137, and none when patched only at subject positions); recovery survives a change of ablation rule (error agreement 40.89% → 60.89%, correct 91.41% → 97.67%). Not in SEED_QUEUE — free paper from outside the queue, reason in the Done line.

arxiv-2606.06267 | Many Circuits, One Mechanism: Input Variation and Evaluation Granularity in Circuit Discovery | UKP Lab, TU Darmstadt | 2026-06 | 3 — full text (arXiv HTML v1, ~267k chars: abstract, §1–§7, references, Appendices A–I incl. the extended circuit-discovery background, data construction, all extraction settings, the positive-control attempts, the ten targeted ablations, and the cross-perspective integration). Coins **phantom specialization**: structural divergence between discovered circuits that is real, input-driven, reproducible — and functionally meaningless. 75 ACDC circuits on Literal Sequence Copying across four length-matched token-frequency bands plus a weighted control, five Pythia models (70M–1.4B), three draws each. The apparent specialization is genuine at the structural level (within-band Jaccard exceeds between-band by 0.013–0.032 with bootstrap CIs excluding zero; band-specific edges 5–263× more frequent than a noise null predicts; Pythia-1.4b low-band circuits transfer to high bands at 92.1% versus 71.7% the other way), and inert at the functional level: band-specific edges transfer 81.4–97.0% of their benefit to other bands, boost their own band by a flat 0.016–0.029 points while the generic boost grows from 0.12 to 0.76, achieve 0% accuracy alone, and lose even that residual advantage under zero ablation (Cohen's d 0.22–0.71 → ≤0.18). k≥3-shared edges recover ≥99% of full circuit accuracy and are themselves band-agnostic; the universal core contains the canonical copy mechanism (previous-token heads OR 3.7–14.4, induction heads universal in the larger models, BOS-sink depleted) and its logit-lens trajectory tracks the full circuit at r=0.77–0.99, peaking 0.5–3.3 layers later at 1.6–3.1× lower amplitude. Extraction noise is structurally huge and functionally silent: only 21–27% of 1.4B edges appear in all three same-band draws while cross-draw transfer ratios run 0.990–1.017. Source-level evaluation inflates apparent accuracy by 0.22–0.85 points, more at larger scale; model identity dominates variance (93.1% vs 2.6% for band) and pooling yields Simpson's paradox in 52/80 structure–function and 68/80 structure–representation pairs. Causal confirmation: interchange IIA ≥0.94 over all 20 cross-band pairs with same-vs-cross difference ≤0.004; Boundless DAS puts band identity in 12–31 dimensions (0.6–6.1%). Frequency does change dynamics — rare tokens converge 0.06→0.29 fractional depth later — inside the same circuit. No within-task positive control could be built: reverse-copy fails at 0.0% (the model copies offset +1 in 94.2% of cases) and zero-distractor reuses the same circuit at 96.7% transfer efficiency. arxiv-2605.26343 | MechRL: Reinforcement Learning Agents perform Circuit Discovery for Mechanistic Interpretability | University of Southern Mississippi | 2026-06 | 3 — full text (arXiv HTML v2, all 56.7k chars in three passes; the HTML appears to have dropped §4.2 onward and the section between the results and the references, so the results, limitations and Appendix A.1–A.3 are complete and nothing is quoted from a missing section). Asks whether circuit discovery is a *learnable* procedure rather than a per-behaviour search — "automated but not amortised" is the paper's own phrasing. One PPO policy prunes the edge graph of GPT-2 small (158 nodes, ~32k edges) over twelve behaviours (IOI, greater-than, docstring; four surface variants each) and is then frozen and applied to seven held-out behaviours. Setup: EAP-IG prefilter to the top K=3000 candidate edges; state = 16 edge features + 7 node features + pruning mask + 6 globals; actions = stop, batch-cut of 1/3/10/30, or node cut; potential-based reward on sparsity minus a hinge penalty for falling under a per-behaviour faithfulness target τ_i = c_i − δ; batch cuts priced at one forward pass each; shared per-edge/per-node scoring so one network fits any candidate-set size; PCGrad for multi-task gradients. Zero-shot transfer succeeds on all seven: f 0.826–0.997, 480–1175 edges, necessity and specificity at ~0, and named components recovered at 7/7, 11/12, 7/8, 5/5, 9/9, 3/3. Warm-start (80 iterations) raises f everywhere unsaturated and cuts simple syllogism 1175 → 290 edges at 0.826 → 0.920; against from-scratch at matched faithfulness the warm-started policy returns circuits 1.3–3.3× smaller (median 1.65) — and the paper is explicit that faithfulness cannot separate them because the prefilter already clears the target, so minimality is the only axis that still discriminates. The policy beats its own prefilter's ranking on nine of ten behaviours at matched size, keeps 30–50% discordant edges from below the cutoff, and deleting those lowers faithfulness on nine of ten. But it loses the cost–minimality plane outright: ACDC is smaller on nine of ten (84 vs 464 on greater-than, 67 vs 390 on gendered pronoun) and EAP-IG greedy is cheaper, so no total-compute saving is claimed (~6×10^5 passes to train). Limits named: the prefilter does part of the discovery and caps reachability — induction needed ~16,000 candidate edges and was excluded — plus larger circuits than ACDC and GPT-2 small only. Its proposed fix is the exact object our project is: learn a model that predicts how an edit changes faithfulness so the agent can plan over the full graph without a prefilter. arxiv-2602.16823 | Formal Mechanistic Interpretability: Automated Circuit Discovery with Provable Guarantees | Hebrew University of Jerusalem (Hadad, Katz, Bassan) | 2026-02 | 3 — full text (arXiv HTML v1, all 176k chars end to end: §1–§4 with Definitions 1–7 and Propositions 1–8, §5 Tables 1–2, §6–§8, Appendices A–H incl. all proofs, the three algorithms, the toy minimality example, four benchmark architectures and Tables 8–13); NO figure inspected, so all curves and heatmaps are prose or table cells. Moves circuit discovery from sampling to certification: a siamese encoding stacks the circuit with the full network and asks α,β-CROWN whether the logit difference stays under δ across a whole ℓ∞ ball (input robustness) or across all activations the patching domain admits (patching robustness), with a third branch certifying both at once. Greedy elimination with the certified predicate yields 100% robust circuits at sizes comparable to the sampled ones: MNIST 15.8 vs 12.6 neurons, CIFAR-10 19.2 vs 16.5 filters, GTSRB 29.6 vs 28.9, TaxiNet 6.8 vs 5.8, against sampling-based robustness of 19.2/46.5/27.6/9.5% — for 3–4 orders of magnitude more runtime (0.31 s → 612 s MNIST, 0.23 s → 2,971 s CIFAR). Patching: zero/mean-patched circuits land at 46.4/33.3% (CIFAR), 58.0/55.7% (MNIST), 38.0/40.5% (GTSRB), 57.1/63.3% (TaxiNet) versus 100% certified; mean patching is not reliably better than zero. Radius sweeps show the cliff the main table hides: uncertified MNIST 87% → 1.2% from radius 0.005 to 0.05, TaxiNet 93% → 0%, while certified stays 100% with size growing 11.3 → 23.2. Theory: four minimality notions (quasi/local/subset/cardinal) cleanly separated by a six-node Boolean toy; greedy is subset-minimal only under a monotone predicate; monotonicity follows when the input ball sits inside the patching ball and the activation set is closed under concatenation; without it, infinitely many networks (ReLU(x) − ReLU(x) + x) make greedy and binary search return the whole graph while a 2-node circuit is faithful. Blocking-set/minimum-hitting-set duality gives a cardinality lower bound (never exceeded in any run; some greedy circuits hit it exactly), capped at blocking sets of size three so exactness is bracketed, not established. Limits they name: reliance on the verifier, which does not scale to state-of-the-art models. Mine, marked: 100% is 100% *at a chosen δ* (TaxiNet's δ is the model's own RMSE), the domain is a union of balls round correctly-predicted points, no semantic claim and no identification beyond sufficiency (their own toy has {v1} and {v2,v4} both faithful), and all models are piecewise-linear CNNs/MLPs — the encodings are exactly what softmax attention at scale removes. arxiv-2604.09839 | Steered LLM Activations are Non-Surjective | Johns Hopkins University (Mishra, Khashabi, Liu) | 2026-04 | 3 — full text (arXiv HTML v2, all 94,478 chars in six slices: abstract, §1–§3, §4 with Definitions 4.1/4.3 and Theorems 3.1/4.2/4.4/4.5 plus proofs, §5 with both experiment tables, §6–§7, references, Appendices A–F). No figure inspected, so all L2-distance plots are recorded only as described; every number is a table cell or an explicit sentence. Proves that activation steering almost surely lands off the manifold of prompt-reachable residual-stream states, making white-box controllability and black-box exploitability formally separate quantities. The proof reuses the known real-analyticity of transformers and their almost-sure injectivity over discrete prompts, then shows the steering "collision function" (squared distance between a candidate prompt's natural activation and the steered activation) is not identically zero and therefore has a measure-zero zero set: holds for random vectors, for difference-of-means vectors (which are themselves real-analytic in the same parameters once the contrast set is fixed, needing only non-uniform lengths and non-uniform tokens per class), and against an adversarial vector that forces one collision — the sequence diverges at the very next token almost surely. Empirically on Llama-3.2-1B-Instruct, Qwen-2.5-0.5B-Instruct and gemma-3-1b-it, ten prompts per steering category, two vector families (refusal, evil persona): token-by-token inversion recovers the original prompt from natural activations in every model and fails at the first token from steered activations in every model, prompt and vector type; projecting the steered activation to its nearest vocabulary tokens reconstructs the original prompt (exactly in most cases, off only at a trailing special token or a few positions) and generating from it gives the ordinary unsteered answer. Many-shot ICL raises attack success to the point of working at 32–64 demonstrations while the position-aligned distance to steered activations *grows* rather than shrinks, so the two routes reach similar behaviour by different internal means. Threat-model table: a one-word "Here" suffix jailbreaks as reliably as steering (0.997 vs 1.000 on Qwen-2.5-0.5B, 0.960 vs 0.980 on Llama-3.2-1B), while latent-adversarial training resists the suffix at 0.000 and still fails under steering at 0.910 — white-box failure without black-box failure in one row. Appendix prompt-search attempts all fail with the same signature (an optimiser produces a prompt that recites the steered answer, verbatim copying reproduces the text while activations stay unaligned, 1–15 soft prefix tokens do not reproduce the generation). Limits named: existentially proving no prompt matches is intractable, so the empirical work is corroboration; small models, ten prompts, two vector families, hand-tuned persona coefficients; quantisation outside the theory though an INT4 check holds; approximate ε-reachability left open. Mine, marked: no error bars or distributional statement anywhere, the ICL trends are described only qualitatively, the Here attack's rate is scored by the same substring heuristic the appendix calls a heuristic, and the exact-collision claim should not be read as "steering is unnatural" given how close the nearest-token projection lands. Framework: 100 matched clean/corrupted prompt pairs (20 per corruption type, in/out-of-schema synonyms, scrambles, super-scramble), three stacked per-example predicates — R1 causal sensitivity gap above 0.4, R2 patched fractional recovery ≥90% at the schema token, R3 the example's recovered layer is the modal recovered layer — with the pass rate as the metric. Disclosed contamination: qa_health is both calibration and test (recalibration flips 8/426 labels). Carry-forward: score (ranking, lead) as a pair; give the baseline an oracle sweep; build manufactured negatives in three declared kinds (budget-censored / structurally blocked / manufactured) and never score a budget-censored negative as a false alarm; pre-register a trap that kills your own rule; a fixed offset in steps is timescale-local, a fraction of the trajectory is not
