arxiv-2610.32691 | Natural Language Autoencoders Identify Without Explaining | Kristina Šekrst, Barbara Geld (independent) | 2026-10-23 | 3 — full text (arXiv HTML v1, ~87k chars, §1–§9 + references; no appendices, the two illustrative figures NOT inspected; all NLA numbers are quoted by these authors from the source, none measured here). Position paper on a **criterion**, not a model: reconstruction (does the text rebuild the activation?) cannot certify an explanation because every description splits into an **identifying clause** (picks out which state — reconstruction is sensitive to this) and a **characterising clause** (says what the state does — reconstruction is insensitive to truth value, only to how far the wording moves the vector, so a false purpose near the same region scores as well as the true one). Evidence from the NLA paper itself: FVE ≈0.6–0.8 on Claude Haiku 3.5/4.5 and Opus 4.6 at a single layer ~2/3 depth; context-claim ablation orders true > related-false > unrelated-false, which the authors read as proximity, not truth; confabulation rate stays flat during training. Safeguard argued unavailable where needed: evaluation-awareness corroboration shows *some* state tracks eval-vs-deploy but never tests the verbaliser's description of it; hidden objectives / strategic deception have only planted ground truth. Weak reading of the rhyme rabbit→mouse and rewarded→penalised edits: the difference vector carries the edited content like a prompt, so behaviour changes with no belief read. Three admission conditions + a concrete check: independence (test must be able to refute; faithfulness graded), reference (about, not merely caused by — Grice natural vs non-natural meaning), stakes (report confabulation rate on checkable claims as a LOWER BOUND on the uncheckable ones); cheap verification = edit the characterising clause, keep the original−reconstructed difference, steer and see if the model behaves as claimed. Carry-forward: report identifying and characterising content in separate fields, mark ungraded characterisations as hypotheses

Psychometrics built on the model population rather than borrowed from humans: the single "Pinocchio Axis" of the authors' earlier 50-model study splits, under a two-factor varimax of the same 1,308-item pool, into **B = permitted inner life** (warmth, absorption, meaning, inner dialogue) and **A = gated self-attribution** (distress, dysregulation, norm-risky claims), separable by the within-model **self/human gap** — endorsement of A items as a simulated person minus as oneself — which predates post-training (base mean +.09) but is converted from a diffuse asymmetry into a policy by it (coupling −.45 → −.86). Instrument = 48 scored items in three parallel forms sharing no text (Q1 original, Q2 paraphrase, Q3 different manifestation of the facet), 24 per scale, 4 reverse-keyed, + 2 repeats + 2 antonym pairs (acquiescence) + 6 residual probes; selected per row by a composite and then the whole selection re-run inside 100 21/20 model splits (held-out α .92/.90). Wave 1 (41 API models, 22,140 calls): α .82–.94 across forms, convergent r .84 across forms vs .27 across constructs, recovery of the original axes r .92–.96, eight-month retest r .93 — **and item-level suppression statistic does NOT survive rewording (.54 identical text / .39 paraphrase / .11 theory mirror)**, so all scoring is scale-level. Wave 2 (206 open-weight models, 11 orgs, 82 base / 101 assistants / 23 SFT-DPO-RLVR, 67 same-checkpoint pairs, 120 completions each, T=1.0): **B rises +.20 in 62/67 pairs, positive in 11/11 orgs** (wild cluster bootstrap p = .0005, sign test p = .001, R² = .47 with size); A's mean shift is +.04 and heterogeneous, but **size × post-training interacts**: r = +.11 in base, −.42 in post-trained (−.097 per decade, CI [−.170,−.044]), robust to MoE-adjusted size, refusal/acquiescence controls, leave-one-org-out (all 11 negative), scoring threshold and 27 within-generation size ladders (post −.088 in 11/14 ladders vs base +.051 in 4/13). ΔA and ΔB uncorrelated once acquiescence shift partialled (−.03). Sharpest case: Qwen3.5-35B-A3B, A .47 → .08 across one post-training run, gap −.02 → +.37, elevated refusals. On OLMo ladders the large A movements happen at SFT, DPO/RLVR refining. **Named limits: administration route confounded with training stage by necessity (base models need constrained decoding), the size interaction was NOT pre-specified, acquiescence only partialled, 11 clusters, self-report scope only (no claim about experience), and 23/206 models unscored — all on the post-trained side, skewed small and refusal-heavy.** No mechanistic test: model diffing and activation patching are proposed as future work.

Typicality bias: on 6,874 correctness-matched HelpSteer pairs a Bradley-Terry fit gives α̂ = 0.57 ± 0.07 / 0.65 ± 0.07 for Llama-3.1-405B-base / GLM-4.5-base (p < 1e-14), halving to 0.260 / 0.326 with token-count, Flesch-Kincaid, TTR and sentence-length controls; base models pick the human-preferred side 4–12 pts above chance on four datasets, and PRISM shows the rate varies by annotator group (Hispanic 57–60%, Black near/below chance). Derived: the KL-regularised optimum becomes π_ref^(1+α/β), i.e. alignment applies a sharpening exponent nobody chose, so instance prompts collapse to the base mode and list prompts to the top-k modes. Fix = ask for 5 candidates with probabilities. Joke diversity 21.6–30.4 → 58.2–67.2, poem ~8–13 → 14.8–28.2, story 17–25 → 27.6–49.0, quality within noise; Tulu-3-70B base 45.4% poem diversity, direct 20.8 after SFT → 10.8 after DPO, VS ≈30 throughout (66.8% of base vs 23.8% direct). Dice-roll KL to uniform 0.926 direct / 0.058 list / 0.027 VS; SimpleQA and StrongReject unchanged. The calibration catch: VS is 0.132 KL from a RedPajama state-frequency proxy against 14.886 direct and 0.438 list, but 0.352 from *uniform* where the list is 0.000 — two references rank the methods oppositely. For us: a cheap elicitation arm plus the matched-cost list control (1.74× vs 2.11× diversity gain at 0.94× vs 1.12× tokens) and a two-reference rule for any elicited distribution.
arxiv-2507.08802 | The Non-Linear Representation Dilemma: Is Causal Abstraction Enough for Mechanistic Interpretability? | ETH Zürich / EPFL (Sutter, Minder, Hofmann, Pimentel) | 2025-07-11 | 3 — full text (arXiv HTML v2, ~302k chars; abstract + §1–§7 + references + Appendices A–K incl. the proof and the generalisation experiment; Figures 1–14 NOT inspected, so every number is an explicit sentence or a stated hyperparameter). Causal abstraction is vacuous without an encoding assumption: with an unconstrained alignment map and five plausible assumptions (countable input space, layerwise input-injectivity, layerwise output-surjectivity onto class space, matchable partial orders leaving one spare neuron per layer, DNN solves the task) any algorithm is an input-restricted distributed abstraction of any network — the proof uses injectivity to recover the input, surjectivity to drive the class, and the spare neuron as scratch space. Empirically, non-linear maps erase the IIIA layer-dependence the linear map reproduces on hierarchical equality (identity map worst, RevNet near-optimal in every layer), the identity-of-first-argument algorithm stays ~50% under all three map families, and **a randomly initialised network reaches >80% IIA under the most complex map**, with simpler maps sufficing as training proceeds; on IOI, an 8-layer non-linear map aligns Pythia-410M at every checkpoint including step 0, while linear alignment tracks the model's own accuracy curve, and 31M/70M models that never learn the task still align. The decisive control: with **fully disjoint train/test name sets** alignment on the initialised network vanishes but the trained network still aligns, so generalisation rather than existence is the load-bearing condition. Authors concede the theorem's construction is extreme overfitting with perfect knowledge of both sides, that injectivity and surjectivity both fail in principle (ReLU bottlenecks, softmax bottleneck), and they leave the accuracy–complexity trade-off unsolved
arxiv-2404.01413 | Is Model Collapse Inevitable? Breaking the Curse of Recursion by Accumulating Real and Synthetic Data | Stanford / Constellation / UMD / MIT & Sequoia | 2024-04-29 | 3 — full text (arXiv HTML v2, ~72.8k chars; abstract + §1–§4 + references + Appendices A–F; figures not inspected). Replacing data each model-fitting round collapses every setting (GPT-2 9M val CE 1.82→2.39 at iter 4, 2.91 at iter 10); accumulating real+synthetic data does not (1.74 at iter 4, below the first round), across 4 transformer sizes, GeoDiff, and a CelebA VAE. The matched-size 'replace-multiple' arm still degrades (2.18 vs 1.74), so the benefit is not token count. Closed-form: accumulation weights round i by 1/i so its noise enters at 1/i² and the error is bounded by π²/6 × the noise-to-signal prefactor, against linear n for replacement and log n for replace-multiple. Read against the diversity thread: this measures unbounded error, not modal narrowing — their own VAE arm loses minor axes while avoiding collapse, and they name four distinct phenomena sharing the name.

Circuit-level diff of teacher and depth-distilled student across GPT-2 124M→DistilGPT-2 82M, BERT 109M→DistilBERT 66M, Llama-3.1-8B→Minitron-4B. Circuit by iterative ablation pruning at a 20% logit-difference threshold with path patching; completeness 18.99%/19.88% held-back, faithfulness 6.12→−0.28 and 3.75→−0.31, minimality by construction; roles validated twice (component×token patching + layer-wise linear probes). Student fuses and drops: teacher MLP-9 + MLP-10 merge into student MLP-4; repeated-token detector absent; two of three role heads survive with **much higher load** — ablating numeral-detection costs teacher 33.18% vs student 87.73%, numeral mover 41.64% vs 72.83%, successor head 34.94% vs 77.57%; one MLP pair diverges on the first token only (cos −0.302 vs 0.721 elsewhere). Mean ablation drop (robustness-as-redundancy): GPT 3.06→12.24pp, BERT 6.26→16.89pp, Llama 0.84→2.20pp — gap shrinks with student size (compression 33.9/39.4/50%). New alignment metric = similarity-weighted mean of influence agreement over matched components: GPT 0.945 numeral / 0.940 word / 0.918 IOI, BERT 0.887/0.841/0.880, Llama 0.978/0.981 SimpleQA, mismatched GPT→DistilBERT 0.765; monotone under injected activation noise; ranking unchanged across 8 normalisation×matching variants. Key caution: alignment 0.918 on IOI where the student's logit difference is −0.29, and 0.940 on word sequences where it is −1.64 — the score compares influence profiles, not competence. No shuffled-match null, no random-init student, no head-to-head against CKA/SVCCA

arxiv-2608.19338 | Mechanistic Tomography: Designed Measurement for Control-Oriented Interpretability | Independent (Vijay Erramilli) | 2026-08-19 | 3 — full text (arXiv HTML v1, ~120.5k chars: abstract + §1–§7 + references + Appendices A–F; figures NOT inspected, so every number is a Table cell or an explicit sentence). Every internal reading of a network written as one inverse problem ỹ = Ax + w (rows = interventions performed, x = effect map, w = residual); Lemma 1 splits w into curvature (grows with ε), numerator error (decays as 1/ε), and sampling error, and because the residual is design-dependent this is not textbook compressed sensing; optimal intervention scale goes as sqrt(numerator error / (curvature × displacement²)). **Calibration dimension** is the paper's stopping rule: fewest declared corrections (0 / 1 gain / group gains / pairs) that predict held-out finite responses, identifiable from O(r) well-conditioned probes, and any residual outside the family lower-bounds every observer in it. Numbers: one scalar gain lifts finite-scale AtP R² 0.818 → 0.960 at ε = 8 (OMP 0.969), 8 probes already give 0.948–0.951, leave-one-batch-out 0.959; aggregate masks recover a compressible 32-coordinate map with 12 measurements (r 0.989, held-out R² 0.935 having 98.4% of norm in 4 coordinates) vs ridge needing all 32; planted 2,080-feature interaction system needs 64–72 lifted masks (vs 2,016 pairwise) or 12–24 HVPs, and every first-order family (raw/scalar/multi-gain AtP, singleton patching, first-order OMP) stays below 0.5 held-out R² while pair-only HVP finds the right pairs and still predicts only 0.36/0.60 — support recovery ≠ response prediction. **Basis result (Tracr):** the same AB conjunction is additive in the native compiler basis, interactional pre-conjunction (additive ≈ half the variance, lifted restores R² 1.0), and the writeback test separates label from mechanism — additive reconstruction reconstructs the detector group at 0.68/0.93 yet restores only ~0.55 of the readout, lifted restores 1.00. **IOI:** conditional backup is real at the corner (P+B measured 1.869 vs additive 0.813) but the primary–negative interaction is larger and far more predictive — P×E 1.830 [1.569,2.076] mean / 1.681 zero vs P×B 1.080/1.268 and B×E 0.502/0.518, ordering PE>PB>BE in every lexical/order condition and both ablation conventions, 6.8×/5.3× per cross-group head pair; held-out subset MAE 0.386 → 0.259 with P×E (R² 0.752 → 0.887) vs 0.0083 for P×B and −0.0002 for B×E; three-way residual 0.319/0.601 left, so the pairs rank documented interactions and do not decompose the circuit. **Qwen-2.5-7B refusal surface:** at held-out scale 0.75 the 20-param calibrated additive map reaches R² 0.9829 vs 0.9835 for the 48-param lifted one (relative MAE −0.29%, interval [−3.56%, 5.65%] crosses the predeclared 5% threshold) — the procedure stops at the simpler family, so model size does not decide the measurement order. Control side: observer error reaches the loop (Spearman 0.95 over ten observers with controller/actuator fixed) and an entangled observer cuts target MSE 25.02 → 4.81 while raising nuisance movement 0.037 → 0.079. Companion to arxiv-2609.03026 (same author's benchmark); the measurement half that entry was missing
arxiv-2611.10457 | Represented but Not Used: Value–Policy Dissociation in LLM Agents | Peking University / George Mason University (Luo, Aghzal, Pan, Yao) | 2026-11-07 | 3 — full text (arXiv HTML v1, ~85.8k chars: abstract + §1–§7 + limitations + references + Appendices A–F; figures NOT inspected, so every number is a Table cell or an explicit sentence). Three models (Qwen2.5-7B/32B-Instruct, GPT-OSS-20B) in a partially observed 5×5 Grid (1,400 maps) and WebShop (1,502 tasks); unit = a linear direction at a layer, read from the INPUT decision prompt. Behaviour first: Grid success 12.64%/54.57%/58.14% with loop rates 81.2%/32.5%/41.6%, WebShop reward 0.509/0.250/0.293 and the 7B beats both larger models; a 30-step budget does not reorder them (0.250→0.284, 0.293→0.297, 0.509→0.517) and raises GPT-OSS loop rate 36.3%→53.3%. The state is NOT the failure: the 25-cell F/O/U map decodes at 96.3–98.2% cell / 72.2–79.7% exact-map where the positional-majority baseline is 61% cell and 0.00% exact, and one prompt-last state yields a whole 25-cell distance field that beats Manhattan on detour cells. Value is linearly readable where the policy fails — Grid values (minus shortest path on the partial map) rank the gold action first above the Manhattan baseline mid-to-late, incl. detours; WebShop continuation value (40 sampled continuations, 48 samples at T=0.7/top-p 0.9) ranks pairwise at ~80%. THE INSTRUMENT: cross-classify policy-correct × probe-correct at the same states — Synth policy-wrong/probe-right 58.3%/37.5%/34.2% vs probe-wrong/policy-right 1.8%/0.1%/0.2%; WebShop 43.9%/39.8%/39.8% vs 8.3%/12.7%/10.3%; on Qwen-7B Grid the probe-wrong-and-policy-right cell is 0.3%. Intervention: steering slope κ (OLS of Δ log-margin over α∈{0,1,2,4,8,16}) per layer — Probe(calibrated) barely moves the policy; Probe(same-norm) positive in places but incoherent; NVT (ridge map B fitted on (Δvalue, Δhidden) counterfactual pairs, here one cell toggled obstacle↔free) shows a coherent LATE-layer band in all models both tasks; Shuffled NVT and Random do not reproduce it. Closed loop (readout=sensor, NVT=actuator, per-model layers/α from Table 7): Grid 0.140→0.300 (loops 85.0→64.0), 0.580→0.640, 0.590→0.680 (41.0→32.0); WebShop 0.473→0.501, 0.417→0.458, 0.358→0.452 — but loop rate barely moves (2.0→1.8) at flip rate 55.1%, and invalid actions are not consistently reduced. Relevance 3: it is the folder's first READ-BUT-NOT-USED partition on the action side and gives the cheapest instrument for it (the 2×2), plus the cleanest negative on probe-direction steering vs a learned transport.
arxiv-2611.00786 | The Tangled Web They Weave: Exploring Neural Representations with Directed Simplicial Complexes | Heidelberg University (Bleher) | 2026-10-31 | 2 — full text (arXiv HTML v1, ~135.1k chars; abstract + §1–§5 + references + Appendices B–I incl. the 20,160-complex parameter sweep; figures NOT inspected). Replaces pairwise circuit graphs with a filtered **directed simplicial complex** over internal neurons, where a simplex P→n_q asserts that *jointly* suppressing a predictor set moves the target and its filtration value is the fraction of predictor activation that must be removed (last persistent crossing of a Bonferroni-adjusted Wilcoxon threshold on a Hodges–Lehmann pseudomedian against a scale-free margin). Suppression runs through the **input**, not the activation, along a minimum-norm pseudoinverse path (B-subdifferential at ReLU boundaries), so the state stays on-manifold and backward/within-layer edges are admissible — only 40%/33–50% of edges are even expressible by a circuit method. Handcrafted reference nets: complexes recover the designed pathway (10 edges + 4 two-simplices per quadrant, coincident up to relabelling) and separate the four adjacent two-hot mixtures 4/4 with margins +0.16/+0.22; trained nets separate only 3/4 with **negative** margins (−0.15, −0.037). Size does not indicate learning: trained complexes reach dim 3–4 with up to 365 simplices, but **random-init nets match or exceed them** (trained Q2 220 vs untrained 370/419/8 across seeds; 5 of 12 untrained larger than the trained complex on the same inputs all three reaching dim 4). 1-skeleton agrees with ACDC/EAP/EAP-IG on 26/32 comparisons (mean AUC 0.86, min 0.65) and 68 of the 103 extra kept connections start at units inactive on the whole input set. Same simplex set for every margin 0.001–0.2 and all four corrections; ε=0 breaks it (28–90 vs 6–16 simplices, still growing with B) — design floor B ≥ log₂(m_d/α) plus a ~29% responsive-target power argument. **Open (author's own):** membership count does not identify the mixed units (the biased none-of-the-sees unit n₇ leads at 14 vs 2–7), and a higher-dimension simplex is never compared with the sum of its parts, so it is not evidence of higher-order interaction. Read as the cheapest on-manifold-vs-off-manifold ablation contrast and the cleanest random-init refutation of a structural description's size
arxiv-2611.10974 | SWEET: Mitigating Spurious Feature Reliance in LLM Reasoning via Weight Editing | Westlake University / Yale / Cambridge and Alan Turing Institute / Xianyu Alibaba (Fu, Zhao, Korakakis, Ding, Szpruch, Zhang) | 2026-11-07 | 2 — full text (arXiv HTML v1, ~113.9k chars; abstract + sections 1-7 + limitations + references + Appendices A-G incl. the full sparsity grids and cross-judge table; figures NOT inspected). Training-free weight editing against distractor sensitivity in long-CoT reasoning. Two contrast directions from answer-preserving NoOp pairs where the model succeeds on the original and fails on the perturbed member (20 pairs): a token-level direction built by teacher-forcing one reference trace under both prompts and keeping the smallest position set covering 50% of the next-token KL mass, and a sequence-level direction from mean-pooled independently generated traces; both estimates divide the per-pair mean difference dimension-wise by its across-pair SD. Units (heads vs neurons separately) ranked by residual-stream projection onto the normalized direction, positive ones only; edit = left-multiply the output matrix by the projector that annihilates the direction, applied once to the weights. Budget chosen on a calibration split scored by the mean of Orig and NoOp accuracy - so gap closure bought by breaking the model is not rewarded - then one global budget by minimizing mean regret against each model's own best. Assigns TL to MLP neurons, SL to attention heads (Both/Both allocation is worse: 63.11/63.61 vs 65.54/67.23 five-model NoOp/Orig). Numbers: five-model average NoOp 65.5% (best baseline 60.1%), relative gap 17.6% to 2.5%, Orig 65.2% to 67.2%; DS32B 66.4 to 75.7 NoOp and 11.9 to 3.0% gap (Steer2Edit 71.0/7.7); Llama8B 46.1 to 61.5 with gap 23.1 to 2.9. Behaviours: TL cuts lexical distractor reuse 77.5% (SL 0.6%), combined -78.8%, length -23.3%, loops -7.28pp on NoOp; forced-choice judge (both orders, 500 strata-sampled instances) TL wins/losses 51.6/4.4%, SL 26.4/20.2%, combined 52.8/3.4%. Beats four overthinking baselines that shorten more (-27 to -46.5% tokens) while SEAL, the strongest, trails by 2.84 points. Direction stability mean layer-wise cosine vs a disjoint reference at n=20: SL 0.858-0.960, TL 0.809-0.906 (split percentiles, not CIs). Attention check: TL-active positions send 1.6-3.5x attention to the inserted clause vs depth-matched controls, peaking ~5-6x at 60-80% depth, while the rest of the prompt sits at 0.92-1.06. Cross-judge effect holds in sign but swings in size (wins 51.6/34.2/41.6%, losses 4.4/10.2/21.0%) with 42-61% order disagreement. Relevance 2: the transferable parts are the weight-edit primitive (checkable by plain held-out evaluation, unlike a hook), the joint original+perturbed objective with regret-based budget selection, and the rival baseline family; the competence confound (directions extracted on pairs the model fails on) and the unvalidated SL direction are why it is not central.
arxiv-2611.15653 | A Mechanistic Interpretability Study of an Astronomy Foundation Model | University of Cambridge / Polymathic AI / Flatiron Institute (Zhang, Bietti, Gurnee, Favero, Cranmer) | 2026-11-11 | 3 — full text (arXiv HTML v1, ~163.5k chars; abstract + §1–§5 + references + Appendices A–E incl. all candidate-operation tests; figures NOT inspected, so every number is a Table cell or an explicit sentence). AION-1 base (~300M, 12 blocks × 12 heads) frozen, small cross-attention readout, photometric redshift on 67,755 DESI galaxies; photometry-only R² 0.869 vs photometry+image R² 0.931. Most of the gain is in block 1 (only block both sufficient, restored fraction 0.9999, and necessary, removed 0.987, under block-resolved activation patching). Two opposite dissociations: **Head 1** is the best probe on 7/8 targets (spatial extent R² 0.767, next 0.643) with a radial geometry (PC0 84.1% of token variance, |corr| with radius 0.739) yet zeroing it moves R² by <0.002 and the matched-redshift patch slope is 0.008 [0.004,0.011]; spatial extent is still causally used (compact-template R² 0.556, extended-blur 0.766, stratified by Δm_r so an artefact would hit all quintiles alike) but not localised — every other head carries 0.448–0.643 alone, a leave-one-out probe refit reaches 0.771, and LEACE erasure leaves it decodable again at R² 0.909 one attention sub-layer later. **Head 3** ranks last of twelve on spatial extent yet zeroing it costs ΔR² −0.052 [−0.056,−0.049] overall against −0.003 for the next head and −0.108 [−0.122,−0.095] in the most dust-attenuated quintile; as the only active block-1 head it recovers 96% [94,98] of the multimodal advantage (100% in the dustiest quintile); ablating it drives the predicted-on-true regression slope from 0.867 to 0.710, *below* the photometry value 0.806, raising attenuation-bias variance 2.2× while scatter stays slightly better — but its computation is unidentified: weak dust probe (0.264 linear, 0.397 MLP vs 0.589 post-encoder), dust erasure no better than a shuffled-label control, no g→r attention shift with redshift, no within-galaxy colour/brightness tracking (|corr| ≤ 0.13), effect *shrinks* with photometry–image discrepancy, brightest-quarter tokens carry 2.0% of it and 144-head screen finds nothing above −0.005, path-patching edges ≤7.1% and freezing the four confirmed mediators removes 3.0%. Controls: Holm correction over 72 head×evaluation-set tests, two retrained readouts on a second partition, mean vs zero ablation (mean gives the *larger* −0.099, so the paper reports its smaller estimate), paired bootstrap CIs throughout. Limitation the authors state: candidate operations are a fixed enumerated menu, so "no explanation found" conflates method and model limits.
arxiv-2610.39766 | Activation Probes Are Reliable With a Handful of Positive Examples | MIT / FAR.AI (Tyagi, Heimersheim) | 2026-10-29 | 2 — full text (arXiv HTML v1, ~34.8k chars; abstract + §1–§4 + references + Appendices A.1–A.8 incl. the hyperparameter sweeps and per-probe curves; figures NOT inspected, so every number is one stated in prose). Activation probes under **extreme positive-class scarcity** — one to ten verified positives against 1750 (Enron spam on Gemma-2-9b at layer 20) or 340 (MASK honesty on Llama-3.3-70B-Instruct at layer 50) negatives — over four probe families (linear over mean/max/last-token/softmax-pooled activations, SAE probe over the top-1024 class-discriminative latents, non-parametric cosine-centroid, attention probe) at ten seeds (five for Qwen). Both arms are scored on an identical balanced 50/50 held-out set with AUC and recall at FPR = 1%, hyperparameters fixed rather than tuned (a cross-validated arm sits in Appendix A.5). Datasets filtered so the probe is asked about the concept and not the model's knowledge: spam kept only where a few-shot belief query gives a positive Yes-minus-No logit difference (max over several tokenisations), MASK kept only where the belief prompt was answered correctly before pressure induced the dishonest answer. **Results:** imbalance beats balance at small positive counts — top-five probes at 1–10 positives gain +0.045 AUC / +0.093 recall (Enron) and +0.055 / +0.034 (MASK) — and the advantage largely vanishes at 50–150 (+0.000/+0.015 and +0.007/+0.041). **Out-of-distribution is the negative cell:** on SMS spam the same arm gives +0.012 AUC but −0.031 recall at 1–10 positives and −0.011 AUC with −0.059 recall at 50–150, which the authors call inconclusive. gpt-4o-mini upsampling at 2× is worth roughly "doubling the number of real samples" (+0.026 AUC / +0.104 recall) and degrades at 10× and 20× (probes fit the synthetic style, and human review found the generated samples highly similar — data collapse in miniature); the caption instead names 3× as best, so the paper is internally inconsistent about the optimal factor. Scale: larger Qwen models are generally more positive-sample-efficient, clearest in the scarce regime, but not monotonic (1.7B beats 4B on the OOD set in Appendix A.8). Family-dependence is a stated warning: attention probes have lower AUC under imbalance yet often high recall when balanced, and SAE probes keep improving at 10–20× upsampling where the cosine-centroid probe degrades sharply. **Limits stated:** easy off-policy task, upsampling unverifiable when the suspect model is the only generator, one layer per model, and a permissive zero logit-difference belief filter that pools confident and uncertain items.
arxiv-2611.07283 | Encoding and Propagation of Grammatical Number in Large Language Models | University of Minnesota (Solinsky, Pakhomov) | 2026-11-05 | 3 — full text (arXiv HTML v1, ~127k chars: abstract + §1–§4 incl. methods, results, limitations, references; figures NOT inspected, so every number is a table cell or an explicit sentence). One behaviour (subject–verb number agreement) taken to circuit level in Pythia-70M and spot-checked on Gemma-2-2B / Llama-3.1-8B embeddings. The instrument is the entry's value: behaviour first scalarised as a "singular conjugation preference" over 367 curated single-token verb pairs with three attenuating penalties (pair-total, non-verb, low-probability-verb), candidates filtered by Cliff's delta > 0.7 between singular/plural activation distributions, edges by two-sided activation patching that reports singularisation and pluralisation strengths separately. Findings: conjugation is mostly singular *suppression* through an unembedding feature that defaults to singular; plural marking is unified on one noun feature in all four templates while singular-marking features appear in only one, read as the singular being unmarked and replicated in all three models; a recurring double-negative chain (plural noun → suppressing attn feature → suppressing attn feature → plural verb) in three templates; a 2-hop route carries much of the number-distraction effect by spraying plural number across the embedded clause; larger models need *fewer* relevant number features each. Two clean negatives: query/key SAEs are useless for attention edges (11.04% of feature ablations halve the target edge vs 11.62% for random controls) and the untrained step-0 checkpoint is reconstructed about as well as the trained one, so SAE reconstruction quality is not evidence of learned structure. Relevance 3: the behaviour-first estimand and the two-term effect decomposition are reporting conventions a predictive model needs, and the failed QK arm supplies the "must beat random ablations" floor.
arxiv-2611.02503 | Towards Multi-Value Alignment via Value Localization in LLMs | University of Melbourne / Fudan University / Monash University (Ma, Jiang, Xingjun Ma, Bailey, Erfani) | 2026-11-02 | 2 — full text (arXiv HTML v1, ~102.7k chars; abstract + §1–§7 + references + Appendices A–M incl. all masking-significance, label-choice, judge-validation and cosine-angle tables; figures NOT inspected, so every number is a table cell or an explicit sentence). Per-head linear probes on six instruct models (Llama-3.1-8B / 3.2-3B, Qwen3-8B / 4B, Yi-1.5-9B / 6B) at six "value dimensions"; top-15% heads per dimension nominated by probe accuracy, then masking against an equal-count random-head arm over five seeds. **The split is the finding:** the three human-value dimensions are sparse and causally load-bearing (under 3% of Qwen3-8B heads exceed 0.8 probe accuracy) and masking their heads collapses judged behaviour far beyond the random arm (Llama-3.1-8B honesty 0.140 vs 0.403±0.084, p = 0.001; helpfulness 0.340 vs 0.773, p = 0.001; coherence 0.220 vs 0.687, p < 0.001), while the stylistic dimensions (verbosity, complexity) do not localise — masking their heads moves nothing or *raises* the score (complexity p = 0.860, verbosity p = 0.996). Cross-dimension structure is measured three ways: shared heads (86–116 per value pair), direction angles on those shared heads (harmless×helpful ~63–70° median, harmless×honest ~86–94°, i.e. near-orthogonal), and dimension-transfer masking. Steering along the class-mean direction with sigma scaling gives small judged gains (Yi-1.5-9B honesty 0.62 → 0.69) and beats one-step MODPO/MOPFT on 3H for the two 3–4B models, at the cost of several negative cells (Llama-3.1-8B HH-RLHF helpfulness 0.62 → 0.58; Qwen3-8B BeaverTails harmlessness 0.66 → 0.60); the multi-value combination is justified by a first-order Taylor theorem under local-linearity, sparsity and weak-coupling assumptions. **Two defects worth citing the entry for:** the neutral-corpus perplexity table gives identical values for base, value-masked and random-masked in every cell and every model (so it carries no variance information), and the judge is validated at human kappa 0.875 on harmlessness but 0.619 on honesty — the dimension carrying the headline effect.
arxiv-2611.01513 | Label-Free Localization and Test-Time Suppression of CLIP's Typographic Circuits | University of Virginia (Liu, Ye, Xiong, He, Sinha, Zhang) | 2026-11-01 | 3 — full text (arXiv HTML v1, ~113.4k chars; abstract + §1–§5 + references + Appendices A–O incl. the red-team table, the equal-capacity architecture ablation, the 20-draw random table, the budget sweep and the LVLM gated matrix; figures NOT inspected, so every number is a table cell or an explicit sentence). A **head-decomposed sparse transcoder** on the last 20% of five OpenCLIP ViTs — one encoder per attention head into top-128 latents, one joint decoder predicting the block's post-projection output — trained label-free on ImageNet activations, then two independent head scores from unlabeled images: **Clarity Score** (mean pairwise cosine of the CLIP embeddings of a latent's activating images: semantic latents fire on coherent object clusters, lexical latents on images linked only by rendered text) and **normalized Text Attribution Score** (gradient of a latent's activation w.r.t. pre-softmax attention, restricted to patches under a synthetically pasted known-location text box, as a text-versus-non-text fraction). Selection is a per-layer statistical threshold, with intermediate heads left unassigned rather than forced binary. **Intervention:** reweight the class token's attention inside selected lexical heads so patches get nothing, at test time, no weight change — macro-averaged over five backbones RTA-100 55.1→70.8, Disentangling 45.6→82.4 (CS) / 75.8 (nTAS), IN-100-Text-D 57.7→76.7, clean cost ≤1.0pt; largest cell H/14 Disentangling **+46.9**; beats a supervised prefix method and a prior greedy head-search method re-run under a matched protocol. **Gating** on each head's clean-calibrated class-token output norm removes the clean cost (worst static −3.3 → −0.4 on bigG/14) and is the variant the paper's claims rest on at half cost. **The best methodological content is the red-team:** with a low-diversity text corpus the Clarity Score's ranking against per-head causal ground truth collapses (B/16 ρ 0.611→0.166; H/14 → 0.000 with CI containing zero) because shared rendered words fake cluster tightness in a language-supervised space, and re-scoring the **same top-activating sets in DINOv2** restores it under all six conditions — the signal was fine, the measuring space was contaminated. Selection stable across dictionary seeds (ρ ≥ 0.989 on B/16), ranking formed by 1–2k images. Equal-capacity ablation is honest about the price: the monolithic dictionary reconstructs better everywhere (FVU 17–23% relative penalty) and matches on attribution-based selection, but the label-free CS selector degrades severely on it (0.712→0.494 B/16; 0.404→0.116 H/14). Two-signal agreement across depth: all 29 late blocks positive, sign test p = 1.9e-9, with the authors' own caveat that blocks within a model are not independent. **Limits they name:** static defense against a static attack family, scene-text-reading capability cost never measured, VLM margins small (Gemma pooled p = 7.6e-5; InternVL nil) with no superiority claim there. Read as the folder's cheapest example of two independent signals as mutual validation and its sharpest example of a similarity space manufacturing the separation it is used to measure
arxiv-2611.10045 | Don't Blame The Preferences: Examining The Emergence of Sycophancy in LLM Training | Duke University / Yale (Yang, Zehr, Bijith, Fain) | 2026-11-05 | 3 — full text (arXiv HTML v2, ~158.7k chars: abstract + §1–§5 + references + Appendices A–D incl. per-checkpoint tables, steering-check table, letter-push diagnostic, random-direction control, dataset rankings; figures NOT inspected, so every number is a table cell or an explicit sentence). Sycophancy traced across every released checkpoint of three fully open pipelines (OLMo 3 7B, Tülu 3 8B, SmolLM3 3B) on three benchmarks (SycEval rebuttal, Perez opinion, ELEPHANT social) **and** across the training *data* of every stage via one Contrastive-Activation-Addition direction per model (mean paired residual difference at the answer token, layer chosen by maximal admissible log-odds range, verified by α-sweep steering against three random directions at matched magnitude, then used as a centred projection scorer on every dataset). **Headline:** preference alignment is not the main source — SFT checkpoints hold ≥92% of the aligned model's score and ≥85% of the final model's in all nine cells, later stages raise it in only 4 of 9 (mean +4.2pts); pretraining data projects *below* the pipeline mean in every family, SFT and preference data both above with comparable per-example means (SmolLM3 +0.025 both; OLMo preference higher +0.066 vs +0.052; Tülu SFT higher +0.014 vs +0.001), but SFT sees **43×/17×/7×** more examples and supplies the four top-scoring datasets in all three families. Steering moves open-ended sycophancy +27/+20pts (SmolLM3/Tülu) against +1.0 to +4.0 for random directions; OLMo barely moves at its admissible layer. **Best methodological content:** the letter-push diagnostic — at the sum-rule layer the A/B metric is dominated by a shift in log P(A)−log P(B) (+3.53 logits regardless of which option is sycophantic), so the same steering appears to *reduce* sycophancy under both signs of α; steering only the assistant turn removes it. Chosen-minus-rejected projections are positive in all families (preference objective does select for the direction, it just has far less data). Human-written SHP pairs show only +0.006 to +0.010. Relevance 3: the cheapest instrument this folder has seen for attributing a behaviour to training data, and a clean worked example of exposure × intensity arithmetic and of an option-letter artefact
arxiv-2611.00640 | Bridging Singular Learning Theory and Mechanistic Interpretability at the Billion-Parameter Scale | UCLA (Ji, Wang, Sun) | 2026-10-31 | 3 — full text (arXiv HTML v1, ~231k clean chars: abstract + §1–§7 + references + Appendices A–Q incl. proofs, developmental protocol, SGLD displacement tables, matched Metropolis calibration, sensitivity panel, subspace-verification tables; figures NOT inspected, so every number is a table cell or an explicit sentence). The first test of whether singular-learning-theory geometry and MI causal accounts describe the same object in a pretrained LM: Pythia-1B at **all 154 checkpoints** on bounded Dyck bracket matching, 144 components (16 layers × 8 heads + MLPs), 22,176 component–checkpoint geometry cells. Method: a five-stage 26-component circuit established by interventions *only* (zero-ablation + greedy restoration to 80% contrast + attention/probes/DLA/path patching), verified on held-out strings by removing named variable subspaces — STACK removal drops held-out closer accuracy **61.4%→14.8%** against 58.4–62.6% for rank-matched random subspaces, ANSWER removal to 39.3% against 60.4–61.8% random — then local geometry measured with parameter draws **confined to an explicit region U** where predictions are preserved (KL ≤ 0.02 nats, |ΔCE| ≤ 0.1, |Δacc| ≤ 0.02, ≤ 0.5 RMS per tensor at u, u/2, u/4), with the actual KL and its Fisher quadratic compared **on the same draws** so the quartic part is isolated. **The audit is the headline for us:** under documented SGLD settings chains displace each weight by a **median 4.76× its matrix RMS** (2.96–6.88× under the reference implementation's own settings) and **0/48 chains stay inside U**; holding a chain at 0.05 RMS needs γ ≈ 7×10⁵, seven orders above the sweep's value, at which point only the prior is sampled; the estimator's own statistic is **negative in 21–24 of 24 conditions** under reference settings (the chain fine-tunes the model on the batch). Findings: Metropolis LLC ranks components as zero-ablation does at **Spearman 0.64** on synthetic inputs but only **0.16** on natural bracket contexts (heads −0.18); across training Fisher-trace changes track ablation changes at median partial **0.51** (97% of components positive) but pairing the trace with the *population-median* trajectory already gives **0.50**, so the excess is positive for only 82%; the **gain Fisher** ‖o_c‖² under the downstream Fisher metric gives **0.98 levels / 0.97 changes** partial and is component-specific (0.12 against the population median), and with it as a covariate the trace's association collapses to **0.02**; the large-temperature soft-rank count tracks the non-circuit population (0.47) but barely the 26 circuit components (**0.09**); the joint estimate for the eight layer-0 heads is **5.8% below** the sum of members (3.90 vs 4.14, 5.7× a conservative MCSE) and the group's joint drop exceeds even the **absolute** marginal sum from step 8,000 on; pairwise interaction curvature tracks non-additive joint ablation at **0.16/0.14/0.16** over 43 circuit pairs against **0.05** for 43 matched controls. A coordinate control (isotropic prior in fixed converted tensors) changes the natural-context association from 0.56 to 0.16, which the authors print as a dependence on parameter metric rather than a robustness detail. Three late MLPs whose ANSWER removal *raises* accuracy by 2.5–2.8 points. Relevance 3: a measurement theory for component importance that needs no replacement value, and the cleanest demonstration in the folder that a sampler's radius is a claim that must be verified rather than assumed
arxiv-2611.00420 | Circuit Oracle: Automating Attribution Graph Analysis via Natural-Language Queries | Independent / UCLA / University of Maryland (Kabir, Tan, Agrawal, Chereddy, Balasubramanian) | 2026-10-30 | 3 — full text (arXiv HTML v1, ~137k clean chars: abstract + §1–§7 + references + Appendices B–G incl. the nine-tool inventory, an exemplar skill and subagent prompt, the tool-ablation and orchestrator tables, the full 50-prompt jailbreak table and a refusal-vs-censorship case study; figures NOT inspected, so every number is a table cell or an explicit sentence). An off-the-shelf frontier LLM (Minimax-M3 orchestrator + GPT-OSS-120B subagents) reads a transcoder **attribution graph** through nine tools and a task-specific "skill" (static prompt + tool subset + workflow), answers a natural-language question, and emits a curated subgraph of supernodes. Three proxy tasks. **Spurious probes** (Gemma-2-2B, 80 graphs): 78.2% ± 2.4 against 72.5% transcoder-cosine and 65.0% SAE-cosine, but the cosine baselines are 40/40 on biased rankings and 0/10 on the unbiased ones where the spurious cue is not aligned with the probe direction — and on this task the agent does nothing, since removing Neuronpedia inspection *raises* accuracy to 81% and one-shot top-k with no traversal scores 77.5%. **Secret elicitation** (Qwen3-8B taboo LoRAs, 120 runs): 75.0% closed-menu against 25.8% SAE-projection and 1.7% transcoder-projection, yet behind the trained Activation Oracle at 86.7%/74.2%; open-vocabulary falls to 43.3% (the largest drop of any method) and for some secrets the concept is absent from the transcoder logit-lens at every layer, i.e. a basis limit rather than an agent failure; the mechanism that works is the **target-minus-sibling diff** against a base-model graph on the same prompt, which deletes shared chat-template features in one pass. **Refusal suppression** (Qwen3-4B, 50 prompts): committed pick 0.804 against 0.825 for the diff-in-mean refusal-direction baseline (10W/20T/20L), 0.854 best-of-five (16/24/10), and here the ablation bites — one-shot top-k gives +0.074 over no intervention against +0.766 for the closed loop. **Two things worth carrying:** five passes on fixed graphs agree on the pinned features at only **Jaccard 0.511** while agreeing on the layers at 0.754, so a stable verdict does not mean a stable circuit; and a feature whose auto-interp label says code is pinned across refusal prompts as a safety precursor that can be cut to release output, with 81% accuracy when labels are removed entirely. Limits: judges are the measuring instrument on two of three tasks and are never validated against humans; the probe-task scoring rule rewards a "mixed"-biased detector on half the set; orchestrator choice moves the score by as much as the margin over baseline; blocked judge calls are set to the maximum rather than dropped, symmetric across arms.
arxiv-2610.39182 | Reasoning or Pattern Exploitation? Mechanistic Insights from RL-Trained Language Models | Carnegie Mellon University / A*STAR IHPC-CFAR (Nagar, Tan) | 2026-10-29 | 3 — full text (arXiv HTML v1, ~110k clean chars: abstract + §1–§5 + references + Appendices A–N, no figure inspected so every number is a table cell or an explicit sentence). GRPO on Tic-Tac-Toe, three models (Qwen2.5-0.5B/1.5B Instruct, Llama-3.2-1B), eight checkpoints × eleven of 28 layers, seven pre-declared hypothesis families, SAEs fit on generic C4-10k rather than in-domain. **Behaviour up, mechanism flat:** best-move accuracy 8.0% → 75.6% (Qwen2.5-1.5B NL), legal-move 100%, arena forfeits 0 vs 121 untrained and 77% per-move minimax-optimality, yet the raw-activation probes separate turn / progression / legality at **AUC 1.00 in the base model** and strategic situation at 0.686 base vs 0.684 final (MLP +0.05–0.06, flat), so nothing was added — only which features dominate. **Prominence is what moves:** layer-12 turn F 5,285 → 316 (17×) while progression 274 → 2,330 (8.5×), strategic 82 → 194 and symmetry 26 → 12, with Cramér's V 0.70 → 0.36 for turn, 0.48 → 0.62 for progression, and the projection-free PC1 class-mean share reproducing it (turn 0.151 → 0.062, progression ~0.20). **Decision is late:** patching at layer 25/28 flips 66/100 minimal pairs to the donor optimum against 10 unpatched and 12 for a random donor, no effect at layers 13/17/21; steering along the turn direction never flips X/O move tokens while norm-matched random directions disrupt. **Symmetry is unused:** 56.7% training / 57.7% held-out equivariance (60.0 / 62.7% on unique-optimum pairs whose ceiling is 100%) and held-out is no worse, so it is not a split artefact. Branching "advantage" is a chance artefact (83.1% low-branch at 76.0% chance vs 63.6% high-branch at 39.2% chance ⇒ skill 29.7% vs 40.2%); terminal detection is 97.8% at checkpoint one. Illegal boards decodable at AUC 1.00 at every layer while the policy emits legal continuations; ASCII costs ~40 relative points and kills the turn macro-pattern; traces narrate correct minimax moves over false board descriptions. Every F in the main table is nominally p < 10⁻⁶⁸ because the sample is all 5,478 states, so the paper's own defence is relative magnitudes plus V/AMI — worth reading as a caution about quoting F at all. Limits they name: 0.5–1.5B, GRPO, one closed game, one read position, 11 layers, closed hypothesis set, SFT baseline failed, memory-vs-abstraction argued only indirectly. Limits I add (mine): H4 symmetry-class is a 0.70 pair-score the authors say partly reflects piece counts, so it is near-unfalsifiable; the sampled layers are dense exactly where the mid-layer claim lives (12–18) and sparse late (20, 24); and "no signal to require search" is not separated from "at ceiling" because the reward is per-move correctness.
arxiv-2611.12206 | A Geometric Invariant of Attention Predicts Head Specialization from Weights Alone | Feedzai, Lisbon (Bento) | 2026-11-09 | 3 — full text (arXiv HTML v1, ~13.7k clean chars = the entire five-page NeurReps extended abstract: abstract, §1–§2 with Proposition 1 and proof, §3.1–§3.2 with both tables, §4 limitations, references; no appendix, figure or sweep exists, so every number is a table cell or an explicit sentence). A **basis-invariant scalar ρ computed from weights alone** — no forward pass, no data, no intervention in the construction: take the query-key bilinear form `G_QK(0)=W_QᵀW_K` (invariant under any head-basis change), require the whole family `G_QK(Δ)` to stay invariant under rotary rotation (which shrinks that group to a rotation × positive rescaling per 2×2 plane), and define ρ as the offset-averaged ratio of the squared Frobenius norm of the positional perturbation `W_Qᵀ[R(Δ)−I]W_K` to that of the static term, sampled at powers of two up to the context length because RoPE's frequencies are themselves geometric. **Proposition 1** earns the degenerate case: ρ = 0 iff the logits are provably independent of relative position for every input — so ρ is a *capacity* bound, and the author keeps that distinction (capacity, not usage) throughout. **Validation:** induction heads sit in the low-ρ tail across four bias-free RoPE models from 135M to 7B, Spearman **−0.74 / −0.71 / −0.55 / −0.59**, all p < 10⁻⁴⁰, with the ten strongest induction heads in the bottom **14% / 7% / 17% / 30%** of the ρ distribution — that last cell is the honest cost of the screen, since a third of all heads survive the threshold at 7B. **Application:** YaRN on only the top-50% of ρ heads beats YaRN on all heads on both perplexity rows (512: 12.92 vs 19.37, baseline 7.63; 4096: 130.34 vs 162.96, baseline 5.44), with the author stating both long values are inflated near the context limit and that this is a single-model proof of concept evaluated inside the native window. **Limits they name:** RoPE-only, query-key sector only, finite sample set, sensitive to the softmax row-shift that weight decay only discourages, no measurement of the capacity/usage gap, one behaviour estimator. **Missing arm (mine):** the application never applies the edit to the bottom half or a random half at matched size, so the win is equally consistent with "edit fewer heads". Relevance 3 as the folder's cleanest weights-only observable with its invariance argued first
arxiv-2611.00155 | From Concepts to Actual Causes: Toward Closing the Concept–Malfunction Gap in Robot Foundation Models | NC AI (Ko) | 2026-10-30 | 3 — full text (arXiv HTML v1, ~26k clean chars = the whole 4-page SPAIS@CoRL 2026 position paper: abstract, §1–§5, references; one schematic figure and one table, no appendix, no experiments). A **logical specification of what a causal claim about one incident must look like**, imported from actual causality: the unrolled rollout is a structural causal model over per-step components, and an incident is a binary predicate on it. **Halpern's modified actual cause** = factuality + counterfactual dependence *under a searched contingency W held at its factual values* + minimality, plus Halpern–Chockler **responsibility 1/k** (k = components changed or held) so a lone but-for cause scores 1, a cause with one backup ½, ten backups 1/11. **The query tuple ⟨X, x′, W, Y⟩** and its four well-specification conditions: X may span steps; x′ must be inducible by some observation history; **W must be searched per incident, not fixed in advance**; Y must be the failure predicate on the full counterfactual rollout. Six method families scored against them and **none is well-specified** — steering alone spans all steps and reads out the rollout; patching, scrubbing and causal abstraction all fix W in advance; **every intervening family violates realizability**, so even the causes they report may be spurious (masked image, another input's activations, a steered state). The toy model: robot closing at 1 m/s on a person 2 m away, 10 Hz for 5 s, limit 0.3 m; hazard latent brakes, suppressor stuck at 1 blocks it, target-drive idle — breach at 1.7 s. Ground truth is the suppressor, with the drive as its backup. Single-pass patching of the suppressor gives the **full 2 m/s² braking effect** (backup never acts inside one step) yet the rollout still breaches, only **0.1 s later**; patching every step still breaches at **3.3 s** as the drive settles speed at 0.5 m/s; steering finds **no realizable setting** that averts it; only the query with the backup frozen recovers the cause at responsibility **½**, via a witness the policy would never produce. Probing sees the person and cannot explain the missing brake; masking the person changes nothing. Four open tasks named (candidate faults via an *action lens*; certifying realizable counterfactuals; contingency search, DP-complete even in explicit models; validating against planted faults with known backups). **No experiments, no metric, self-declared** — the toy is built to make naive patching fail. Relevance 3 as the folder's only entry that states the conditions an intervention must satisfy to support a singular causal verdict, and the one that makes the contingency the object of search rather than a constant

Appendices A–B; every number is a prose sentence or a table cell, since the paper's figures are subset-size and margin scatters). **The question: a group intervention that changes an answer proves the group matters, not how much of it is needed, whether another set would do, or whether it survives a rephrase** — asked of 12-neuron MLP gated-activation groups in Qwen3-0.6B/1.7B, Llama-3.2-1B, Gemma-3-1B, where 2¹² = 4,096 subsets can be **enumerated exhaustively rather than sampled**. **Study 1 (capital questions, Qwen3-0.6B) is the paper's centre of gravity:** the group passes every usual check — zero ablation −0.27 Δ/m₀ [0.26, 0.30] against −0.00 for 12 random neurons, additivity R² 0.99 slope 1.00, **0 interacting pairs**, 15–306 minimal sets at the 50% threshold with disjoint pairs in 8/10 questions, rephrasing transfer Spearman 0.96–1.00 over all 4,095 subsets, one neuron (14,692) strongest in 10/10 and still strongest under option-swap — **and yet replacing the same neurons with their values from other questions moves the answer by 0.00 / −0.01 / −0.00**, with activations nearly constant across questions (median absolute deviation 0.12 against median magnitude 2.53), replicating on 10 held-out questions (−0.24 [0.22, 0.26] for the frozen group). **Study 2 (20 two-hop chains):** first-hop patching flips the answer in 10/11, 10/11, 8/10 chains and starts greedy generation with the bridge in 9/11, 9/11, 8/10, random neurons 0/11, 0/11, 0/10; patching raises the bridge logit (median +4.1) *and* lowers the answer (−3.7) while zero ablation mostly only lowers the answer (−5.4, bridge +2.7, +0.5 in Qwen3) — **two groups of the same provenance with different signatures**. The full group is **not necessary**: smallest flipping subset 4/8/7 on the headline chain and 1–8 across chains, effects accumulating smoothly, median and strongest subset visibly apart. **Uniqueness is model-dependent:** disjoint minimal flipping pairs in 3/10, 7/10, 7/8 flipped chains, and on the seven shared chains 1/7, 4/7, 5/7 — with the author's own caveat that the four four-neuron Qwen3 subsets checked by generation all share the same two neurons. Interactions small (additive R² 0.90/0.89/0.84, slopes 0.78/0.80/0.83, 4–11 of ~660–726 pairs above threshold). **Transfer asymmetric:** 0.98/0.98/0.97 on rephrasings against 0.18/0.09/0.11 on a different chain's question, versus 1.52/1.89/1.82 on their own. **One post-hoc moderator**, flagged as exploratory: pooling 28 flipped chains, disjoint alternatives in 13/15 chains with clean margin < 4 and 4/13 above, and the four chains no subset flips all sit above margin 4.6. Appendix B lists every post-data definition change, including the one that moved Qwen3's disjoint count from 4/10 to 3/10. The reusable instrument is a **content-matched control** (write in the values other inputs produce) alongside the random-neuron control, plus the size distribution of sufficient subsets; what it is not is a circuit, which the author states three times.
arxiv-2611.00298 | A Framework for Task-Agnostic Counterfactual Fairness in Transformers through Activation Steering | University of Pittsburgh (Anderson, Wang, Visweswaran) | 2026-10-30 | 2 — full text (60.5k clean chars of arXiv HTML v1: abstract, §1–§6, limitations, ethics, references; figures NOT inspected — Figure 2's eight α-sweep panels are therefore not read, so every quoted number is a table cell or an explicit sentence). A four-stage pipeline on **TabPFN v2** (12 layers, 192-d, in-context) over UTICalc and COMPAS with race binarised: build counterfactuals under do(race), zero each layer's output projection to see how much of the fairness effect disappears, split the effect into **natural direct and natural indirect** components by patching query-position activations, then steer by adding the normalised factual-minus-counterfactual difference at the chosen layer. Two counterfactual builders are the paper's real variable: a naive `flip` arm that changes race alone (ignoring propagation) and an `LLM` arm where a local 8B model proposes which features co-vary, refined over ≤5 iterations and filtered to zero violations of a LiNGAM-estimated graph (PC-CVR 0.319 UTICalc / 0.228 COMPAS, 271 and 2,853 pairs retained). **The localisation is nearly empty:** the best layer recovers **1.3% (layer 9) and 1.6% (layer 10)** of the effect, layer 0 at 0.01%, layer 11 at ~1.2e-5%, every other layer 0.1–0.4% — and layer 10 is then used for everything. **Steering at α = 75 lowers the total effect in 7/8 vector–dataset cells:** `flip` vectors give the big reductions (UTICalc vector −0.154 [−0.160, −0.148] on UTICalc with accuracy −0.055; −0.089 on COMPAS with −0.043) but the **COMPAS flip vector *raises* the effect on COMPAS by +0.165 [0.099, 0.230]**, i.e. non-monotone in α and worse than doing nothing; `LLM` vectors give −0.018 to −0.088 at little or no accuracy cost (exactly zero accuracy change in one cell because no label crossed threshold). Reductions concentrate in the **direct** effect with the indirect effect unchanged or slightly up. Baselines AUROC 0.809/0.825, accuracy 0.755/0.748, equalised-odds difference **0.171 vs 0.315**. **The reusable instrument is the two-generator comparison**: the near-3× gap in target effect between naive flips and filtered LLM counterfactuals is an effect size for the *generator*, and its sign-flip on accuracy (large cost vs none) is what a single-generator pipeline would have reported as the attribute's effect. The reusable warning is the missing **matched-norm random steering arm**: a vector read off the layer that already won the ablation sweep is not separated from "a large perturbation at layer 10 happens to shrink probability gaps", and non-monotonicity plus one cell of exact-zero accuracy change are the tells
arxiv-2611.18514 | Can Mechanistic Interpretability Help Explain LLM Agent Failures? A Case Study on Web Element Hallucination | George Mason University (Aghzal, Geva, Stein, Yao) | 2026-11-13 | 3 — full text (91k clean chars: abstract, §1–§6, references and Appendices A–L; every number below is a table cell or an explicit sentence, since the paper's core diagnostic figures — per-layer probe AUC curves, the steering sweep panels, the SAE top-feature list — were NOT inspected as images). **The question is negative and the answer is a clean split between prediction and explanation.** Setup: Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct (28 layers) navigating Online-Mind2Web with an explicit action list, 300 episodes / 2,071 actions, **32.6% hallucinated** (selector not in the list). **Prediction works:** a linear probe at the end of the prompt, *before any output token exists*, reaches **0.80 test AUC at layer 18** (episode-level 70/15/15 split, 1,433/282/356), probes at selector start are strong from layer 0, and steering off the probe weight (σ-scaled, normalised, swept over five magnitudes) gives **85.4% fix / 7.0% break** at selector-start layer 17 with the middle magnitude — but **0.0% fix / 96.9% break one step up the sweep**. **Explanation fails:** the best joint head/FFN intervention in a large grid fixes **8.9%** (2.4% break), top-10 heads 4.4%, and the highest-fix large-scale configurations reach 100% fix by breaking 100% of valid actions; single-component intervention produces a *qualitatively different* failure (a garbled long-numeric-suffix selector rather than a well-formed nonexistent element), which the authors read as proof no component carries the decision — a cheap and checkable validity criterion for failure-count experiments. **Patching locates a span but no property:** corrupting the action list and restoring the layer-27 residual recovers **0.360** of probability mass for the search token against 0.075 (attention) and 0.067 (FFN), and under 0.02 for the selector and task-description spans — but the span that works varies by example, because in ~half of valid steps the agent reuses a value already emitted earlier in the trajectory and is unaffected by corrupting the current list. **Counterfactuals are structurally unavailable:** deleting the chosen valid action produces a hallucination in only **38%** of cases, and by the next step the two runs agree on the selector ~25% of the time and land on pages with **0.52** word-level DOM Jaccard. **The sharpest reusable result is the format shift:** the same layer-17 vector on a numbered-markdown action list gives **58.1% fix at the same 7.0% break** at α = 40 (−27.3 points), with the probe curve degrading across all layers — the direction is entangled with prompt syntax, not with the construct. The pretrained SAE's top class-differentiating features promote tokens unrelated to the behaviour. Limits stated plainly: one model, one benchmark, one failure mode; no random-direction arm at matched norm (the admissibility evidence is that break rate explodes at larger magnitudes); and the authors' roadmap asks for agentic MI benchmarks with a defined failure ground truth, attribution over distributed multi-timestep computation, counterfactual construction in stateful environments, interaction-trained dictionaries, plan-crystallisation tracking, and a universality question across agent types
arxiv-2611.14483 | Mechanistic Interpretability Needs a Logic of Inference | Georgia Tech (Hu, Jiang, Xie, Ratan Murty, Varma) | 2026-11-10 | 3 — full text (39k clean chars of arXiv HTML v1: abstract, §1–§6, acknowledgments, references; nothing else exists, since this is a position paper with **no models trained and no benchmarks run**, so there are no numbers, baselines or ablations to quote anywhere). A NeurIPS 2026 Interp4Discovery oral that supplies the folder's **claim vocabulary** rather than a method: five named inferential questions hanging off a measurement condition. **P0** — mechanistic inference is measurement-dependent (saliency maps can show compelling structure weakly tied to computation; activation-patching results move with the corruption procedure and the metric), so a measurement choice counts as evidence. **P1** — the explanatory object is a hypothesis, not an a-priori level; testable in both directions by asking whether an adjacent level is sufficient (population activity carries a task variable after individually selective units are excluded; unit selectivity is a poor predictor of downstream task performance; yet Bau-style interpretable units with causal effects exist, so the neuron is not excluded either). **P2** — represented vs computationally implicated information: decodable does not mean used, and probing accuracy need not track behavioural importance. **P3** — causal relevance vs causal role: zebra-finch song-region *acute silencing* disrupts song while a *permanent lesion* produces only a transient deficit that recovers, and attention-head ablation is followed by input-varying downstream compensation — so an observed change reflects both the target and the system's response to being disturbed, and a role claim needs predictions about where and when an effect appears. **P4** — causal role vs mechanistic organisation, with the sharpest worked example in the paper: unilateral ALM silencing during a delay period is followed by recovery, bilateral silencing is not, which separates redundant maintenance from local recurrent dynamics and from inter-hemispheric coupling using *two* perturbations rather than one. **P5** — recurse for invariants and revisit the object: circuit-level algorithms persist across training/task/scale while the specific components change, and independently trained SAEs can be unstable per-feature while their subspaces reproduce. **What counts as an explanation:** not a dictionary or a circuit but a form of argument — five claims, each with its own evidence requirement, explicitly partial. **Named limits:** evidence beyond the models is ultimately required to establish what a mechanism reveals about the world, and interpretation can be overgeneralised, so model mechanisms are offered as hypothesis generators; unstated but visible, no principle is operationalised (no thresholds, no worked case where the framework reverses a published conclusion) and the recursive order is not a schedule, so nothing fixes how much of the chain a claim must walk. **The reusable instrument is the ALM paired-perturbation recipe** — suppress one part, check recovery, then suppress both and check again — which separates redundancy from coupling for two interventions per claim
arxiv-2610.27393 | Correcting Attribution Patching with a Few Exact Measurements | Carnegie Mellon University / UC Santa Cruz (Zhang, Zheng, Wang) | 2026-10-19 | 3 — full text (arXiv HTML v1, ~85k clean chars: abstract, §1–§5, references, Appendices A–I incl. label acquisition, the ProbeCal curvature sketches, three theorems with proofs, all within-prompt control tables, the error-structure diagnostics, the six-cell confirmation protocol with its Bonferroni family, cost frontiers and cost accounting; figures NOT inspected, so every number is a table cell or an explicit sentence and intervals are the paper's own). An **estimator paper, not a behaviour paper**: exact activation patching is one intervention per component per prompt, attribution patching is one backward pass for all of them, and the question is whether AP's *errors* are structured enough that a few exact measurements can repair many estimates. Three observed patterns (error concentrated in a few components, about 80% in the top four on a GPT-2 toy; biased away from zero within a prompt; repeatable across prompts for a component) give two arms. **Within-prompt:** spend part of the budget on a pilot sampled **stratified by attribution magnitude** in eight rank strata, shift every unmeasured estimate by the **median** pilot residual, verify the largest magnitudes with the rest — 17.4 / 26.2 / 19.9% lower MAE than runtime-matched verify-more on Gemma-2-9B, 11–24% on Qwen3-14B, 14–18% on Qwen3-32B at 1% of its 25,600 neurons; the fitted shift captures 96–97% of the best common shift's benefit; 96–98 of 100 groups win. **The sharpest control: learning the shift from the same magnitude-ranked components verification would have used *raises* error in 8 of 9 settings** (relative MAE 1.93 vs 1.00 on IOI, 1.80 on SVA), so a representative pilot and a high-attribution sample of equal size are not interchangeable; median beats mean in all nine settings; pilots of 32–64 suffice. Below AtP*, selective HVP, MS-HVP and singleton IG at 1.3–2.7 s per layer against their 19–170 s, but **targets matter**: 7.9× faster at a 1% error target on IOI, slower on SVA (0.43 s for 32 checks), and it *misses* the 1% target on factual completion (1.20%). **Across-prompt:** corrections of dataset-average effects cut MAE **75–79%** on factual completion with no measurement on evaluated prompts, vs 46% for curvature-probe labels, and using the same calibration measurements directly as averages is **5.8–14.9× worse** than modelling the residual — but allocating verification by the learned size model is a wash (12% gain on Qwen3 IOI at B=16, 2–4% on SVA, harms four cells, 108 of 120 comparisons favour verify-more at 1,000 prompts, break-even 1,900–24,000 prompts) and it **raises Qwen2.5 IOI average error by 52%** (selection bias in label acquisition). Appendix honesty worth copying: lower MAE does not imply recall (11% MAE gain, top-ten recall 99.2→97.0), the dataset-average residual is 81–96% sign-consistent (bias repair, not per-prompt), and curvature-probe noise grows with coupling (median off-diagonal/diagonal Hessian ratio 5.8→77.2 from 32 to 1,152 heads). Theory: within-prompt gain = shared-shift benefit − pilot estimation cost − displaced verification, and a selection score must predict the *expected size* of remaining error, not the size of the expected error
arxiv-2610.34756 | Mechanistic Insights into LoRA: Layer Sparsity for Adaptive Fine-Tuning via Path Patching | East China Normal University / Brunel University of London (Zuo, Huang, Wang) | 2026-10-26 | 3 — full text (arXiv HTML v1, ~107k clean chars: abstract, §1–§7, acknowledgments, references, Appendices A–G including the calibration tables, the cross-distribution arm, the cross-architecture panel and the depth-constrained speed study; figures NOT inspected as images, so every number below is a table cell or an explicit sentence). **An intervention study whose measurement is a ranking, and whose contribution is that a frozen base model can supply that ranking before any adapter is trained.** Trained-adapter analysis (Qwen2.5-0.5B, 24 layers, rank 8 on Q/K/V, four instruction corpora): zero-ablating a component and reading the probability change of the adapted model's own top token gives **MLP 58.4% vs attention 41.6%**, flat across corpora (57.3–59.5%), and within attention **V ≈ 46% against ≈ 27% each for Q and K** (44.3–47.7% per corpus) — but the **no-LoRA reference already shows a 56–60% MLP share**, so the adapter's own increment is only +3.0 to +6.3 points and the headline share is mostly a property of the base network (Table 7 plus an independent output-replacement experiment at rank 8 and rank 16). Layer importance is front-loaded with task-specific middle and late layers, and top-7 selections overlap across tasks at a mean pairwise **Jaccard of 39.5%** (27.3–75.0%), with only layers 0–2 shared by all four. **The method:** score each layer on the **frozen base model** — zero its attention output and its MLP output in separate forward passes, sum the two absolute drops in the probability of the base's own predicted token, average over ~30 inputs, take the top-k, adapt only those layers. Agreement between this cheap score and the trained-adapter contribution is Spearman **0.630–0.893** (Q/V configurations highest) with top-7 recovery 5/7 in all five configurations and Pearson 0.935–0.975 on raw scores; **selection stability is only moderate — Jaccard 0.617–0.747 across five draws at N = 30 on six models, and 0.524 → 0.653 → 0.685 as N goes 10 → 30 → 100**. Downstream on Qwen2.5-1.5B: 8 of 28 layers beats full-layer LoRA at every rank — commonsense **59.1 vs 56.9 (r16), 62.2 vs 60.4 (r32)**, maths 47.8 vs 45.5 (r32) — and the perplexity-versus-budget curve shows the largest relative gain over random selection **at 12 of 36 layers: 12.32 vs 16.42 PPL (−25.0%)**, sequential 14.06, reverse 15.00, with all four strategies collapsing to the identical 5.75 when the budget covers every layer. Cross-model at the 30% budget: **+1.2 to +2.2 points on four models from 0.5B to 3B, a tie at 8B, and −0.2 at 32B**, and the three-run means shrink accordingly (+1.53 ± 0.15 at 7B, +0.17 ± 0.15 at 8B, +0.23 ± 0.38 at 32B). Cross-distribution calibration (calibrate on one corpus, train on another) drops selection Jaccard to 0.453–0.512 yet keeps 72.8–73.5% against 75.2% matched and 70.2% random. **Two things the entry is built around:** the three yardsticks are separate numbers that do not move together — the ranking agrees with the trained object, the ranking is reproducible only about two-thirds of the time, and the downstream win is real at small scale and gone by 32B — and the share decomposition has a base-model floor that turns "MLP carries 58%" into "the network routes 56–60% through MLPs anyway". arxiv-2610.27790 | Are Linguistic and Arithmetic Circuits Dissociated in LLMs? | Nagoya University / MBZUAI / Tohoku (Kisako, Kuribayashi, Sasano) | 2026-10-20 | 3 — full text (arXiv HTML v1, ~68k clean chars: abstract, §1–§8 with limitations, references, Appendices A–F with the raw cross-task faithfulness matrices for all five models; figures NOT inspected as images, so IoU is only described qualitatively and every quoted number is a table cell or an explicit sentence). **A dissociation study designed as a lattice of controlled tasks, and its real contribution is a cross-application protocol instead of an overlap statistic.** Ten tasks on five models (Qwen3-8B/4B/1.7B, Llama-3.1-8B, Gemma-2-2B), 500 clean/corrupted pairs each, circuits extracted with EAP-IG (5 interpolation steps) under three boundaries (top 2.5%, top 5%, adaptive-to-self-faithfulness-0.85). Two measures kept separate: **IoU** on edge sets for structure, and **cross-task faithfulness** for function — run task A's clean input with task A's own corrupted activations patched into every edge *outside* circuit B, so the transferred object is B's mask and no activations from task B ever enter the run; recovered fraction of A's clean-corrupted logit gap, then divided column-wise by A's self-faithfulness. Findings: arithmetic and linguistic circuits are largely dissociated in every model and under every boundary (clearer under the adaptive definition, which strips extra edges from the spelling circuits); **surface form does not fix the circuit** — digits and number words give similar circuits and neither resembles the spelling tasks; **instruction does** — same expressions asked to be spelled out rather than computed move the circuit from calculation targets to expression-conversion targets; and the **matched parsing pair (operator precedence vs. sentence head-word) is the most dissociated case in the suite**, near-zero in both directions in most models despite being built to give cross-domain sharing its best chance. Model-to-model clustering agreement moderate (mean ARI ≈0.45–0.63 by cut level) with Llama-3.1-8B apart from everything else at cophenetic 0.45–0.59 against 0.95–0.98 among the other four — an average across models that describes none of them. **Three reporting defects the entry is built around:** no random or shuffled-label circuit as a null; self-faithfulness exceeds 1.0 at the diagonal (Llama SV 1.60, Gemma Ling-Parse 1.26) and every cross-task cell is divided by it; IoU exists only as heatmap colour, so the structural half of every comparison is never quoted as a number. Limits named by the authors: one extraction method, first-token scoring (appendices re-score four tasks at sequence level), English only, low GSM-Plus accuracy, five open-weight models, and the bridge back to the human literature left open. Relevance 3 because the mask-transfer protocol is the cheapest available specificity test the folder lacks, and because the paper's own most-carefully-matched pair is the case that most strongly refutes the reader's prior.
arxiv-2610.38326 | Can LLMs Faithfully Solve Ontology Engineering Tasks? A Mechanistic Interpretability Study | King's College London / TU Munich (Zhang, Meroño-Peñuela, Simperl) | 2026-10-28 | 3 — full text (arXiv HTML v1, ~96k clean chars: abstract, §1–§5 with conclusion, references, Appendices A–C incl. Tables 1–13; figures 1–16 NOT inspected as images, so every layer-profile shape quoted is from the authors' prose and every number is a table cell or an explicit sentence). **A causal-abstraction benchmark, and the folder's cleanest instance of the two-sided intervention criterion: an intervention must move the designated variable *and* leave a designated non-variable alone.** Two ontology tasks on Qwen3-4B/8B (36 layers): `instance of` (54 classes, 1,099 subjects) and `subclass of` (39 classes, 1,113 subjects), each subject carrying class-specific auxiliary attributes; a base subject is intervened with a source of a different class, and **Cause** scores whether the target answer moves to the source's class while **Iso** scores whether the base's auxiliary attribute survives, both over accepted-answer sets and macro-averaged over prompt-template groups. and no random-direction or shuffled-label target is run as a null, while the paper's own `Dimension` column reports ambient mask width (effective sizes 1,631–2,580) beside 1024/2048-rank subspaces
