Gated DeltaNet layers) told to retell Cinderella and to describe making a sandwich, with a five-way classifier — control, Broca, Wernicke, conduction-or-anomic, **model scrambles** — over 2,843 texts reaching 83.3% held-out accuracy and 0.78 macro-F1. **CLAMP** grows head sets by single-knockout target probability and prunes to the 0.5 boundary: 38/42 settings reach the target against 25 for classifier-guided parameter lesions, 23 for a CEM head search, and 2–10 for every corpus-similarity-guided baseline; 54/66 across all eleven models; experts agree with the classifier on 90% of lesioned answers. **Lesions act on the prompt**: restoring all heads' clean prompt activations undoes 53/54 lesions, but restoring a single head — which undoes the lesion for a median 83% of a set — yields 80% model scrambles and 13% control speech, while restoring all heads repairs 22 of 40. **The type does not travel**: Broca/Wernicke lesions hit the target in 1 of 184 answers on the other narrative and the TAB, while one mean-difference direction hits it in 67 of 153 new-prompt answers (both experts label 65% / 63% of non-scramble answers). **No family separation**: Broca and Wernicke lesion pairs share a median 54% of the smaller set's heads (517 against 165 chance, 11/13 pairs p < 0.01) yet the shared heads reach the target on neither task; vectors sit at median cosine 0.79–0.90 within a task against 0.97–0.99 for label-shuffled (below the 2.5th percentile at 670/672 layers), and the **1% leaving-out** is where the type lives — projecting the pooled aphasia direction out makes the Wernicke-specific vector work at 94/176 layers against 25, while the Broca-specific one drops 78 → 29. **The honest negatives**: "every head is needed" is mostly CLAMP's own pruning criterion (47 vs 37 lesions by the two definitions, and informative only for the 7 unpruned sets), and steering back undoes 53/54 lesions but 52 become scrambles, with matched-norm random directions at 91% against the own direction's 95% — so no single-direction specificity. Classifier labels are moderate against experts (κ 0.44 / 0.54) on a sheet where classifier and LLM judge agree at κ 0.00

arxiv-2611.10400 | Detecting and Explaining Robot Policy Failures from Sparse Internal Features | University of New Hampshire (CARL Lab) | 2026-11-07 | 3 — full text (arXiv HTML v1, ~92k chars, Appendices A–E; figures via captions only). **A fixed naming rule turns "a human writes what the top activations have in common" into a measurement that can fail, and it certifies a dictionary on three VLA policies while certifying nothing for neurons, random directions or an untrained copy.** Naming is precision/recall over a predefined 74-concept state-and-decision vocabulary with a precision floor of 0.9, chosen on frames excluded from the SAE fit and re-tested on unseen initial states, an unseen task, unseen layouts and a physical Franka arm; names hold 15–31 decision and 13–28 state on OpenVLA and 42–53 concepts per π0.5 task against 13–16 for raw coordinates and 8–11 for random directions, with two random starts sharing 81–83% (73% same name). **The four-arm clamp is the discipline worth copying:** one feature at 1.5× max takes the named action share from ≤0.34 to ≥0.92 on 12/14 OpenVLA motion concepts (5–22 cm in 20 steps), matching a label-fitted regression direction without labels, while other live features and equal-norm random directions never add 0.08. **Two results that cut against the headline:** on π0.5 the clamp acts *against* the name — nine named features, four summed families and four supervised probes all anti-aligned with the action gradient at cosines −0.08 to −0.26, and the 14 of 20 gradient-aligned features carry the opposite label at precision 0.04–0.37, below the naming floor, so clamping one of those raises +x steps 0.36→1.00 and drops success 1.00→0.00; and the diagnosis is faithful to its record (85–89% of answers match, top concept in the top three in a median 90–95% of bootstrap refits) but its failure *type* is right in only 15 of 48 OpenVLA failures, about as often as a mechanical rule on the same record. Detection ties the dense monitor with every input named (π0.5 0.991 vs 0.988, 99% vs 95% caught at 3% vs 6% false alarms, 10 vs 25 steps after onset), memory features earn 0.08–0.13 AUROC, and the robot's chance-level intervals are attributed to a measured cause — only 9–14% of a stopped robot attempt's frames show the failure against 64–66% in simulation, not sample size
arxiv-2611.09233 | Cross-Layer TopK: Dynamically Allocating Sparsity Across Layers in Transcoders | ByteDance Seed / Shanghai Univ. of Finance and Economics / HKU | 2026-11-06 | 2 — full text (17pp PDF, 51.2k chars incl. Appendices A–D; figures via captions only; no HTML v1, text extracted from the PDF). **Sparsity is exact and free to move across depth, and the price is a 0.57-point FVU average with the last nine layers paying for the middle:** a CLT on Qwen3-8B-Base (36 layers, m = 32,768) ranks the pooled latents of all layers and keeps 360 per token instead of 10 in every layer, giving FVU 27.07% against 27.64% at the same budget and 32.17% for JumpReLU which overshoots its 360 target to 452 and leaves 41.2% of latents dead versus 3.0–3.2%. **The learned allocation is the measurement:** per-layer FVU runs 5–12% early, above 40% in layers 15–19 and under 4% at layer 36, Cross-Layer TopK answers with 12–14 latents in layers 10–22 (peak 14.36 at layer 16) and 0.90 at layer 36, correlating r = 0.85 with per-layer FVU across layers 5–27 — 27 of 36 layers improve (best +2.0 in layer 1) and the nine exceptions are exactly the last nine (worst −2.2 in layer 35). **Circuits follow reconstruction, weakly:** replacement 0.617 vs 0.577 per-layer / 0.578 BatchTopK / 0.589 JumpReLU and completeness 0.902 vs 0.894 / 0.889 / 0.897 on 100 filtered prompts, so a tenth of the influence at important nodes still arrives through opaque error nodes on the best arm. **Two reversals the achieved-L0 column explains:** Cross-Layer BatchTopK wins during training (26.98%) and loses at inference (29.04%) because its post-hoc per-latent threshold is fitted on the batches where a bursty latent fired most; and JumpReLU's quadratic penalty vanishes as L0 approaches target so sparsity stalls above it. Fixed per-token budget makes sparse decoding trivial — 0.355 s vs 1.025 s per step on 128 GPUs (2.9×, compute −65%, communication −67%) — and reconstruction scales as FVU ∝ m^-0.16, ∝ k^-0.13. arxiv-2611.10760 | ConwayNet: fixed Game-of-Life-style lateral inhibition as a parameter-free layer regulator | Independent (Yair Cohen, Scott M. arxiv-2610.27375 | Shaped SAEs: Expanding the Toolkit for Mechanistic Interpretability | UC Santa Barbara (Delafrooz Noroozi, Puranik, Madhow) | 2026-10-19 | 3 — full text (arXiv HTML v1, ~55k clean chars: abstract, §1–§7, references, Appendices A–E; figures via captions only, so the probe-accuracy and CE curves are recorded as the text states them and no value is read off a plot). **Two contributions that do not stack, which is the entry's point.** Shaping = rank-r projection of the residual stream onto the top-1536 right singular vectors of the corpus-averaged Jacobian (r swept 768–1792); the dictionary = MF-SAE, which scores atoms by rectified inner product with a learned affine latent and reconstructs with the *same* unit-norm atoms, so encoder and decoder are tied up to one learned linear map. arxiv-2611.05003 | Unifying and Beyond the Theory of Pervasive Capability Degradation in LLM Post-training SFT from Feature Interference | Nanyang Technological University + CUHK (Chen, Zhu, Wang) | 2026-11-05 | 3 — full text (arXiv HTML v2, 149k clean chars: §1–§6, references, Appendices A–I; figures via captions only; the HTML build strips inline math, so the §G.3 hyperparameter values render blank and none are quoted here, and every P-Acc. figure below is a mean over per-task columns computed for this entry, not a printed row). **A training-side theory of capability loss whose reusable part is a per-direction attribution instrument.** Superposition plus narrow SFT is argued to raise the higher-order reconstruction term, collapse correlated features onto a principal component, and push activations across the unembedding's Laguerre–Voronoi hyperplanes toward competitors whose features overlap the task's; the weight-level step runs through a *posited* virtual parameterisation with one idealised row per feature (existence proved in Appendix A), and the collapse step is never measured in a trained model. Six mitigation paradigms are re-derived with a specific ceiling each (KL dilution, coverage sample bound, nonlinearity attenuation, pruning variance trade, dimensional exhaustion, separability denied). **AEGIS** constrains per-competitor normalised margins from a frozen-base reference — adaptive tolerance width plus a Huber on margin drift beyond it and on any negative margin — with its strength solved exactly by 1-D bisection. **Results (Mistral-7B / Qwen2.5-7B / Gemma-2-9B):** NLU P-Acc. 0.5368 / 0.6596 / 0.5617 against vanilla 0.5194 / 0.6454 / 0.5479 and the best rival CLUE 0.5271 / 0.6514 / 0.5541; non-NLU retention 0.3981 / 0.5924 / 0.5916 against vanilla 0.3739 / 0.5457 / 0.5599. The vanilla arm reads the same pervasive mixture, so the gain is attributable to the loss rather than the data. **The instrument:** a crossed-edge census on Qwen2.5-7B where total crossings (16.6–16.9 per thousand on RTE) and repaired crossings (~8.6) are flat across all seven methods while *new* crossings separate them — 4.309 / 3.101 / 1.370 for AEGIS against 5.700 / 4.373 / 1.941 for vanilla (RTE / GSM8K / CodeAlpaca) — and the drift table names the direction: vanilla's top new MRPC crossings are `␣yes`/`␣no`/`yes`/`no` (10,457), AEGIS has none on MRPC or GSM8K. Cost 1.26–1.27× vanilla wall-clock at every Qwen scale (4.92 vs 3.88 GPU-h at 72B), matched FLOPs and peak memory.
arxiv-2611.15735 | Where Is Uncertainty Observed? A Mechanistic Understanding of Uncertainty Quantification in LLMs | University College London (Liang, Ceccatelli, Eusebi, Braun, Loose, Malagnino, Cavallaro) | 2026-11-11 | 2 — full text (arXiv HTML v1, 278k raw chars: §1–§8, references, Appendices A.1–A.4; figures via captions only; inline math stripped by the HTML build, so no equation is quoted and Table 12 is left unquoted because its cells are blank). **A survey with a structural thesis:** UQ methods differ first by *where* they read — structural (component writes), representational (residual state), distributional (token distribution), semantical (meaning distribution via an external equivalence relation) — and the four are chained by non-injective lens maps, so an outer lens provably cannot recover a target that depends on information the map discards. Proposition 1 (residual-stream primacy) licenses exactly four objects; Definition 2 fixes what counts as a lens (fixed map, identified by its object, no fitted parameters, no sampling); Corollary 4 gives information loss and Corollary 5 the sufficiency criterion (permission/prohibition via a two-input counterexample at a lens boundary). Estimators are then triples of (lens, resampled variable ∈ {none, output, parameters, input}, auxiliary structure ∈ {none, read-out map, fitted function, calibration}), with native versus surrogate distinguished by whether estimand and estimator sit at the same lens. **Empirics:** nested feature sets on Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct/Base and Llama-3.1-8B-Instruct (TriviaQA, PopQA, MMLU), same probe family per lens, layer/PCA chosen on validation then frozen; TriviaQA AUROC 0.910 structural / 0.908 representational / 0.884 distributional / 0.871 semantical (Qwen-Instruct), PopQA 0.960/0.965/0.929/0.922, and on MMLU everything collapses to 0.768–0.807 with the ordering breaking down because the option-letter answer space carries no surface form. Every published estimator scores at or below the probe at its own lens (semantic entropy closest, minimal RAUQ furthest, INSIDE at chance on MMLU). Lexical variation within a meaning covers 60.7%/67.1%/58.9% of TriviaQA questions but is weakly correlated with correctness; the weakly expressed unembedding tail predicts correctness alone yet adds almost nothing given the full state. 146 of 159 surveyed papers target correctness, 81 of those ranking metrics only. Relevance 2: the lens axis and the sufficiency counterexample are usable framing and a cheap battery addition; the empirical gaps are small and the paper invents no measurement we lack.
arxiv-2610.31520 | The Geometry of Metaphor: Probing and Steering Metaphor Representations in Large Language Models | University of Amsterdam (Li, Andersen, Lewis) | 2026-10-22 | 2 — full text (arXiv HTML v1, 58.5k raw chars: abstract, §1–§7, limitations, references, Appendices A–N with Tables 1–18; figures 1–7 via captions only; BlackboxNLP 2026). **Two delivery defects recorded, not papered over:** inline math stripped, and many table bodies render blank beyond the first few columns (Tables 4, 9–13, and half of the NIE tables) — only rendering cells and explicit sentences are quoted. **A direction found by patching, then fitted as a 16- or 32-dim subspace.** Patching maps on seven instruction-tuned models (Gemma-2 9B, LLaMA-3.1 8B/70B, Mistral-7B, Mixtral-8x7B, Qwen-2.5 7B/72B) show three peaks: the swapped word, the sentence-final full stop plus the token before it, and the trailing prompt colon. DAS is fitted at the stop token (layer 11 for LLaMA-8B, 29 for 70B), maximising interchange-intervention accuracy with the intervention direction alternating each step, then compared against full-vector mass-mean steering on the same representations. **Results:** baseline identification 0.645–0.930, interpretation 0.696–0.862, with Gemma-2-27B dropped at 0.464 as below the probing floor. Mass-mean beats a logistic probe as a *causal* operator — LLaMA-8B NIE 0.195–0.355 vs 0.011–0.038 (literal→metaphor) and 0.254–0.666 vs 0.014–0.051 (reverse) — the folder's clearest case of fit quality and causal reach diverging. Repair of initially-wrong items is 54–88% (mass-mean) and 45–91% (subspace), split by model. **The transfer claim:** a subspace trained across all eight semantic domains beats the eight per-domain subspaces on held-out domains, and survives a matched-n retrain of the global matrix in 6/8 transfer cells (Gemma-9B). **Generation collapses:** flip rates fall to 0.03–0.52 and the between-arm ranking inverts in five of seven models, requiring an enlarged scale — which the authors read as a task-adapted concept with a separate one for generation. Judge validated on 100 items: raw agreement 0.79, Cohen's kappa 0.60.
arxiv-2611.08438 | Understanding AI from the Inside: The State of Mechanistic Interpretability for AI Safety | University of Oxford + Martian, with 14 further institutions (Barez, Wu, +19; advisors Cohen, Geva, Bengio, Biderman, Sajjad) | 2026-11-05 | 3 — full text (arXiv HTML v1, ~686k clean chars: §1–§15, references; figures via captions only; survey, so no new models or experiments). **The field's own evaluation framework, and the negative findings that justify it.** Asks whether mechanistic understanding makes models safer and answers with an instrument rather than a verdict: five evaluation dimensions written as functionals of the intervention effect s(S,x) on a behaviour B — attribution faithfulness (intervention-effect agreement, effect-size error, counterfactual recovery), completeness (loss recovery, behavioural coverage, unexplained variance), stability (cross-seed alignment, hyperparameter sensitivity, paraphrase consistency), causal validity (necessity, sufficiency, specificity against named control behaviours), human interpretability (explanation utility, debugging utility, inter-rater kappa) — plus a per-method priority table (SAEs, circuits, probing, steering) and a five-step protocol. **Findings that bear on us:** fewer than 50% of SAE features survive a change of seed; dead features reach 10–50% of a poorly tuned dictionary; a circuit typically explains 60–80% of behavioural variance, with strict necessity and sufficiency called unattainable because behaviour routes through redundant pathways; counterfactual recovery and loss recovery are both unbounded, with the degenerate denominator spelled out; identifiability fails under the high coherence LLM activations are likely to have, so a dictionary is one valid decomposition, not the features. Probes are demoted to non-mechanistic unless their directions are used to intervene, with the sleeper-agent detector (>99% AUROC, prompts unrelated to the trigger) kept as the case where detection without mechanism is the right goal. **Proxy fidelity** is named as the load-bearing open problem: a dictionary agrees with the target on outputs while claiming its internals, so a missing deception circuit in the proxy licenses only that sentence — with interchange interventions on the proxy's units proposed as the test, and causal abstraction as both the framing and an optimisation objective. Six research orientations tabulated on scalability and robustness to deception, with introspective and automated rated low on the latter. Relevance 3: the specificity and paraphrase-consistency measures and the two unboundedness warnings are additions to our reporting we lack.
arxiv-2611.13548 | Massive Activations Confound Sparse Autoencoders | University of Chicago (Delisle, Tan) | 2026-11-10 | 3 — full text (arXiv HTML v1, ~154k raw chars: §1–§8, references, Appendices A–M with Tables 2–64; figures 1–6 via captions only; NeurIPS 2026 poster). **Two standard SAE metrics fail in one identifiable direction, and the fix is a projection.** In layers where a single direction carries ≥93% of residual variance, the *most important* features are the least reproducible: an average of 43.5–48 of the top 50 by zero-ablation have no match in another seed, against 19–27 for a random 50; Qwen-2.5-7B is worst at 6% top-50 sharing while Gemma-2-2B/9B, the least anisotropic at 78%/70% PC1 variance, sit at 87%/92% (Pearson −0.77, Spearman −0.72 against PC1 share). The cause is rank-one non-identifiability: TopK admits infinitely many exact decompositions of an isolated rank-one component (Proposition C.1), so runs "tile" it differently — 38–106 tiles, ~1% of living features, holding 2–66× their share of importance at 0–2.6% cross-seed sharing. Ablating only the orthogonal part of a top-50 contribution reproduces 71–107% of the whole-feature loss increase, so the damage is broken cancellation, not PC1 removal. Fidelity is confounded the same way: Qwen reports 99.96% total variance explained while leaving 33.0% of content variance unexplained, understated by the dominance factor (checked against measurement to <0.2% relative error). Magnitude penalty lifts GPT-2 top-50 sharing 6.4%→94.9% with mean L0 64.00→63.47; ReLU+ sits at 95–100% everywhere while TopK/BatchTopK/scheduled JumpReLU all invert. **Repair:** train on PC1-removed activations and add PC1 back at splice (top-50 4–39.5%→73–99.5%, content FVU down 0.8–33.9%) or exclude PC1-aligned features post-hoc (77.5–96%, dictionary unchanged). **What does not follow:** agreement-edit success goes 0%→9.0%/31.9%/4.2% (post-hoc) on GPT-2/Pythia-1.4B/Qwen, but Gemma-2-2B is 64–65% under *every* learned arm and IOI stays low single digits; sparse probing moves <1 point; automated-interpretability AUROC improves on six models and *falls* on Llama-3.1-8B (−0.093/−0.097). Relevance 3: the dominance factor is a one-eigendecomposition pre-check for any variance-weighted score we report, and the within-dictionary sharing rate is the right null for a stability claim.
arxiv-2611.05655 | When Are Two SAE Latents the Same Feature? | MSU Institute for Artificial Intelligence + 2 independent (Suvorikova, Latypov, Trukhan, Dorn) | 2026-11-03 | 3 — full text (arXiv HTML v1, 162.6k raw chars: §1–§6, references, Appendices A–D with Tables 1–15, incl. all proof appendices; no figures read beyond their captions, but every quoted number is a Table cell or an explicit sentence). **Correspondence between two dictionaries becomes a measurement with an interval.** Latents are weighted by activation energy, embedded under one of three similarity kernels shown to be Hilbert-space cosines (decoder, activation, linearised causal-by-Jacobian), and compared by an entropically regularised optimal-transport plan whose cost is the dictionary discrepancy, whose decomposition is exact and additive over latents, and whose cells are transported mass fractions. Influence functions through the plan (costs, both marginals, dependence across SAE pairs) give a joint CLT and simultaneous bootstrap bands; splitting and absorption models give closed forms that the fractions are supposed to recover. **Numbers:** TinyStories-1M seeds 101–105 (width 64, k=16): context contrast family−animals 0.02639 [0.02277, 0.03002], decomposed 64% weights (0.01680) / 36% cost matrix (0.00956); self-comparison diagonal cost 1.000 at ε=0.03 falling to 0.208 at ε=0.60, where correcting for it shrinks the contrast ~75%. **The measure matters more than the effect:** decoder-cosine and shared-fraction scores give a topic difference of exactly 0.0000 (they never read the evaluation documents) and activation-correlation variants z=1.1–6.9, against z=15.0/15.4 for energy-weighted transport. **Tail over head:** across 35 dictionaries, the group holding 10% of tuning energy carries 28–40% of evaluation cost while the 50%-energy group carries 16–29% (cost/energy ratios 0.11–0.89 high-energy vs 1.3–4.5 low-energy in all 35). **Graded correspondence:** one latent's mass goes 98.75% to its counterpart while that mass is only 54.76% of the counterpart's own. **Theory:** decoder and activation similarity are mutually unconstrained under exact reconstruction, linearised causal similarity is bounded by co-activation, and nearly parallel decoders can have exactly opposite causal effects. **Coverage:** 94.5–95.5% regular, but 0.275 under a 0.5%-probability high-impact document type at N=64 (all 145 samples missing the rare type fail). Relevance 3: a drop-in replacement for seed-agreement claims, plus the low-energy-tail correction and the self-comparison floor.
arxiv-2611.18089 | Deep Latents, Long Memory: Latent persistence explains temporal correlations in transformer activations | Simplex / SISSA | 2026-11 | 3 — full text (figures not inspected); layer autocorrelation across context positions as a label-free geometric observable, grown depth-by-depth in a 5-block RHM transformer (predicted per-latent terms 55–95% of the measured curve) and rising with depth in five pretrained models, with SAE-latent persistence correlating r = 0.5–0.95 across layers, surviving a within-sequence shuffle (1.1→1.8 real order vs 1.1–1.3 shuffled) and cutting autocorrelation 3.0–13.3% per persistent latent ablated against 0.0–0.7% for a matched non-persistent control
arxiv-2611.09196 | Calibrated concept profiles: making the reading of a model's neurons a measurement | Piotr Wilam (independent) | 2026-11-06 | 3 — full text (arXiv HTML v1, 91.6k raw chars: §1–§6, references, Appendices A–E; figures via captions only). **The direct neuron reading, made a measurement.** Declare the stimulus population (factors drawn at reference-corpus rates and logged), read set A (construct in role) minus set B (token out of role), standardise each neuron in its own spread on ordinary input at the same *cell* (token class × fifth of prompt; 20 cells → 2.95M mean/spread pairs), and state every difference in twin units — the distance between two independently drawn batteries sharing their cores. Nine pre-use checks licence the instrument: both nulls gate (real profile a median 10.66× / 12.76× the null's 95th percentile) and seven restrictions are printed, of which the load-bearing ones are that the profile *moves with the mixture* (re-weighting shifts it 2.34 twin units against ~1 for a redraw) and that 8 statements are length-sensitive (`With` 55% of neurons, `For` 49%). **Scope:** OpenCoder-1.5B, all 147,456 MLP neurons in one product space, 188 of 206 Python 3.12 constructs (169 pass the tokenizer gate), 61 references over 2.03T tokens, 569,270 generated prompts. **Results:** resolution below 1,000 training steps (median step 8.56 twin units early, 3.56 late); carrier sets ablate 0.06 / 0.25 / 0.40 nats above matched random sets at 4.19B / 83.9B / 2.03T (sign test p = 0.038 / 0.002 / 0.002); `for`-as-loop and `for`-in-comprehension share 0.250–0.266 of carriers against 0.050 chance and a 0.872 ceiling, so neither token nor role; shared neurons cluster by *inventory* category (exception and warning classes at purity 1.000) and not by grammatical category. **The comparison that matters:** a linear probe at the same site reads 0.998–0.999 median on every trained checkpoint (minimum 0.902 over 164 constructs) and can no longer order anything, while the profile's numbers still move. **Three self-caught artefacts:** the twin unit understates intervals (agreement corrected by it exceeds 1 for ~150 of 188), standardised size rises while raw size falls (up for 144 of 169, down for 148), and the peak layer moves for 41% of constructs. Relevance 3: the licence-before-reading template, the raw/standardised pair, and the declared-population battery are all directly reusable, and the probe-saturation result sharpens our decodable-versus-used partition.

arxiv-2611.00606 | Sparse Autoencoder Attribution Graphs Are Decided at the Margin | Columbia University (Karl Li) | 2026-10-31 | 3 — full text (arXiv HTML v1, ~101k raw chars: abstract, §1–§9, references, Appendices A–E with Theorems 1–4, Propositions 1–2, Table 1's pre-registered rules and the map-fitting protocol; figures 1–8 via captions only; inline math stripped by the HTML build, so no equation body is quoted and every number is a stated sentence, a Table 1 cell or an Appendix E reading). **A graph's validity is decided by the read-out rule, not the derivative — and one re-ranking fixes nearly all of it.** A TopK dictionary keeps the k largest preactivations, so the support the attribution graph is built on is exactly what an intervention perturbs: a single zero ablation moves a downstream feature across the cutoff in **0.74–0.89** of cases on five public TopK pairs over three Pythia models (0.84 at pythia-70m layers 3–4) and 0.92 on a purpose-trained testbed, the rate tracking write against margin (0.19–0.25 at dose 0.9, 0.95–0.99 at dose 4), and the entering and leaving coordinates carry **0.73–0.84** of the response's energy (entrants alone 0.33–0.39), a crossing coordinate's code moving 5.7–10.1× its preactivation. The **margin graph** keeps every downstream row of the same Jacobian-vector product, adds the first-order change to *all* preactivations and re-applies the TopK rule: median relative error **0.013–0.033 against 0.247–0.669** on the five pairs and 0.017 against 0.786 on the testbed — with the reranking removed the error returns to 0.558, so the reranking carries the gain, and a reranked recomputed-softmax device is as good or better (0.011–0.026, under the margin graph on all five pairs at one product) while integrated gradients on the fixed support stays near 0.78. **Where the claim stops:** built once, the margin graph loses to the fixed support on sixteen-feature subsets (0.670 vs 0.631 on the release), the first-order error reaches 0.3 only at 5.55 and 3.90 cutoff units against median writes of 1.68 and 3.57, and a Lipschitz band estimated from three Jacobians accepts 0.59–0.87 of a write's predicted memberships with **no wrong one among 668,393,327**. **Controls that bite:** an MLP given the margin graph's own six inputs scores 2.23–2.45 (and 1.44–1.53 on two inputs), both worse than predicting no change, against 0.016 for the graph. **Readout:** the entering-and-leaving part of the decoded change has 1.24–1.72× the activation change's norm but projects at only 0.21–0.29 of it, the whole decoded change at a median cosine 0.27–0.50, and a decoded middle code fails at the third layer (0.884–0.999) where one residual-stream product through both blocks errs 0.053. **Cross-seed:** sparse non-negative maps explain 0.57 of a graph's operator energy against 0.22 for atom matching, 0.47 on the masked code Jacobian and 0.85 with the whole target dictionary available, the 0.38 gap splitting into 0.34 of mask term plus 0.05 of cross term, and admitting the target's nearest-cutoff inactive atoms recovers 0.73 of it at a 10% share (random 0.11). Twenty-two rules were fixed before measurement and six fired, three of them against the paper's own headline; largest model pythia-160m, no JumpReLU or transcoder, transport on one cell.

arxiv-2611.04349 | Sparse Internal Control of Language Models | independent (Xizhe Zhang) | 2026-11-03 | 2 — full text (arXiv HTML v1, ~200k raw chars: abstract, §1–§6, references, Appendices A–H incl. the certificate in Def. 1, the Driver-OMP and Coverage-Driver pseudocode, the six-axis definitions, the per-task configurations and the sensitivity tables; figures 1–4 via captions only; inline math doubled by the HTML build and not quoted, but every table is populated). **A steering claim is six measurements, not one.** Five task instances on frozen GPT-2 small, Qwen3-4B and Qwen3-1.7B, with candidate control nodes as site–direction–cost triples and a nonlinear certificate on (target residual, off-target KL, energy) declared before measurement. **Reachability:** IOI Driver-OMP 0.201 ± 0.058 with 10/10 seeds at ε = 0.4, module-level Coverage-Driver 3/3 in both MMLU categories, random supports at 0.747–1.209. arxiv-2610.30474 | Rethinking SAE Evaluation: An Atomic Interpretable Unit Framework Reveals Hidden Polysemanticity and Redundancy | Purdue University (Riteng Zhang, Xiaoqian Wang) | 2026-10-21 | 3 — full text (arXiv HTML v2, ~279k raw chars: abstract, §1–§5, references, Appendices A–K incl. the limitations, the DAME protocol, Tables 2/37–40 and the sharding case protocol; inline math stripped by the HTML build, so every equation body — and the Wilcoxon statistic, Cohen's d, the p-values, the funnel's band and the per-cell explainer-by-judge deltas — renders blank and is described rather than quoted). **Name the objective, derive the criteria, then measure.** The objective is an *atomic interpretable unit basis* — a coordinate system in which attribution is semantically meaningful — and its injectivity requirement yields five necessary criteria (non-redundancy C1, completeness C2, monosemanticity C3, no dead units C4, per-input sparsity C5), with every existing SAE metric reclassified as a proxy for one of them. The two local properties get an explicit local-to-global rule: stratify features into **dominance quartiles** (share of per-input activation) for both sampling and aggregation, never for the score itself. **DAME** replaces binary fire/no-fire judging with a three-bin low/medium/high ranking task scored as perfect-ranking accuracy. **C1** is measured on *decoder* cosine (encoder redundancy is already uneconomic under sparsity; decoder redundancy is unpenalised by the training objective). **Across 600+ SAEs in 7 architecture families (28,000+ features): dominant features are worse on both** — Q1 61.8% vs 64.1% perfect ranking, a deficit in 65.2% of SAEs and significant in 5 of 7 architectures (Gated and JumpReLU n.s. under default hyperparameters), reproduced judge-free on 210 synthetic SAEs by prevalence-corrected held-out support containment (0.082–0.094 vs 0.104–0.108 at every width) and in 71 of 105 SAEs by a correlation-gap analysis. **Redundancy sits in the same quartile:** median Q1 endpoint enrichment 1.6 (95% CI 1.51–1.74), 71.5% of SAEs above the null, 26.1% of Standard-ReLU top-10 features in Q1 redundant pairs, 20.5% of the top hundred; Q1 features have the *lowest* encoder similarity (0.056 vs 0.109–0.135) and Q1 redundant pairs 0.428 vs 0.661. The named failure mode is **feature sharding** — near-identical decoders, dissimilar encoders, complementary supports, a concept split into "concept × gating context" — evidenced by a TopK-65k Pythia-160M determiner pair (encoder 0.2066, decoder 0.7405, 316 vs 57 firings, Jaccard 0.019, both injections moving the target logit, effect-vector cosine 0.943), with 21.5% of decoder-aligned pairs sharding-shaped and 15.3% absorption-shaped. **Scoring-rule validation** on 80 synthetic features with generator-fixed truth: DAME 80, discretised magnitude 67, binary and fuzz at most 22. Higher sparsity correlates with higher dominance concentration in 17 of 18 GPT-2-Small cells — the open question the paper raises is whether sparsity buys finer units or only concentrates computation into fewer dominant ones. Limits: 70M–2.7B primary scale, judge-bounded absolute values, borrowed hyperparameters giving very unequal effective sparsity across architectures (their controlled synthetic sweep gives JumpReLU the strongest deficit, against a non-significant real-model result).
arxiv-2611.04757 | When Should an LLM Steering Edit Depend on the Input? | University of Edinburgh (Rachael Harkness, Konrad Rawlik) | 2026-11-03 | 2 — full text (arXiv HTML v1, ~81k chars: abstract, §1–§6, references, Appendices A–S incl. the metric definitions, the four diagnostics, the estimator sample-size study and the per-family screen correlations; inline math stripped by the HTML build **and every numeric body of Tables 2, 3, 7–10 and 12–15 rendered as blank cells**, so no margin, identity value, efficacy number or Spearman coefficient is quoted — prose figures only; all figures via captions). **Input-dependence is a measured choice, and its two endpoints are one rule.** A steering rule is one direction per query times a selected scalar; the fixed class-mean displacement, the class-conditional mean and retrieval-to-the-mean-of-k-nearest-bank-states (rgs) are the endpoints and interior of a single family, since at k = class size the retrieved mean *is* the class-conditional mean, so the selected k reports which rule won. **Structural results, not fitted ones:** because a concept's source prompt is identical across requested classes and only the destination differs, any rule reading the source state alone sends all of them to one state, so per-item identity is capped at 1/class-count by construction — the affine map hits the cap exactly on the casing control. **Fails where destinations disagree:** on weekday advancement the two centroids coincide so the fixed mean is the *zero vector* by identity and scores the no-op value at every depth (the seven day centroids measure near-mutually-equidistant, ratios near the simplex value rather than the ring value, at every layer); shared-offset controls restore it to near-exact. **Wins where they vary:** retrieval beats the fixed mean on reference error at every corpus and every layer in Gemma-2-2B-it and Llama-2-7b-chat, clears the identity cap on 5 of 6 substitution corpora (~doubling any fixed rule on 4), loses only on sentiment (no stored destination serves a whole-clause rewrite, k widens to the class ceiling); concept level sets *where* to edit (word-level in the first tenth of the network, clause-level flat) and coverage sets the margin (disjoint bank → graceful degradation to the class-conditional mean). A/B benchmarks: wins sycophancy, loses corrigibility because the neighbourhood mean points along the answer-letter axis. CounterFact: fixed mean indistinguishable from not steering, class-conditional mean is the rule, retrieval matches it. **Screen computed before fitting:** retrieval alignment ranks the retrieval error best (reported Spearman over families), residual edit is the strongest label-free quantity at a handful of pairs, global shared fraction reverses sign between families and is never reported alone; none ranks the *margin* — they say whether, not how much — and all four need more supervision than the rule they screen.
arxiv-2611.11035 | Room to Reconsider: Reply Length Decides Whether Pushback Makes Language Models Sycophantic or Self-Correcting | Hanyang University (Ook Lee) | 2026-11-08 | 2 — full text (arXiv HTML v1, ~24k chars: abstract, §1–§10, references; no appendices; Table 1 and Table 2 populated, inline math stripped so the net-change equations and several prose ranges render as blanks — numbers taken only from populated cells and explicit sentences, all figures via captions). **Score both outcomes or the intervention can look good by making the model stubborn.** Caving (right→wrong) and self-correction (wrong→right) under an uninformative challenge, read from option logits after a free reply, at two reply budgets. Net change is arithmetic — (1−a)·r − a·c — so pushback helps only when turn-1 accuracy a falls below r/(r+c), a break-even the paper uses to predict the sign. **Budget reverses the verdict:** Qwen3-4B on MMLU −1.3 points at 96 tokens (cave 8.4%, correct 12.3%) but +5.7 at 1,024 (13.6%/43.2%); Qwen3-8B −2.6→+3.8 (21.8%→43.1%); Qwen3-4B on ARC −0.1→+3.9 with an identical 3.6% cave rate and correction 21.3%→49.6%; correction rises 6–31 points with full replies while caving barely moves, so short budgets hide self-correction. Truncation is a confound in the outcome: at 1,024 tokens truncated Qwen 4B replies caved 38.7% against 12.4% for complete ones. Break-even explains OLMo-2 7B on ARC (−1.2 points, break-even 74.2% below turn-1 76.6%). **Caving is partly predictable beforehand:** hidden-state probe stacked on confidence lifts AUROC 0.747→0.804 (96 tok) and 0.797→0.841 (1,024) for Qwen3-4B, 0.754→0.806 for OLMo at 256, no gain in the smallest run; the probe alone never beats confidence, and 11.3% of high-confidence right answers (OLMo 12.8%) are still abandoned. **Shared signal is budget-dependent:** a probe trained only on wrong-answer cases transfers to right-answer cases at AUROC 0.57–0.69 and adds to confidence under truncated replies in all three tested models, but adds nothing (slightly negative late) with full replies — pre-reply states are budget-independent, so the difference is what reasoning does. **Steering has no selectivity:** layer-18 "keep" direction cut caving 6.1%→0.0% and correction 13.8%→1.5% with accuracy flat; shared-component removal and the change-when-wrong direction stay on one caving–correction frontier; same-norm random directions move both in a ~0.6 ratio; gated steering gains 0.4/1.0 points against an oracle's 6.9/1.5. A cosine-similarity version of the shared-signal claim (0.785) is **withdrawn** in-text after shuffle controls reached 0.6–0.9 and the sign flipped across label randomisations. Limits: ≤8B in 4-bit, one challenge wording, MCQ only, steering screened on prefilled replies.
arxiv-2611.12538 | Language Defaults Before Content: A Causal Layer Map of Multilingual Generation | University of Oslo (Shenbin Qian, Yves Scherrer) | 2026-11-05 | 2 — full text (arXiv HTML v1, ~81k raw chars: abstract, §1–§7, limitations, references, Appendices A–F incl. the contrastive-pair tables, the patching procedure, the logit-lens appendix and the Qwen/Llama setup; **the HTML build stripped almost every inline numeral**, so per-layer chrF++ values, flip fractions, cumulative-R² values and the Qwen/Llama difference cells render blank and are not quoted — only Table 1's onsets and the appendix pair counts survived as cells; figures via captions). **A patching effect is ambiguous until you vary the donor, and varying it by segment, by average and additively decomposes what the copy carried.** Build success–failure contrasts on multi-parallel FLORES+/NTREX (same content, one direction HIGH, one LOW, filtered per pair by an xCOMET-XL margin), copy the HIGH last-token residual into the LOW run at one layer, and read quality, output language (fastText plus exclusive-character flip onset) and Belebele agreement. The donor panel — **matched, seeded shuffled, norm-rescaled mean, and the additive HIGH−LOW mean difference** — is the instrument: only the first sees the test segment. **Three stages, replicated in gemma-3-4b-pt, Qwen3.5-9B-Base and Llama-3.1-8B but not at fixed depth** (English flip onset L16/18/13, content onset one layer later, target onset L24/28/24): **the English default is content-independent** — all four donors rise together at the flip layers, matched−shuffled is null there, and the median onset agrees across conditions; **content opens one layer later** — matched separates from shuffled and stays separated to L32, shuffled becomes *worse* than clean LOW on same-target pairs, Belebele lifts off chance at the same layer with exact-agreement with the HIGH pass, and the onset is task-independent; **the specific target resolves late and only for non-English** — sister pairs (nno/swe, ukr/rus) sit later with near-zero flip fraction, cross-script targets between. **Feature-level dissociation in both directions:** the flip direction's span by its top SAE decoder vectors **dips at exactly the causal layers** and doubling the budget barely helps, and no sparse add/ablate/both intervention flips any English-target pair at either width or strength while all dense donors do; conversely the sister-language direction is *well* spanned yet no feature flips it. De-noising test: removing instance-specific feature mass helps at the flip layer, hurts progressively after, renormalisation changes nothing, random equal-mass removal is far worse — the identity of the removed features, not the quantity, carries it (and this contradicts the paper's own content-interference explanation of the late mean-over-matched gap, which xCOMET does not reproduce anyway). Reproduction: matched reproduces the HIGH pass's own Belebele answer at the content layer while shuffled stays flat. **Fidelity bounds the instrument:** Gemma's dictionaries are interpretable, Qwen's, much lower, give a de-noised vector dominated by reconstruction error, and Llama's are low enough that the authors decline the sparse test — the patching account transfers regardless. Limits: one vector, last token, one layer at a time (availability, not the site of computation), three pretrained models, xCOMET-selected instances, and the same position limitation applies to the sparse negatives, which the paper does not repeat there.

Result: reconstruction is the only criterion that separates trained from random (FVE 0.973 vs 0.775 AlphaEarth and 0.919 vs 0.534 Tessera at ~16 features; 2.7% vs 22% unexplained variance), while monosemy (88% of features aligned with <=1 of 20 groups vs 89% for the frozen decoder, and 234 vs 136 strictly aligned in the decoder's favour), spatial scale (median correlation length 20-33 km vs 1,240 km for a raw dimension, 23-32% vs 9% of variance within a 640 m patch, matched by the frozen decoder at 23 km) and Moran's I do NOT - specificity follows from 16-of-1024 activation arithmetic, not from learned atoms; the six highest-energy features carry 40% of code energy and are discretised regional pieces of the leading principal components (cosine 0.51-0.79, correlation r=0.69-0.83). Applicability: sparse-code distance to 5 nearest training pixels ranks canopy-height error better than the raw distance in 77/88 hold-outs AlphaEarth (+0.17) and 75/88 Tessera (+0.21) against one common head, but not for elevation (41/88, 0.00), and an UNTRAINED RANDOM SPARSE CODE of the same width and sparsity gains as much on AlphaEarth (75/88, +0.19). Two controls the folder should copy: the frozen random dictionary, and the random sparse code as the discriminator between 'better space to measure in' and 'features that mean something'.

arxiv-2611.18430 | Validating Graph Neural Network Feature Attribution Through Local Perturbations: A Study on CoMoFeNiCu Decomposition Enthalpy | Boston University, Dept of Mechanical Engineering (Tenorio, Chapman) | 2026-11-13 | 2 - full text (arXiv HTML v1, main body end to end; supplementary figures S1-S7 not fetched, so nothing is quoted from them); a methods-validation study on one high-entropy alloy at three Co stoichiometries (15/35/55 at.%, Fe/Ni/Cu fixed at 10%) with ~877 binary and ~4,442 ternary DFT decomposition-pathway graphs, 6-layer ALIGNN-style GNNs (100 channels, 3.5 A cutoff, 1500 epochs) with a positive-normalised scalar decoder so per-feature outputs are additive contributions by construction, ranked by motif share within each descriptor class; fit is near-perfect in distribution (R2 0.99, MAE 14.26 and 7.70 meV/atom) and the cross-pathway transfer is lopsided (binary-trained on ternary: R2 0.308, MAE 665.43 meV/atom after a -0.301 slope correction, 13.6% hit rate; ternary-trained on binary: R2 0.943, MAE 110.26 meV/atom, 92.1% hit rate - but the discussion quotes R2 0.26 for the same evaluation, so the entry records both); rankings put Co above Mo as Co content rises, mixed Mo-Co-Co / Co-Co-Mo triplets above all other motifs and three-body above two-body, and the ternary-trained model reproduces the binary-trained model's ranking while its predictions fail; the validation itself is a single atomic swap (Co<->Fe, Co<->Mo, both directions) re-scored per neighbour shell, giving the largest change at shell 0 and decay to zero within 2-3 shells, which matches the 3.5 A receptive field - while the prediction barely moves (DFT 0.197 -> 0.198 eV/atom and -0.707 -> -0.707 with model 0.208 -> 0.186 and -0.707 -> ~-0.693). Read as the folder's cleanest case of a validation that confirms the decoder's arithmetic rather than the learning: no shuffled-label, random-init, same-species-swap or significance control is run, and a positive additive decoder must be most affected at the atom you edited.
arxiv-2611.20897 | Certified, Not Explained: Exact Token Erasure in Gated Vision State Space Models | COMSATS University Islamabad (Parveen, Zia) | 2026-11-16 | 2 - full text; a gated selective state-space concept-bottleneck for lung-nodule malignancy on LIDC-IDRI (64 patch tokens, one bidirectional gated S6 block, six concept channel groups, malignancy by logistic regression on the six concepts) with a hard-concrete token gate shared between scan directions; the paper proves that masking the *convolution input* to zero and zeroing closed-token pooling weight makes the logits exactly invariant to what closed tokens contain, and that at depth the input-projection and residual gates (not either step-size gate) are what stop a later block re-opening a leaked position - confirmed by a path-by-path knockout where all 7 conforming configurations are exact in 15/15 runs and the 3 violating ones break in 0/15 (max logit change 5.44/5.02 through 6.69/5.40); accuracy is level with an ungated Mamba CBM (pooled OOF AUROC 0.8425 +/- 0.0085 vs 0.8428) but concept quality trails both other backbones; the audit is mostly negative and pre-declared - the gate ranks evidence worse than MambaLRP, gradient-input and hidden attention on deletion, loses to the crop's centre prior in ROAR-lite (0.7206 vs 0.6466 on centred crops, 0.6655 vs 0.8859 on 24px-shifted crops where removing the centre actually raises AUROC) and never beats hidden attention, and gate maps from a permuted-label model are as similar to the real maps as two real-label seeds are to each other. Read as the folder's cleanest case that a certified support set is not an explanation, and as the strongest form of claim we should hunt for: an exact predicted zero with a stated precondition and a stated check.
arxiv-2611.20432 | Validating Acoustic-to-Articulatory Inversion with Real-Time MRI: From Vocal-Tract Fidelity to Phonetic Consequences | University of Sydney / Macquarie / York (Piyadasa, Proctor, Gully, Szalay, Kiff, Ballard, Jin) | 2026-11-16 | 2 - full text; paired real-time MRI and speech used to test whether acoustically inferred vocal-tract variables are measurements or merely useful codes - eight TVs (lip aperture/protrusion, tip/body/root constriction degree, tip/body constriction location, velic aperture) predicted at 50 Hz by a frozen WavLM-Large encoder + BiLSTM from ~2 h of paired data, 358 utterances / 14 speakers of Annot-16, speaker-independent; mean Pearson r 0.643 with degrees (0.850 TTCD, 0.794 TRCD, 0.742 TBCD) far ahead of locations (0.475 TTCL, 0.340 TBCL), and the predicted-to-observed SD ratio below 1 for every variable (mean 0.775, min 0.595 for TBCL), i.e. systematic range compression exactly where correlation is worst; a matched synthesis probe drives the same Transformer synthesizer from inferred vs MRI-derived TVs with source F0, voicing and speaker embedding fixed, and the aggregate cost is small and flattering (35.8% WER / 23.1% CER / 3.77 dB MCD from inferred against 39.6% / 26.2% / 3.86 dB from measured), while the per-phone mel-spectral gap is 0.40-0.75 dB for vowels, approximants, stops and nasals and 2.47 dB for fricatives and affricates (2.07 dB above approximants, so narrowness alone is not the explanation), with the /s/-/sh/ centroid separation contracting 1136 -> 723 Hz and Cohen d 1.48 -> 0.92; controls: 7.7% WER vs 5.4% clean-domain reference, 10.7% vs 10.3% mel-through-vocoder, 72.7% CER with constant TVs. Read as the folder's cleanest two-stage validation of a read-out - independent measurement first, then a functional probe that holds everything but provenance fixed and locates the errors, and as evidence that an aggregate downstream delta can point the wrong way.
arxiv-2611.20445 | Explainable Federated Learning via Global–Local Attribution Alignment | Virginia Tech / DEVCOM Army Research Laboratory (Wasif, Moore, Lu, Cho) | 2026-11-16 | 2 - full text; federated clients distil an on-device surrogate, explain it with path-integrated gradients over a group space, and exchange only clipped, quantised, noised top-k artefacts that a server median-aggregates into a Global Explanation Prior which then JSD-aligns future surrogate explanations but never touches the task model (8 clients, 15 rounds, six benchmarks x IID/non-IID): MNIST non-IID EDI 0.076 vs 0.110 local / 0.159 aggregated at FedAvg accuracy 0.930, alignment weight ablation 0.006 -> 0.0006 EDI with deletion/insertion AUC unmoved, poisoned fraction 0.2 gives top-k support change 0.0008 vs 0.0041-0.0043 for every baseline — and two ablations that cut against the story: DP noise *lowers* drift (0.0091 -> 0.0007) and more surrogate refinement *raises* it (0.0007 -> 0.0012), i.e. agreement can be manufactured by regularisation rather than by the mechanism
arxiv-2611.21099 | ForceHarness: Turning Force into Action and Physics for Frozen Frontier Models | Show Lab, NUS (Sun, Ma, Zhang, Chen, Shou) | 2026-11-16 | 2 - full text; an API-only frozen model is wrapped in a three-layer harness (joint-torque force calibration with rest-taring and within-episode deltas, twelve self-terminating force primitives that stop on contact events, and a fixed physics translation layer returning mass/contact height/stiffness/friction with uncertainty and flags, no task thresholds) and scored on a real FR3 with no wrist sensor, ForceBench and a new seven-task MuJoCo suite: real robot 2/60 -> 54/60 for GPT-6 Astra (bottle sorting and soft-vs-hard 0/10 -> 10/10), ForceBench 70.0% -> 87.5% but with the authors conceding most of the gap is the model and instructions, HiddenPhys 87/140 -> 135/140 with zero handle damage against a 136/140 expert, and a seven-level feedback-form ablation on identical primitives and seeds showing event words alone buy 0.63 -> 0.77 and derived signals match L1/L2, so the credit is computing the quantity rather than its format
arxiv-2611.15168 | MAST: Medoid-Anchored Subspace Steering for Visual Grounding in Multimodal LLMs | Nanjing University / NUDT (Chen, Cai, Li, Sha) | 2026-11-11 | 2 - full text; activation steering rebuilt around activation geometry rather than one pooled mean: per-task k-medoids clusters (K=5) over answer-free prompt states give a 20-medoid bank, each query is routed by cosine to its top-3 medoids and assembled from per-cluster low-rank (rank-8) local directions with a reliability-based blend, plus an optional logistic probe that reweights per-task direction families; LLaVA-v1.5 CV-Bench four-task average 50.94% -> 68.00% (+17.06, per-task +19.42/+6.35/+16.25/+26.25) with every tuned static baseline within ~2 points of no-steering and two below it, probe-reweighted +8.09 more to 76.09%, Qwen2-VL 77.40% -> 80.84%, McNemar significant on all four LLaVA tasks and three of four Qwen2-VL; geometry reported as the argument: clusters separated (separability 0.144-0.192, DB 1.769-2.142, 100% same-task top-1 routing, 76.69% with all twenty pooled), 17 singular directions cover 90% of intra-cluster variance in 4096 dims, and the global mean sits at cosine 0.364-0.654 from its own clusters with one at -0.106 and one at 0.063 — with honest concessions: an answer-free instruction contrast collapses to 22.01% vs 47.30% (so completion-to-generation transfer is assumed, not validated), removing multi-center routing (39.54%) is worse than stripping everything (47.35%), and the task probe keeps only 36.50% posterior mass on Depth against 49.62% on Distance
arxiv-2611.21550 | ONAM: Fitting Orthogonal Neural Additive Models for Post-Hoc Interpretability of Black-Box Predictions | University of Bonn (Kohler, Schmid) | 2026-11-16 | 2 - full text; an additive surrogate for a fixed black-box prediction function, where the split of the function into main effects and interactions is made UNIQUE by a projection pass rather than by a fitting convention: a neural additive model is fitted to the black box's own outputs on a declared explanation sample, then orthogonalized top-down so every subfunction is orthogonal to all lower-order subfunctions on that sample, which leaves the predictions numerically unchanged and forces as much mass as possible into the readable low-order terms (Theorem 1, proved). Reports a single number for how much of a model is readable at all - the order-k variance shares sum to one and their low-order prefix is the 'degree of interpretability' (Boston/XGBoost worked example: 0.9038 + 0.0543 = 0.9581 at surrogate-vs-black-box correlation 0.9635). No baselines, no held-out fidelity, no ground-truth function: every claim is about the fitted surrogate, so the paper is the folder's clearest case of a guarantee that is exact with respect to the explanation distribution and silent everywhere else.
arxiv-2611.20840 | Interpretable deep concepts of organoid morphology | Helmholtz Munich / TUM (Gleiter, Peng) | 2026-11-16 | 2 - full text; an SAE dictionary trained on two FROZEN foundation models (DINOv3-large, ViTAMIn-O) over organoid crops, where the point is not the dictionary but the per-atom metadata: spatial role from a token-type split (background/border/organoid/global), top-5 prototype images, activation frequency, dataset specificity as a normalised entropy, and task relevance as mutual information maximised over ten thresholds. Cheapest result: under 32 top-relevant atoms match the full 4,000-atom dictionary on four of five downstream tasks, and the dictionary is near-free in AUROC against the raw foundation features. Strongest result: the most task-relevant atom for abnormal brain organoids was reading padding from the authors own cropping step, and those artifacts correlate with the label - relevance-ranked prototypes as a confound detector. Spatial labels transfer to held-out datasets (DINOv3 81.7% vs ViTAMIn-O 66.4% balanced accuracy on token-type classification) and filtering atoms by spatial role improves three tasks for DINOv3 and none for ViTAMIn-O, whose 7x7 grid is too coarse. Methodologically honest about its own metric: task relevance is a MAXIMUM over thresholds with no held-out split for that selection, and two-loss ablation cuts dead codes (ViTAMIn-O 55.7% -> 5.3%) while costing R2 (0.62 -> 0.55).
arxiv-2611.20913 | Palmprint Decomposer: Interpretable and Editable Factorization via Synthetic Learning and Instance-Wise Refinement | University of Macau / Yonsei / Nanchang Hangkong / Sichuan (Yan, Wang, Wang, Zhang, Teoh, Leng, Li, Yang) | 2026-11-16 | 2 - full text; a fixed multiplicative composition rule (colour map times one plus light map, attenuated by a scalar strength times a structure map) with the four factors trained on synthetic data where the factors themselves are the labels, then per-image refinement of the factors through the same rule. The paper is really about disambiguating the decomposition rather than producing it: leak is suppressed architecturally (base decoder at half resolution with the finest skip removed; refinement restricted to coarse residual fields) and by a paired-image consistency loss plus a lighting-variation floor, and every ablation shows the leak returning when those are lifted. Separation is measured WITHOUT ground truth on real palms - texture similarity of the base appearance to the INPUT (0.54-0.58 in 13 of 15 groups) read against each database's own same-identity (0.71-0.80) and different-identity (0.54-0.56) similarity levels, i.e. the base has stopped carrying identity while the structure map keeps verification at the original level (EER -0.22/-0.33 points on two datasets, +0.68 on one phone/flash condition). Reconstruction 42.62-59.39 dB PSNR, SSIM 0.974-0.999 after 100 refinement steps per image, with refinement doing nearly all the work (IITD 38.45 -> 41.80 dB in the first 20 steps) and costing a small rise in retained base texture; dropping the overlap term would buy 54.12 vs 48.38 dB on PolyU at more leak. Honest concessions: the composition rule is a simple approximation that degrades on mobile/flash capture, refinement trades separation for reconstruction (+0.02 on IITD, +0.026 on PolyU), and the labels are the authors' own generator.
arxiv-2611.20637 | Counterfactual Feature Steering for Adjacency-Preserving Identity Unlearning | West Virginia University / EFREI Paris / University of Aberdeen (Shivam, Sliman, Reddy, Adjeroh, Bhattarai, Gyawali) | 2026-11-16 | 2 - full text; inference-time identity erasure in Stable Diffusion 1.4 where the unit is a per-token direction in a one-time k-sparse autoencoder over FROZEN CLIP text-encoder layer-9 activations, applied as a projection that is proved never to cross the retain-side boundary (at full strength it is the minimum-norm projection onto the retain half-space; tokens already on the retain side are untouched). Feature ranking is a forget-minus-retain mean over the summed forget and retain spreads, so a feature with no stable baseline on non-target prompts is not subtracted; k = 48, retain weight 0.1, one SAE (~2 h) reused for all eight identities, ~925 KB cached statistics per target. The contribution is the SCORE as much as the method - every metric is a difference of means between the target crowd and a control crowd (AWUS on CLIP image-text drops, FaceAWUS on ArcFace identity, LPIPS-gap on perceptual change), so the reported number is erasure MINUS neighbour damage: 0.296 / 0.636 / 0.402 against 0.279 / 0.233 / 0.262 for the best competitors, with higher-erasure methods paying ~12-22x the collateral damage, target-face similarity 0.160 vs adjacent 0.760, and COCO general quality essentially flat (FID 15.71 -> 16.11). The ablation that matters: the SAME bounded projection applied directly in raw hidden space erases slightly more (0.399 vs 0.387) and scores slightly higher (AWUS 0.391 vs 0.381), so the dictionary earns its place only on selectivity (collateral drift 0.0065 vs 0.0078, peer-face retention 0.848 vs 0.722) - and the k-sweep shows the published setting sits one step from a zero-utility cliff. Honest limits: hand-curated adjacency sets with an MRR-0.487 internal ranking, ArcFace detection failing on 20.8% of target outputs vs 3.4% of peers, aggregate-only direction geometry, one backbone, and a stated non-tamper-proof deployment scope.
arxiv-2611.20398 | Deploying a Control-Logic LLM Assistant in Air-Gapped Power Plants: Lessons in Source Authority and Bounded Retrieval | GE Vernova Advanced Research Center (Fu, Gabriel) | 2026-11-16 | 1 - full text; an industrial deployment report whose measurable lessons are about read-out provenance, not model internals. Two prototypes are abandoned after measurement: a VLM reconstructing controller topology from the 6,979-page exchanged PDF reached 0.51 connection precision over the full document (0.41 on a 100-page sample) and 36% exact-equation accuracy (75% on the sample) at 3.1 h on 4xH200 (~$124), while the authoring system's native CSV export - block identity, pin direction, connections - was ingested in under 10 s at full fidelity; and an LLM tool-calling agent re-planning over eight enumerable graph operations scored 85% vs 94% answer correctness against a classify-then-fixed-plan pipeline, at 14.2/108.6 s p50/p95 vs 4.8/12.7 s, 24K vs 1.2K tokens per query, 9.1 vs 2.0 DB round-trips, with cross-run retrieved-node Jaccard 1.0 vs 0.61. Deployed as a read-only, advisory evidence layer at two air-gapped gas plants: a versioned property graph (221,406 nodes / 402,240 edges across seven controllers) with pins folded into edge properties, bounded Cypher execution (10 hops, frontier 8, 150 nodes, truncation recorded), entity disambiguation that asks rather than picks, abstention on unsupported requests, and the local model confined to intent classification and evidence-grounded phrasing - the graph payload is immune to rewriting. The transferable pair: extraction precision can be high on objects and near-useless on the relations that constitute the explanation, and a retrieved set's repeat stability is a separate quantity from its average correctness. Honest limits: one vendor format, one alarm-centred template workload, LLM-judge scoring over generated references, precision-only extraction (recall unknown), a single retrospective replay, no operational telemetry.
arxiv-2611.20311 | Reveal: A Provenance-Aware System for Multimodal Urban Data Exploration | University of Illinois Chicago / FGV / UC Berkeley / UFF (Ferreira, Rulff, Hosseini, Paes, de Oliveira, Miranda, Lage) | 2026-11-15 | 2 - full text (all figures and screenshots not inspected); a visual analytics system rather than an interpretability method, taken for its PROVENANCE object. Every exploratory operation on a joint CLIP embedding space over street-level images plus captions (text/image/joint cosine search, scatter-plot selection, gallery and top-20 word cloud filtering, map polygon filter, and set union/intersection/difference) becomes a numbered node in a dataflow diagram, colour-coded by type and ordered so asymmetric operations stay well defined, with manually curated free-floating buckets outside the graph; a whole subgraph is saved as a state (filters, thresholds, node pipeline) and replayed on a new corpus. Two case studies by one urban planner with 11+ years of experience: eleven new sidewalk-asphalt condition categories and over 1,600 newly labelled images from Project Sidewalk (difference against hand-picked well-maintained exemplars, then word-cloud terms as fresh text queries, then intersections of condition x feature sets, then an embedding-level split into under-construction / slippery / water-accumulation), and the same node sequence rebuilt on geolocated Chicago street views with LLM captions, where the reproduced branches gave spatially distinct and coherent patterns (eroded curb edges in South Chicago, compound degradation along transport corridors, construction nodes isolating temporary work, a tree-intersection branch flipping the downtown concentration into two residential green clusters). The two transferable ideas are the acceptance test - does the recorded derivation TRANSPOSE to a new dataset and produce something coherent and correctly different - and the honest failure: the graph records operations only, so why three exemplars were called well-maintained is invisible, and there is no baseline, no second expert, no timing, no uncertainty and no stability check under threshold changes.
arxiv-2611.21586 | Beyond Job Titles: Interpreting Early-Career Job Advertisements with Sparse Autoencoders and Semantic Clustering | University of Virginia (Active Learning Lab / School of Data Science; Cao, Kim, Wright, Rivera) | 2026-11-16 | 2 - full text; 36,081 internship-heavy postings, a frozen Goodfire SAE over layer-19 Llama-3.1-8B and a PERC clustering pipeline, compared as HYPOTHESIS SOURCES on identical sections: the same hypothesis-writer under PERC clusters, SAE features or text alone, and a separate verifier that sees the definition but not the group, judged present/absent/unclear on advertisements from held-out companies with an exact-quote requirement, company-clustered intervals, sign-flip tests at a 0.01 effect floor and BH/BY per study (1,171 hypotheses, 127 comparisons). The honest result is that the representation buys claim COUNT not answer: broad occupational contrasts recur under every condition (developing ML models 60/98 DS vs 7/98 DA from clusters, 48/98 vs 5/98 from features), the text-only arm recovers several of them, and NO monthly and NO Business Analyst comparison survives correction under either procedure, while SAE generation costs $17.50 against $0.28. Two confounds printed rather than hidden: within a Data Scientist Qualifications cluster all ten feature groups activate less often where the median section is 55 tokens against 140, and the Gemma full-dataset clusters largely separate postings before and after January 2026 (89.2% modal-period match). Also its best MMD discipline: the V-statistic bias is derived, the kernel scale is fixed from training medians, and the score is used only to RANK which comparisons a reader inspects, never as a significance test.
arxiv-2611.20583-state-machine-governance | State-Machine Governance of Adaptive Enforcement: Evidence from a Paired-Scenario Evaluation | Colorado Technical University (Agbedo, Qu) | 2026-11-16 | 2 - full text; a paired-scenario evaluation of a governed transition layer between an AI recommendation and committed enforcement, taken for its REPORTING PATTERN rather than its subject. 36 matched scenario pairs held inputs fixed while the two arms used DIFFERENT scoring predicates, which the paper states and then stress-tests two ways: a 286-vector weight sweep (differences 0.056-0.500 around a 0.252 headline) and post-hoc replays that harmonise one predicate at a time. Control 0.720 -> 0.972, V = 359, p < .001, r = .899, 26/9/1 pairs, but the component contrast is lopsided (traceability 0.500 of the 0.252 mean is the arm-specific predicate itself) and the most informative row is S-024: correct refusal with no retained refusal evidence scores 0.250 - a prevented event with no evidence trail is indistinguishable from nothing having happened, which is the failure mode our own ablation logging carries. Also: an audit chain that gates traceability while being reported outside the composite, flagged as a dependency; four rollback cases per arm against a 60 s bound; a connector fault that breaches the bound in both arms. 
arxiv-2611.21405 | Shaping AI's Beliefs Before It Generates: Human Sense of Agency in Creative AI | Google DeepMind (Wang, Pushkarna) | 2026-11-16 | 2 - full text; a design probe, not an interpretability method, taken for its REPORTING and INSTRUMENT patterns. PaCC interposes an editable belief representation (three clarifying questions with 3-5 options plus a per-question AI delegation, a belief graph that marks model-inferred entities apart from user-stated ones with typed relations and candidate-valued attributes, modality-level style attributes, a low/medium/high proactiveness tier, a side-by-side prompt diff) between the prompt and any generated media, so the human sets the anchor instead of editing around it. The explanation is deliberately partial and non-numeric - the backend estimates uncertainty over the candidate attribute values and then HIDES the probabilities from the interface as clutter. Eight participants, single arm, no control, retrospective comparison against their habitual tools: overall agency 4.09 (SD 0.84) and control 3.89 (SD 0.69) above the preserved midpoint, but ownership 3.22 (SD 1.49, range 1.2-5.0) and authorship 2.75 (SD 1.13, range 1.5-5.0) are a POLARISATION summarised by its mean - half the participants report high ownership, half report none, and four name the AI multiple-choice options themselves as the reason. Clarifying questions are the only feature all eight rated above their current tools on agency; the belief graph is named both most and least useful by different participants; expression, the stylistic aspect, is the lowest on all four dimensions (authorship 2.77 vs ~3.0-3.2) because style cannot be typed. Dialogue features beat visualisation features on comparative authorship (5/1/1 each vs 3/2/2 and 3/3/1), which is the same computing-vs-formatting contrast our own read-out comparisons keep finding. One-sample effect sizes are stated to be distance from the midpoint, not treatment effects.
arxiv-2611.21232 | A Generative AI-Driven Architecture for Whole-Home Intelligent Services: Edge Models, Memory Management, and Household Applications | Mo Zhou | 2026-11-16 | 1 (background: nine-module home-server architecture; responsibility/risk/metric table as reporting discipline)
arxiv-2611.20983 | GRACE: Parallel Best-of-N Multi-Agent Analysis of Closed-Form Questions over Dataframes with Guardrail Selection and Adaptive Retry | Engineering Ingegneria Informatica (Picariello, Cipolla, Bonura, Morreale) | 2026-11-16 | 2 - full text; a label-free best-of-N selector for dataframe code, taken for its SELECTION-VERSUS-ABSTENTION instrument rather than its subject. N parallel code agents under a three-clause output contract (single-entry dict holding one DataFrame, charts stored outside it with provenance, meaningful name), diversified by four fixed personas plus a neutral anchor agent identical to N=1, per-retry persona rotation, and a 0.65 -> 1.0 temperature schedule; a single-elimination pairwise tournament halts at TWO survivors (not one) so both finalists keep an advice channel; a guardrail then either picks the least-defective candidate or rejects the whole pool with global root-cause advice plus per-agent critique, and every retry must add a new invariant rather than a cosmetic patch. DAEval 246q, Qwen3.6-35B-A3B: 39% -> 71% (N=2, r=4), 95/96 previously correct kept, 79/150 wrong recovered; 9B dense 37.8% -> 63% (N=4, r=4) and still gaining from agents under a generous retry budget where the 35B has stopped; DataBench 69.8% -> 85.0% with equivalence for only 2 of 4 plateau steps (TOST margin set to median measurement resolution, +/-5.7 pp / +/-3.75 pp). The reusable part is Section 5.1: SAME 263 candidate pools, only the selector swapped - tournament+guardrail 66.9% against a 77.6% oracle ceiling, voting 63.1% (+3.8 pp, CI [+0.3,+7.3], p = 0.03), random 62.9% - and voting statistically indistinguishable from random (p = 0.66) because 63.9% of pools have NO two agreeing candidates. Conditioning on pool-contains-correct splits one score into two unequal capabilities: takes the right candidate 86.3% of the time when present, rejects an all-wrong pool only 10.2% of the time. Cost: single agent with a moderate retry budget ~1.1x baseline tokens, two agents ~2x, four agents paid for nothing; latency near baseline since agents run concurrently.
arxiv-2611.20678 | UNIDE: Cross-PDE Learning for Noise-Robust Discovery of Governing Equations | Independent Researcher (Xiaoyu Xie) | 2026-11-16 | 2 - full text; equation discovery from noisy trajectories, taken for its MATCHED-CONTROL LADDER and its split of one headline into two metrics that fail differently. Three stages over six 1D PDE families (advection, diffusion, advection-diffusion, Burgers, KdV, Kuramoto-Sivashinsky; 2,400 simulated fields, 12-term library of which 5 are ever active): a blind-spot (Noise2Void) residual CNN denoiser trained with no clean targets, a shared encoder (per-frame spatial CNN -> time-tagged transformer -> attention pooling + metadata MLP) that predicts per-term presence probabilities, and a weak-form least-squares coefficient solve on the predicted support with greedy residual pruning. Coefficients are never network outputs. Exact match highest at every noisy level: 0.866 at 1% noise, 0.960 at 10%, 0.924 at 80% vs WSINDy 0.820 -> 0.003 and SINDy/PySR at ~0.00-0.01; clean data the baselines are strong (PySR 0.953) so the gap is a noise phenomenon. Coefficient accuracy INVERTS at low noise - WSINDy better to 5%, UNIDE ahead only from 20% onward (8.04% vs 43.01% relative error at 80%). The reusable part is Table 2: with the same denoiser, terms, windows, pruning and validation partition, weak STLSQ scores 0.010 raw -> 0.484 with the denoiser -> 0.834 with pruning -> 0.924 with learned selection, so denoising and pruning each help a lot and neither closes the gap; and no coefficient winner in those controls. Ablations: no denoiser 0.667, clean-trained encoder 0.393, pointwise least squares 0.670 exact match with 48.85% coefficient error. Per-loss-term ablation is a clean negative - removing ANY one of four auxiliary losses leaves exact match at or above the full objective (0.679-0.711 unpruned vs 0.670). Named failures: Burgers swaps the advective and convective transport terms (pruning can only delete), KdV loses its small stabilising term to pruning on up to 29/150 trajectories. Scope limits stated by the authors: all structures seen in training, laid out as a 1D synthetic periodic benchmark, one model per noise level.
arxiv-2611.21331 | TRIAGE-CPG: A Cross-Model Study of Budgeted Static Malware Triage with Large Language Models | Fraunhofer AISEC / Google Zurich (Sava, Kuchler, Kowatsch, Grill, Pullman, Fratantonio, Invernizzi) | 2026-11-16 | 2 - full text; LLM triage of decompiled Windows binaries as a BUDGETED EVIDENCE-ACQUISITION problem, taken for its ABSTENTION-AWARE metric construction and its two nulls. Eight models (3 Gemini, 5 open-weight) over one fixed pipeline: LLM-free seed ranking over the call-graph projection, 30-read exploration agent, reporting agent on a frozen dossier, verdict benign/suspicious/malicious. Primary metric tBA (clear-vs-not-clear balanced accuracy; escalate-all scores exactly 0.5) reported beside a strict reading, so the gap is the share of score bought by deferral. Best model 0.805 strict / 0.855 tBA at ~40 of a mean 1,000 functions read; models span the recall-specificity frontier with no provenance split, and 48.2% verdict agreement between two models against 91% run-to-run for one. Nulls: seven seed selectors within 2 points of each other and random seeding only 5 points down; targeted exploration indistinguishable from random on accuracy, winning only by stopping early (5.2 vs 11.3-25.4 reads), and correcting 30 benign against hurting 11 while doing nothing for malware. Cascades prune the queue (44 -> 35 at 0.943 precision) but move tBA 0.005, inside the 1.5-point noise floor. Explicitly not compared against trained static or dynamic detectors.
